+\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}r^{\delta-2}\left[|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\widehat{\psi}|^{2}+\lambda|\pi_{\leq l}\widehat{\psi}|^{2}\right]\Big|_{r=r_{+}}\,d\upsigma\\
\leq\boxed{C\int_{\frac{1}{3}R_{\infty}}^{\frac{1}{2}R_{\infty}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}r^{-3+\delta}\left[|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\widehat{\psi}|^{2}+\lambda|\pi_{\leq l}\widehat{\psi}|^{2}\right]\,d\upsigma dr}+C_{s}\int_{r_{+}}^{\frac{1}{2}R_{\infty}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}r^{-3+\delta}|\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa}\widehat{\psi}|^{2}\,d\upsigma dr.

Proof.

Let
\widehat{\phi}=r^{-1}\widehat{\psi}
. Without loss of generality, we will assume
\widehat{\phi}=\pi_{\leq l}\widehat{\phi}
in the derivations below. The
\pi_{>l}\widehat{\phi}
part of general
\widehat{\phi}
can be estimated in exactly the same way by simply taking
\lambda=0
.

We multiply
r^{-2}\rho^{2}\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda}\widehat{\psi}
with
\chi_{r\leq\frac{1}{3}R_{\infty}}r^{\delta-1}\overline{\partial_{r}\widehat{\phi}}
, where
\delta>0
will be fixed later, and where
\chi_{r\leq\frac{1}{3}R_{\infty}}
is a smooth cut-off function that is equal to 1 for
r\leq\frac{1}{3}R_{\infty}
and vanishes when
r\geq\frac{1}{2}R_{\infty}
. We then apply the Leibniz rule to obtain:

\re(\chi_{r\leq\frac{1}{3}R_{\infty}}r^{\delta-1}\overline{\partial_{r}\widehat{\phi}}\cdot r^{-2}\rho^{2}\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa}\widehat{\psi})=\Delta r^{\delta}\chi_{r\leq\frac{1}{3}R_{\infty}}\re(\overline{\partial_{r}\widehat{\phi}}\partial_{r}^{2}\widehat{\phi})+\frac{d\Delta}{dr}\chi_{r\leq\frac{1}{3}R_{\infty}}r^{\delta}|\partial_{r}\widehat{\phi}|^{2}\\
+2ar^{\delta}\chi_{r\leq\frac{1}{3}R_{\infty}}\re(\overline{\partial_{r}\widehat{\phi}}\partial_{r}\partial_{\varphi_{*}}\widehat{\phi})+r^{\delta}\re(\overline{\partial_{r}\widehat{\phi}}\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\widehat{\phi})+(4Mr-2(\mathbbm{h}-1)\Delta))r^{\delta}\chi_{r\leq\frac{1}{3}R_{\infty}}{\re s}|\partial_{r}\widehat{\phi}|^{2}-\lambda r^{\delta}\re(\overline{\partial_{r}\widehat{\phi}}\widehat{\phi})\\
=\frac{1}{2}\partial_{r}\left(\Delta r^{\delta}\chi_{r\leq\frac{1}{3}R_{\infty}}|\partial_{r}\widehat{\phi}|^{2}-r^{\delta}\chi_{r\leq\frac{1}{3}R_{\infty}}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\widehat{\phi}|^{2}-\lambda\chi_{r\leq\frac{1}{3}R_{\infty}}|\widehat{\phi}|^{2}\right)+\partial_{\varphi_{*}}(\ldots)+\textnormal{div}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(\ldots)\\
+\frac{1}{2}r^{\delta}\chi_{r\leq\frac{1}{3}R_{\infty}}\left[\frac{d\Delta}{dr}-\delta r^{-1}\Delta+(8Mr-4(\mathbbm{h}-1)\Delta)){\re s}\right]|\partial_{r}\widehat{\phi}|^{2}+\frac{\delta}{2}r^{\delta-1}\chi_{r\leq\frac{1}{3}R_{\infty}}\left[|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\widehat{\phi}|^{2}+\lambda|\widehat{\phi}|^{2}\right]\\
-\frac{1}{2}\frac{d\chi_{r\leq\frac{1}{3}R_{\infty}}}{dr}\left(\Delta r^{\delta}\chi_{r\leq\frac{1}{3}R_{\infty}}|\partial_{r}\widehat{\phi}|^{2}-r^{\delta}\chi_{r\leq\frac{1}{3}R_{\infty}}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\widehat{\phi}|^{2}-\lambda\chi_{r\leq\frac{1}{3}R_{\infty}}|\widehat{\phi}|^{2}\right),

where we employed the schematic notation
\partial_{\varphi_{*}}(\ldots)+\textnormal{div}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(\ldots)
for total derivatives and divergences on
\mathbb{S}^{2}
, as these terms vanish after integration over
\mathbb{S}^{2}
.

We first estimate via Young’s inequality:

\left|\re\left[r^{\delta-1}\overline{\partial_{r}\widehat{\phi}}\cdot r^{-2}\rho^{2}\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa}\widehat{\psi}\right]\right|\leq\frac{\delta}{2}r^{\delta}(r-M)|\partial_{r}\widehat{\phi}|^{2}+\delta^{-1}r^{-2+\delta}(r-M)^{-1}|\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa}\widehat{\psi}|^{2}.

Furthermore, using that
\mathbbm{h}\leq\frac{r^{2}+a^{2}}{\Delta}
, we obtain

8Mr-4(\mathbbm{h}-1)\Delta\geq 8Mr-4(r^{2}+a^{2})+4\Delta=0

so for
\re s\geq 0
, we have that
(8Mr-4(\mathbbm{h}-1)\Delta)){\re s}\geq 0
.

For
\re s<0
, we use that
\mathbbm{h}\geq 1
to obtain

(8Mr-4(\mathbbm{h}-1)\Delta)\re s\geq 8Mr\re s

and we have that

(12.2)

\frac{d\Delta}{dr}-\delta r^{-1}\Delta+8Mr{\re s}-\frac{\delta}{2}(r-M)>2(r-M)\left(1-\frac{3}{4}\delta+2\frac{r_{+}-M}{r-M}\kappa_{+}^{-1}{\re s}\right),

where we recall the following expression for the surface gravity
\kappa_{+}
:

\kappa_{+}=\frac{r_{+}-M}{2Mr_{+}}.

Hence, for
{\re s}>-\frac{\kappa_{+}}{2}
and
0<\frac{3}{4}\delta<1+\min\{2\kappa_{+}^{-1}{\re s},0\}
, the right-hand side of (
12.2
) is non-negative definite.

Since
\frac{d\chi_{r\leq\frac{1}{3}R_{\infty}}}{dr}>0
and there exists a numerical constant
C>0
such that
-\frac{d\chi_{r\leq\frac{1}{3}R_{\infty}}}{dr}\leq CR_{\infty}^{-1}
, we can estimate:

-\frac{1}{2}\frac{d\chi_{r\leq\frac{1}{3}R_{\infty}}}{dr}\left(\Delta r^{\delta}\chi_{r\leq\frac{1}{3}R_{\infty}}|\partial_{r}\widehat{\phi}|^{2}-r^{\delta}\chi_{r\leq\frac{1}{3}R_{\infty}}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\widehat{\phi}|^{2}-\lambda\chi_{r\leq\frac{1}{3}R_{\infty}}|\widehat{\phi}|^{2}\right)\geq-CR_{\infty}^{-1}r^{\delta}\left(|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\widehat{\phi}|^{2}+\lambda|\widehat{\phi}|^{2}\right).

After setting
\delta=1+2\kappa_{+}^{-1}\min\{{\re s},0\}
and integrating over
[r_{+},\frac{1}{2}R_{\infty}]\times\mathbb{S}^{2}
, we obtain the following estimate: for
{\re s}>-\frac{\kappa_{+}}{2}
, there exists a
C_{s}=C(\kappa_{+},{\re s},\delta)>0
, such that

\int_{r_{+}}^{\frac{1}{3}R_{\infty}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\frac{1}{2}(r-M)(1+2\kappa_{+}^{-1}\min\{{\re s},0\})r^{\delta}|\partial_{r}\widehat{\phi}|^{2}\,d\upsigma dr\\
+\frac{1}{2}(1+2\kappa_{+}^{-1}\min\{{\re s},0\})r^{-1+\delta}\left[|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\widehat{\phi}|^{2}+\lambda|\widehat{\phi}|^{2}\right]+\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}r^{\delta}\left[|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\widehat{\phi}|^{2}+\lambda|\widehat{\phi}|^{2}\right]\Big|_{r=r_{+}}\,d\upsigma\\
\leq C\int_{\frac{1}{3}R_{\infty}}^{\frac{1}{2}R_{\infty}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}r^{-1+\delta}\left[|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\widehat{\phi}|^{2}+\lambda|\widehat{\phi}|^{2}\right]\,d\upsigma dr+C_{s}\int_{r_{+}}^{\frac{1}{2}R_{\infty}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}r^{-3+\delta}|\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa}\widehat{\psi}|^{2}\,d\upsigma dr.

∎

In order to improve the range of allowed values of
{\re s}
, we need to consider higher-order
r
-derivatives of
\widehat{\psi}
. In addition, we will consider higher angular derivatives.

Lemma 12.2
.

Let
k,m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
r_{+}\leq\varrho\leq\frac{1}{2}R_{\infty}
. Then, for
\widehat{\phi}=r^{-1}\widehat{\psi}
:

(12.3)

\partial_{r}^{k}\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{m}(r^{-3}\rho^{2}\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa}\widehat{\psi})=\Delta\partial_{r}^{2+k}\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{m}\widehat{\phi}+(1+k)\frac{d\Delta}{dr}\partial_{r}^{k+1}\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{m}\widehat{\phi}+2a\partial_{\varphi_{*}}\partial_{r}^{k+1}\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{m}\widehat{\phi}+\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{1+m}\partial_{r}^{k}\widehat{\phi}\\
+4Mrs\partial_{r}^{k+1}\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{m}\widehat{\phi}-(\lambda\pi_{\leq l}-k(k+1)-4kMs)\partial_{r}^{k}\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{m}\widehat{\phi}-2\sum_{j=0}^{k}\frac{k!}{j!(k-j)!}\partial_{r}^{k-j}((\mathbbm{h}-1)\Delta)\partial_{r}^{j}\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{m}\widehat{\phi}.

Proof.

We will first prove (
12.3
) with
m=0
via induction. The
k=0
case follows from (
7.10
). Assume (
12.3
) holds for
k=K
. Then

\partial_{r}^{K+1}(r^{-3}\rho^{2}\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa}\widehat{\psi})=\partial_{r}\Big(\Delta\partial_{r}^{2+K}\widehat{\phi}+(1+K)\frac{d\Delta}{dr}\partial_{r}^{K+1}\widehat{\phi}+2a\partial_{\varphi_{*}}\partial_{r}^{K+1}\widehat{\phi}+\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\partial_{r}^{K}\widehat{\phi}+4Mrs\partial_{r}^{K+1}\widehat{\phi}\\
-(\lambda\pi_{\leq l}-K(K+1)-4KMs)\partial_{r}^{K}\widehat{\phi}-2\sum_{j=0}^{K}\frac{K!}{j!(K-j)!}\partial_{r}^{K-j}((\mathbbm{h}-1)\Delta)\partial_{r}^{j}\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{m}\widehat{\phi}\Big)\\
=\partial_{r}^{3+K}\widehat{\phi}+(K+2)\frac{d\Delta}{dr}\partial_{r}^{K+2}\widehat{\phi}+2a\partial_{\varphi_{*}}\partial_{r}^{K+2}\widehat{\phi}+\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\partial_{r}^{K+1}\widehat{\phi}+4Mrs\partial_{r}^{K+2}\widehat{\phi}\\
-\left(\lambda\pi_{\leq l}-K(K+1)-\frac{d^{2}\Delta}{dr^{2}}(K+1)-4M(K+1)s\right)\partial_{r}^{K+1}\widehat{\phi}\\
-2\sum_{j=0}^{K+1}\frac{(K+1)!}{j!(K+1-j)!}\partial_{r}^{K+1-j}((\mathbbm{h}-1)\Delta)\partial_{r}^{j}\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{m}\widehat{\phi}.

Using that
K(K+1)+\frac{d^{2}\Delta}{dr^{2}}(K+1)=(K+2)(K+1)
, it therefore follows that (
12.3
) with
m=0
holds for
k=K+1
.

The case
m>0
follows immediately from the fact that
\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}
and
\partial_{r}^{K+1}(r^{-3}\rho^{2}\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa}(\cdot))
commute.
∎

We state now a higher-order version of Proposition
12.1
.

Corollary 12.3
.

Let
N_{+},m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,
{\re s}>-\kappa_{+}\left(\frac{1}{2}+N_{+}\right)
and
\delta=1+2N_{+}+2\kappa_{+}^{-1}\min\{{\re s},0\}
. Assume that

\lambda-N_{+}(N_{+}+1)\gg\frac{N_{+}^{2}M^{2}|s|^{2}}{1+2N_{+}+2\kappa_{+}^{-1}\min\{{\re s},0\}}.

Then there exists a constant
C_{s}=C_{s}(\kappa_{+},s)>0
and
C=C(N_{+})>0
such that:

(12.4)

\sum_{k=0}^{N_{+}}(1+2N_{+}+2\kappa_{+}^{-1}\min\{{\re s},0\})R_{\infty}^{2k}\int_{r_{+}}^{\frac{1}{3}R_{\infty}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(r-M)r^{\delta}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{m}\partial_{r}^{k+1}(r^{-1}\widehat{\psi})|^{2}\\
+r^{-3+\delta}\left[|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{m+1}\partial_{r}^{k}\widehat{\psi}|^{2}+(\lambda\pi_{\leq l}-k(k+1))|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{m}\partial_{r}^{k}\widehat{\psi}|^{2}\right]\,d\upsigma dr\\
\leq\boxed{C\sum_{k=0}^{N_{+}}R_{\infty}^{2k-2}\int_{\frac{1}{3}R_{\infty}}^{\frac{1}{2}R_{\infty}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}r^{-1+\delta}\left[|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{m+1}\partial_{r}^{k}\widehat{\psi}|^{2}+(\lambda\pi_{\leq l}-k(k+1))|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{m}\partial_{r}^{k}\widehat{\psi}|^{2}\right]\,d\upsigma dr}\\
+C_{s}R_{\infty}^{2N_{+}}\int_{r_{+}}^{\frac{1}{2}R_{\infty}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}r^{-1+\delta}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{m}\partial_{r}^{N_{+}}(r^{-3}\rho^{2}\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa}\widehat{\psi})|^{2}\,d\upsigma dr.

Proof.

We repeat the proof of Proposition
12.1
, but with (
7.10
) replaced by the higher-order equation (
12.3
) and, for
m>0
, we additionally integrate by parts in the
\mathbb{S}^{2}
direction. This gives for
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
:

(12.5)

\int_{r_{+}}^{\frac{1}{2}R_{\infty}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(r-M)(1+2k+2\kappa_{+}^{-1}\min\{{\re s},0\})r^{\delta}\chi_{r\leq\frac{1}{3}R_{\infty}}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{m}\partial_{r}^{k+1}\widehat{\phi}|^{2}\\
+\frac{2}{3}(1+2k+2\kappa_{+}^{-1}\min\{{\re s},0\})r^{-1+\delta}\chi_{r\leq\frac{1}{3}R_{\infty}}\left[|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{m+1}\partial_{r}^{k}\widehat{\phi}|^{2}+(\lambda\pi_{\leq l}-k(k+1))|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{m}\partial_{r}^{k}\widehat{\phi}|^{2}\right]\,d\upsigma dr\\
+\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}r^{\delta}\left[|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{m+1}\partial_{r}^{k}\widehat{\phi}|^{2}+(\lambda\pi_{\leq l}-k(k+1))|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{m}\partial_{r}^{k}\widehat{\phi}|^{2}\right]\Big|_{r=r_{+}}\,d\upsigma\\
\leq C\int_{\frac{1}{3}R_{\infty}}^{\frac{1}{2}R_{\infty}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}r^{-1+\delta}\left[|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{m+1}\partial_{r}^{k}\widehat{\phi}|^{2}+(\lambda\pi_{\leq l}-k(k+1))|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{m}\partial_{r}^{k}\widehat{\phi}|^{2}\right]\,d\upsigma dr\\
+Ck^{2}M^{2}|s|^{2}\int_{r_{+}}^{\frac{1}{2}R_{\infty}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}r^{\delta}\chi_{r\leq\frac{1}{3}R_{\infty}}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{m}\partial_{r}^{k}\widehat{\phi}|^{2}\,d\upsigma dr+C_{k}M^{2}|s|^{2}\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}\int_{r_{+}}^{\frac{1}{2}R_{\infty}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}r^{\delta}\chi_{r\leq\frac{1}{3}R_{\infty}}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{m}\partial_{r}^{j}\widehat{\phi}|^{2}\,d\upsigma dr\\
+C_{s}\int_{r_{+}}^{\frac{1}{2}R_{\infty}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}r^{-1+\delta}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{m}\partial_{r}^{k}(r^{-3}\rho^{2}\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa}\widehat{\psi})|^{2}\,d\upsigma dr.

Note that we can absorb the term with a factor
Ck^{2}M^{2}|s|^{2}
into

+\frac{2}{3}(1+2k+2\kappa_{+}^{-1}\min\{{\re s},0\})r^{-1+\delta}\chi_{r\leq\frac{1}{3}R_{\infty}}\left[|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{m+1}\partial_{r}^{k}\widehat{\phi}|^{2}+(\lambda\pi_{\leq l}-k(k+1))|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{m}\partial_{r}^{k}\widehat{\phi}|^{2}\right]\,d\upsigma dr,

if we take
\lambda-k(k+1)\gg\frac{k^{2}|s|^{2}}{1+2k+2\kappa_{+}^{-1}\min\{{\re s},0\}}
. This gives the desired control for
k=N_{+}
in the sum on the left-hand side of (
12.5
).

In order to control the
k<N_{+}
terms in the sum on the left-hand side of (
12.5
) as well as bound the
k<N+1
terms on the right-hand side of (
12.5
), we will use that we can absorb these lower-order terms into the
k=N_{+}
terms by applying a standard Hardy inequality
N_{+}-k
times, see for example
[
AAG23a
]
[Lemma 2.6, eq. (2.21)], and by taking
\lambda
to be appropriately large. For example,

\int_{r_{+}}^{\frac{1}{2}R_{\infty}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\chi_{r\leq\frac{1}{3}R_{\infty}}r^{-1+\delta}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\partial_{r}^{k-1}\widehat{\phi}|^{2}\,d\upsigma dr\leq C\int_{r_{+}}^{\frac{1}{2}R_{\infty}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\chi_{r\leq\frac{1}{3}R_{\infty}}r^{1+\delta}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\partial_{r}^{k}\widehat{\phi}|^{2}\,d\upsigma dr\\
+C\int_{\frac{1}{3}R_{\infty}}^{\frac{1}{2}R_{\infty}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}r^{-1+\delta}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\partial_{r}^{k-1}\widehat{\phi}|^{2}\,d\upsigma dr\\
\leq CR_{\infty}^{2}\int_{r_{+}}^{\frac{1}{2}R_{\infty}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\chi_{r\leq\frac{1}{3}R_{\infty}}r^{-1+\delta}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\partial_{r}^{k}\widehat{\phi}|^{2}\,d\upsigma dr+C\int_{\frac{1}{3}R_{\infty}}^{\frac{1}{2}R_{\infty}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}r^{-1+\delta}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\partial_{r}^{k-1}\widehat{\phi}|^{2}\,d\upsigma dr.\qed

In the sections below, we will consider a coupled system of estimates consisting of (
12.4
) with
k+l\leq N_{+}
and
l\geq 1
, and appropriate elliptic estimates in the region
\{r\geq\frac{1}{4}R_{\infty}\}
. In this way, we will be able to absorb the integral over the region
\{\frac{1}{3}R_{\infty}\leq r\leq\frac{1}{2}R_{\infty}\}
in the boxed term on the right-hand side of (
12.4
).

We can in fact improve the estimate in (
12.3
) in the case of
|a|\ll M
or under the restriction to fixed azimuthal modes by applying a standard elliptic estimate in
r\leq\frac{1}{2}R_{\infty}
in order to include higher-order derivative terms on the right-hand side, with weights that degenerate at
r=r_{+}
. This will be relevant when considering the cut-off resolvent operator.

Proposition 12.4
.

Let
N_{+},m\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,
{\re s}>-\kappa_{+}\left(\frac{1}{2}+N_{+}\right)
and
\delta=\frac{2}{3}(1+2N_{+}+2\kappa_{+}^{-1}\min\{{\re s},0\})
. Assume that

\lambda-N_{+}(N_{+}+1)\gg\frac{N_{+}^{2}M^{2}|s|^{2}}{1+2N_{+}+2\kappa_{+}^{-1}\min\{{\re s},0\}}.

Then for
|a|\ll M
or
\widehat{\psi}=\sum_{m=-m_{0}}^{m_{0}}\widehat{\psi}_{m}
, with
m_{0}>0
and
\Phi\psi_{m}=im\psi_{m}
, there exists a constant
C_{s}=C_{s}(a,m_{0},\kappa_{+},s)>0
and
C=C(N_{+},m_{0})>0
such that:

(12.6)

\sum_{k=0}^{N_{+}}(1+2N_{+}+2\kappa_{+}^{-1}\min\{{\re s},0\})R_{\infty}^{2k}\int_{r_{+}}^{\frac{1}{3}R_{\infty}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(r-M)r^{\delta}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{m}\partial_{r}^{k+1}(r^{-1}\widehat{\psi})|^{2}+\Delta^{2}r^{\delta-1}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{m}\partial_{r}^{k+2}(r^{-1}\widehat{\psi})|^{2}\\
+r^{-3+\delta}\left[|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{m+2}\partial_{r}^{k}\widehat{\psi}|^{2}+\Delta|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{m+1}\partial_{r}^{k+1}\widehat{\psi}|^{2}+(\lambda\pi_{\leq l}-k(k+1))^{2}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{m}\partial_{r}^{k}\widehat{\psi}|^{2}\right]\,d\upsigma dr\\
\leq\boxed{C\sum_{k=0}^{N_{+}}R_{\infty}^{2k}\int_{\frac{1}{3}R_{\infty}}^{\frac{1}{2}R_{\infty}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}r^{-3+\delta}\left[|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{m+1}\partial_{r}^{k}\widehat{\psi}|^{2}+(\lambda\pi_{\leq l}-k(k+1))|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{m}\partial_{r}^{k}\widehat{\psi}|^{2}\right]\,d\upsigma dr}\\
+C_{s}R_{\infty}^{2N_{+}}\int_{r_{+}}^{\frac{1}{2}R_{\infty}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}r^{-1+\delta}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{m}\partial_{r}^{N_{+}}(r^{-3}\rho^{2}\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa}\widehat{\psi})|^{2}\,d\upsigma dr.

Proof.

We complement the estimates in the proof of Proposition
12.3
with a standard elliptic estimate. In the
N_{+}=0
case, we integrate

\chi|r^{-2}\rho^{2}\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa}\widehat{\phi}+\lambda\pi_{\leq l}\widehat{\phi}-sr(4Mr-2(1-\mathbbm{h})\Delta)\partial_{r}\widehat{\phi}-r\frac{d\Delta}{dr}\partial_{r}\widehat{\phi}-2ar\partial_{r}\partial_{\varphi_{*}}\widehat{\phi}|^{2}=\chi|r\Delta\partial_{r}^{2}\widehat{\phi}+\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\widehat{\phi}|^{2}

and integrate by parts the mixed term arising from the square norm on the right-hand side.

We moreover use that the integral of
\chi r^{2}a^{2}|\partial_{r}\partial_{\varphi_{*}}\widehat{\phi}|^{2}
can be absorbed into the remaining terms of
|a|
sufficiently small or for
\widehat{\phi}
supported on bounded set of azimuthal modes.
∎

12.2.
Weighted elliptic estimates in (B)

Here we will use that

R_{\infty}\gg M,

so in the region
\varrho\geq\frac{1}{4}R_{\infty}
, the operator
\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa}
is suitably close to the analogous operator in Minkowski (i.e. the
M=0
case).

To make the elliptic estimates compatible with the Gevrey estimates in §
13.2
, we will need to add a sufficiently degenerate weight to the elliptic estimates:
(\mathfrak{l}+1)^{-w_{p}}
, where
w_{p}:[0,r_{+}^{-1}]\to\mathbb{R}
was defined in (
8.1
). Recall,

w_{p}(x)=\begin{cases}p&x\leq\frac{1}{4R_{\infty}},\\
0&x\geq\frac{1}{3R_{\infty}}.\end{cases}

We make the following observations regarding
w
:

•

We have that
w\geq 0
and
w\equiv 0
in the region
\varrho\leq 3R_{\infty}
where
\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa}
does not agree with the corresponding operator in Minkowski.

•

By employing standard cut-off functions, we can define
w
such that

(12.7)

\left|\left|\frac{dw_{p}}{dx}\right|\right|_{L^{\infty}[0,r_{+}^{-1}]}\leq CpR_{\infty},

for some numerical constant
C>0
.

From (
7.12
), it follows that we can split

\displaystyle\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa}\widehat{\psi}=

\displaystyle\>\partial_{x}(x(x+\kappa\chi_{\varrho\geq R_{\infty}})\partial_{x}\widehat{\psi})+(\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2},\vartheta}-\lambda\pi_{\leq l}-\chi_{\varrho\geq R_{\infty}}S_{\lambda}\pi_{\leq l})\widehat{\psi}+\mathfrak{R}_{s,\lambda},\ \textnormal{with}

\displaystyle\mathfrak{R}_{s,\lambda}\widehat{\psi}:=

\displaystyle\>2s\partial_{x}\widehat{\psi}+\chi_{\varrho\leq R_{\infty}}(\mathcal{K}_{s}^{\infty}\widehat{\psi}+Mx\mathcal{B}_{s}^{\infty}\widehat{\psi})-\chi_{\varrho\leq\frac{1}{2}R_{\infty}}\mathcal{K}_{s}^{+}\widehat{\psi}.

We will treat
\mathfrak{R}_{s,\lambda}\widehat{\psi}
as an inhomogeneous term that can be absorbed, because it will come with additional factors of
x
compared to the terms on the left-hand side of the elliptic estimates and
x
will be small in the regions under consideration. Note that
\mathfrak{R}_{s,\lambda}\widehat{\psi}-2s\partial_{x}
contains all deviations from corresponding operator in Minkowski. This will follow from the following lemma:

Lemma 12.5
.

Let
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. Then there exists a numerical constant
C=C(k)>0
, such that

\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}|\partial_{x}^{k}\mathfrak{R}_{s,\lambda}\widehat{\psi}|^{2}\,d\upsigma\leq C\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\Big[|s|^{2}|\partial_{x}^{k+1}\widehat{\psi}|^{2}+M^{2}\chi_{\varrho\leq R_{\infty}}\Big(x^{6}|\partial_{x}^{k+2}\widehat{\psi}|^{2}+x^{4}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\partial_{x}^{k+1}\widehat{\psi}|^{2}+x^{2}|\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\partial_{x}^{k}\widehat{\psi}|^{2}\\
+x^{2}|\partial_{x}^{k+1}\widehat{\psi}|^{2}+|s|^{2}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\partial_{x}^{k}\widehat{\psi}|^{2}+(|s|^{2}+x^{2})|\partial_{x}^{k}\widehat{\psi}|^{2}\Big)\Big]\,d\upsigma.

Proof.

When
\varrho>R_{\infty}
, we simply have that

\mathfrak{R}_{s,\lambda}\widehat{\psi}=2s\partial_{x}\widehat{\psi},

so the estimate is immediate. When
\varrho\leq R_{\infty}
, we observe that the top-order derivative terms in
x\mathcal{B}_{s}^{\infty}
and
\mathcal{K}_{s}^{+}
have additional powers of
x
compared to the top-order derivative terms in the remaining terms of
\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa}
.
∎

Proposition 12.6
.

Let
0<\delta\leq 1
and assume the following relations:

\displaystyle 0<\eta<

\displaystyle\>\frac{1}{4},

\displaystyle\kappa\ll

\displaystyle\>\eta R_{\infty}^{-1},

\displaystyle M^{-1}R_{\infty}\gg

\displaystyle\>1,

\displaystyle\lambda\gg

\displaystyle\>1+|s|^{2}\eta^{-2}R_{\infty}^{2}+p.

Then, there exists a numerical constant
C>0
such that:

(12.8)

\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\int_{\eta R_{\infty}^{-1}}^{3R_{\infty}^{-1}}x^{1-\delta}(\mathfrak{l}+1)^{-w_{p}}\Bigg(|\partial_{x}(x(x+\kappa\chi_{\varrho\geq R_{\infty}})\partial_{x}\widehat{\psi}_{\ell})|^{2}+|(\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}-\lambda\pi_{\leq l}-\chi_{\varrho\geq R_{\infty}}S_{\lambda}\pi_{\leq l})\widehat{\psi}_{\ell}|^{2}\\
+2x(x+\kappa\chi_{\varrho\geq R_{\infty}})\left[|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\partial_{x}\widehat{\psi}_{\ell}|^{2}+\lambda\pi_{\leq l}|\partial_{x}\widehat{\psi}_{\ell}|^{2}+\chi_{\varrho\geq R_{\infty}}|\sqrt{S_{\lambda}}\pi_{\leq l}\partial_{x}\widehat{\psi}_{\ell}|^{2}\right]\Bigg)\,d\upsigma dx\\
\leq\boxed{C\int_{\frac{1}{4}R_{\infty}}^{\frac{1}{3}R_{\infty}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}r^{-2+\delta}\left|(\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}-\lambda\pi_{\leq l})\widehat{\psi}\right|\cdot\left|\partial_{r}\widehat{\psi}\right|\,d\upsigma dr}\\
+\boxed{C(\mathfrak{l}+1)^{-p}\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}x^{2-\delta}(x+\kappa)\mathfrak{l}(\mathfrak{l}+1)|\widehat{\psi}_{\ell}|\cdot|\partial_{x}\widehat{\psi}_{\ell}|\,d\upsigma\Big|_{x=\eta R_{\infty}^{-1}}}\\
+C\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\int_{\eta R_{\infty}^{-1}}^{4R_{\infty}^{-1}}x^{1-\delta}(\mathfrak{l}+1)^{-w_{p}}|(\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa}\widehat{\psi})_{\ell}|^{2}\,d\upsigma dx.

Proof.

We split:

\partial_{x}(x(x+\kappa\chi_{\varrho\geq R_{\infty}})\partial_{x}\widehat{\psi})+(\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}-\lambda\pi_{\leq l}-\chi_{\varrho\geq R_{\infty}}S_{\lambda}\pi_{\leq l})\widehat{\psi}=\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa}\widehat{\psi}-\mathfrak{R}_{s,\lambda}\widehat{\psi}

Then we consider the
\ell
-th spherical harmonics
\widehat{\psi}_{\ell}
, take the square norm of both sides, multiply both sides with the weight factor
x^{q}(\mathfrak{l}+1)^{-w_{p}}\chi_{\varrho\geq\frac{1}{3}R_{\infty}}
, with
q\in\mathbb{R}
and
\chi_{\varrho\geq\frac{1}{3}R_{\infty}}
a smooth cut-off function which equals 1 when
\varrho\geq\frac{1}{3}R_{\infty}
and vanishes for
\varrho\leq\frac{1}{4}R_{\infty}
, and we integrate over
\mathbb{S}^{2}
to obtain:

\overbrace{\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}x^{q}(\mathfrak{l}+1)^{-w_{p}}\chi_{\varrho\geq\frac{1}{3}R_{\infty}}\left(|\partial_{x}(x(x+\kappa\chi_{\varrho\geq R_{\infty}})\partial_{x}\widehat{\psi}_{\ell})|^{2}+|(\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}-\lambda\pi_{\leq l}-\chi_{\varrho\geq R_{\infty}}S_{\lambda}\pi_{\leq l})\widehat{\psi}_{\ell}|^{2}\right)\,d\upsigma}^{=:J_{0}}\\
+\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}2x^{q}(\mathfrak{l}+1)^{-w_{p}}\chi_{\varrho\geq\frac{1}{3}R_{\infty}}\re\left((\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}-\lambda\pi_{\leq l}-\chi_{\varrho\geq R_{\infty}}S_{\lambda}\pi_{\leq l})\overline{\widehat{\psi}}_{\ell}\cdot\partial_{x}(x(x+\kappa\chi_{\varrho\geq R_{\infty}})\partial_{x}\widehat{\psi}_{\ell})\right)\,d\upsigma\\
=\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}x^{q}(\mathfrak{l}+1)^{-w_{p}}\chi_{\varrho\geq\frac{1}{3}R_{\infty}}|(\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa}\widehat{\psi})_{\ell}-(\mathfrak{R}_{s,\lambda}\widehat{\psi})_{\ell}|^{2}\,d\upsigma.

For the sake of convenience, we will restrict to
\ell\leq l
. The
\ell>l
case proceeds entirely analogously, but without the
\lambda
and
S_{\lambda}
terms.

We integrate by parts on
\mathbb{S}^{2}
to obtain:

\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}2x^{q}(\mathfrak{l}+1)^{-w_{p}}\chi_{\varrho\geq\frac{1}{3}R_{\infty}}\re\left((\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}-\lambda-\chi_{\varrho\geq R_{\infty}}S_{\lambda})\overline{\widehat{\psi}}_{\ell}\cdot\partial_{x}(x(x+\kappa\chi_{\varrho\geq R_{\infty}})\partial_{x}\widehat{\psi}_{\ell}\right)\,d\upsigma\\
=\overbrace{2\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\partial_{x}\left[x^{q}(\mathfrak{l}+1)^{-w_{p}}\chi_{\varrho\geq\frac{1}{3}R_{\infty}}\re\left((\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}-\lambda-\chi_{\varrho\geq R_{\infty}}S_{\lambda})\overline{\widehat{\psi}}_{\ell}\cdot x(x+\kappa\chi_{\varrho\geq R_{\infty}})\partial_{x}\widehat{\psi}_{\ell}\right)\right]\,d\upsigma}^{=:J_{1}}\\
+\overbrace{2\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}x^{q}(\mathfrak{l}+1)^{-w_{p}}x(x+\kappa\chi_{\varrho\geq R_{\infty}})\chi_{\varrho\geq\frac{1}{3}R_{\infty}}\left[|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\partial_{x}\widehat{\psi}_{\ell}|^{2}+\lambda|\partial_{x}\widehat{\psi}_{\ell}|^{2}+\chi_{\varrho\geq R_{\infty}}|\sqrt{S_{\lambda}}\partial_{x}\widehat{\psi}_{\ell}|^{2}\right]\,d\upsigma}^{=:J_{2}}\\
\overbrace{-2\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\partial_{x}(x^{q}(\mathfrak{l}+1)^{-w_{p}})x(x+\kappa\chi_{\varrho\geq R_{\infty}})\chi_{\varrho\geq\frac{1}{3}R_{\infty}}\re\left((\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}-\lambda-\chi_{\varrho\geq R_{\infty}}S_{\lambda})\overline{\widehat{\psi}}_{\ell}\cdot\partial_{x}\widehat{\psi}_{\ell}\right)\,d\upsigma}^{=:J_{3}}\\
+\overbrace{2\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}x^{q}(\mathfrak{l}+1)^{-w_{p}}x(x+\kappa\chi_{\varrho\geq R_{\infty}})\partial_{x}(\chi_{\varrho\geq R_{\infty}})\chi_{\varrho\geq\frac{1}{3}R_{\infty}}\re(S_{\lambda}\overline{\widehat{\psi}}_{\ell}\cdot\partial_{x}\widehat{\psi}_{\ell})\,d\upsigma}^{=:J_{4}}\\
-\overbrace{2\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}x^{q}(\mathfrak{l}+1)^{-w_{p}}x(x+\kappa\chi_{\varrho\geq R_{\infty}})\partial_{x}(\chi_{\varrho\geq\frac{1}{3}R_{\infty}})\re\left((\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}-\lambda-\chi_{\varrho\geq R_{\infty}}S_{\lambda})\overline{\widehat{\psi}}_{\ell}\cdot\partial_{x}\widehat{\psi}_{\ell}\right)\,d\upsigma}^{=:J_{5}}.

Note first that
J_{2}
is non-negative definite. We will show that we can absorb
J_{3}
and
J_{4}
into
J_{0}
and
J_{2}
by taking
\lambda
suitably large.

We apply Young’s inequality together with (
12.7
) to obtain for
x\in[\eta R_{\infty}^{-1},4R^{-1}_{\infty}]
:

|J_{3}|\leq\\
\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\left(|q|x^{-1}+\left|\frac{dw_{p}}{dx}\right|(\mathfrak{l}+1)^{-1}\right)x^{q+1}(x+\kappa\chi_{\varrho\geq R_{\infty}})(\mathfrak{l}+1)^{-w_{p}}(\ell(\ell+1)+\lambda+2l\ell\chi_{\varrho\geq R_{\infty}})|\widehat{\psi}_{\ell}\|\partial_{x}\widehat{\psi}_{\ell}|\,d\upsigma\\
\leq\left(\frac{|q|}{\mathfrak{l}x}+CpR_{\infty}(\mathfrak{l}+1)^{-1}\mathfrak{l}^{-1}\right)\mathfrak{l}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}x^{q+1}(x+\kappa\chi_{\varrho\geq R_{\infty}})(\mathfrak{l}+1)^{-w_{p}}(\ell(\ell+1)+\lambda+2l\ell\chi_{\varrho\geq R_{\infty}})|\widehat{\psi}_{\ell}\|\partial_{x}\widehat{\psi}_{\ell}|\,d\upsigma\\
\leq\frac{1}{4}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}x^{q}(\mathfrak{l}+1)^{-w_{p}}|(\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}-\lambda-\chi_{\varrho\geq R_{\infty}}S_{\lambda})\widehat{\psi}_{\ell}|^{2}\,d\upsigma\\
+C\left(\frac{|q|}{\mathfrak{l}x}+CpR_{\infty}(\mathfrak{l}+1)^{-1}\mathfrak{l}^{-1}\right)^{2}x(x+\kappa\chi_{\varrho\geq R_{\infty}})\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}x^{q}(\mathfrak{l}+1)^{-w_{p}}x(x+\kappa\chi_{\varrho\geq R_{\infty}})\mathfrak{l}^{2}|\partial_{x}\widehat{\psi}_{\ell}|^{2}\,d\upsigma.

The first term on the very right-hand side above can be absorbed into
J_{0}
. The second term on the right-hand side can be absorbed into
J_{2}
by taking
\kappa\ll\eta R_{\infty}^{-1}
and
\lambda\gg q^{2}+p
to obtain:

C\left(\frac{|q|}{\mathfrak{l}x}+CpR_{\infty}\mathfrak{l}^{-2}\right)^{2}x(x+\kappa\chi_{\varrho\geq R_{\infty}})=C\left(|q|\mathfrak{l}^{-1}+CpxR_{\infty}\mathfrak{l}^{-2}\right)^{2}(1+\kappa x^{-1}\chi_{\varrho\geq R_{\infty}})\leq\frac{1}{4}.

and noting that

\mathfrak{l}(\mathfrak{l}+1)=\ell(\ell+1)+l(l+1)+2l\ell\leq 2(\ell(\ell+1)+2l(l+1)).

We conclude that
|J_{3}|\leq\frac{1}{4}J_{0}+\frac{1}{4}J_{2}
.

To estimate
|J_{4}|
, we first note that there exists a constant
C>0
such that
\left|\frac{d\chi_{\varrho\geq R_{\infty}}}{d\varrho}\right|\leq CR_{\infty}^{-1}
, so that

\left|\frac{d\chi_{\varrho\geq R_{\infty}}}{dx}\right|\leq CR_{\infty}.

By repeating the estimates for
|J_{3}|
and taking
\lambda\gg M^{-1}R_{\infty}
, we conclude that
|J_{4}|\leq\frac{1}{4}J_{0}+\frac{1}{4}J_{2}
. We conclude that

\sum_{j=0}^{4}J_{j}\geq\frac{1}{2}(J_{0}+J_{2}).

We can estimate
J_{5}
by observing that
\partial_{x}\chi_{\varrho\geq\frac{1}{3}R_{\infty}}
is supported in
\frac{1}{4}R_{\infty}\leq\varrho\leq\frac{1}{3}R_{\infty}
, where
\varrho=r
,
w(x)=0
and there exists a numerical constant
C>0
such that
|\partial_{x}\chi_{\varrho\geq\frac{1}{3}R_{\infty}}|\leq CR_{\infty}
. We obtain:

|J_{5}|\leq\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}r^{-q+1}|(\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}-\lambda)\widehat{\psi}_{\ell}|\cdot|\partial_{r}\widehat{\psi}_{\ell}|\,d\upsigma

and
\supp J_{5}\subset\{\frac{1}{4}R_{\infty}\leq r\leq\frac{1}{3}R_{\infty}\}
.

We apply Young’s inequality to estimate:

\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}x^{q}(\mathfrak{l}+1)^{-w_{p}}|(\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa}\widehat{\psi})_{\ell}-(\mathfrak{R}_{s,\lambda}\widehat{\psi})_{\ell}|^{2}\,d\upsigma\\
\leq 2\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}x^{q}(\mathfrak{l}+1)^{-w_{p}}|(\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa}\widehat{\psi})_{\ell}|^{2}\,d\upsigma+2\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}x^{q}(\mathfrak{l}+1)^{-w_{p}}|(\mathfrak{R}_{s,\lambda}\widehat{\psi})_{\ell}|^{2}\,d\upsigma.

Note that
(\mathfrak{R}_{s,\lambda}\widehat{\psi})_{\ell}
does not need to agree with
(\mathfrak{R}_{s,\lambda}(\widehat{\psi}_{\ell}))
. However, since
w\equiv 0
in
\{\varrho\leq 2R_{\infty}\}
and
\mathfrak{R}_{s,\lambda}\widehat{\psi}=2s\partial_{x}\widehat{\psi}
in
r\geq R_{\infty}
, we can conclude that:

\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(\mathfrak{l}+1)^{-w_{p}}|(\mathfrak{R}_{s,\lambda}\widehat{\psi})_{\ell}|^{2}\,d\upsigma=\begin{cases}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}|\mathfrak{R}_{s,\lambda}\widehat{\psi}|^{2}\,d\upsigma\quad&\textnormal{if}\quad x\geq\frac{1}{2R_{\infty}},\\
\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(\mathfrak{l}+1)^{-w_{p}}|\mathfrak{R}_{s,\lambda}(\widehat{\psi}_{\ell})|^{2}\,d\upsigma\quad&\textnormal{if}\quad x<\frac{1}{2R_{\infty}}.\end{cases}

By applying Lemma
12.5
, we can take
MR_{\infty}^{-1}\ll 1
and
\lambda\gg|s|^{2}\eta^{-2}R_{\infty}^{2}
to obtain:

2\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}x^{q}(\mathfrak{l}+1)^{-w_{p}}|(\mathfrak{R}_{s,\lambda}\widehat{\psi})_{\ell}|^{2}\,d\upsigma\leq\frac{1}{4}\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}(J_{0}+J_{2}).

We now integrate over
x
to obtain:

\frac{1}{4}\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\int_{x(\eta^{-1})}^{x(\frac{1}{4}R_{\infty})}x^{q}(\mathfrak{l}+1)^{-w_{p}}\Bigg(|\partial_{x}(x(x+\kappa\chi_{\varrho\geq R_{\infty}})\partial_{x}\widehat{\psi}_{\ell})|^{2}+|(\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}-\lambda-\chi_{\varrho\geq R_{\infty}}S_{\lambda})\widehat{\psi}_{\ell}|^{2}\\
+2x(x+\kappa\chi_{\varrho\geq R_{\infty}})\left[|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\partial_{x}\widehat{\psi}_{\ell}|^{2}+\lambda|\partial_{x}\widehat{\psi}_{\ell}|^{2}+\chi_{\varrho\geq R_{\infty}}|\sqrt{S_{\lambda}}\partial_{x}\widehat{\psi}_{\ell}|^{2}\right]\Bigg)\,d\upsigma dx\\
\leq C\int_{\frac{1}{4}R_{\infty}}^{\frac{1}{3}R_{\infty}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}r^{-q-1}\left|(\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}-\lambda)\widehat{\psi}\right|\cdot\left|\partial_{r}\widehat{\psi}\right|\,d\upsigma dr\\
+2(\mathfrak{l}+1)^{-p}\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}x^{q+1}(x+\kappa)\mathfrak{l}(\mathfrak{l}+1)|\widehat{\psi}_{\ell}|\cdot|\partial_{x}\widehat{\psi}_{\ell}|\,d\upsigma\Big|_{x=\eta R_{\infty}^{-1}}\\
+2\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\int_{x(\eta^{-1})}^{x(\frac{1}{4}R_{\infty})}x^{q}(\mathfrak{l}+1)^{-w_{p}}|(\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa}\widehat{\psi})_{\ell}|^{2}\,d\upsigma dx,

and we conclude that (
12.8
) holds after taking
q=1-\delta
.
∎

We will also consider higher-order analogues of (
12.8
). For this it is useful to define:

\widehat{\psi}^{(n)}:=\partial_{x}^{n}\widehat{\psi}.

Then it can be easily verified that:

\partial_{x}^{n}(\partial_{x}(x(x+\kappa\chi_{\varrho\geq R_{\infty}})\partial_{x}\widehat{\psi})+(\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}-\lambda\pi_{\leq l}-\chi_{\varrho\geq R_{\infty}}S_{\lambda}\pi_{\leq l})\widehat{\psi})=\partial_{x}(x(x+\kappa\chi_{\varrho\geq R_{\infty}})\partial_{x}\widehat{\psi}^{(n)}\\
+(\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}-\lambda\pi_{\leq l}-\chi_{\varrho\geq R_{\infty}}S_{\lambda}\pi_{\leq l})\widehat{\psi}^{(n)}+2(k+1)\left(x+\frac{1}{2}\kappa\chi_{\varrho\geq R_{\infty}}\right)\partial_{x}\widehat{\psi}^{(n)}\\
+\kappa\sum_{k=0}^{n+1}{n+1\choose k}\partial_{x}^{n+1-k}(x\chi_{\varrho\geq R_{\infty}})\partial_{x}\widehat{\psi}^{(k)}-\sum_{k=0}^{n}{n\choose k}\partial_{x}^{n-k}(\chi_{\varrho\geq R_{\infty}})S_{\lambda}\widehat{\psi}^{(k)}.

Define

\mathfrak{R}^{(n)}_{s,\lambda}:=\partial_{x}^{n}\mathfrak{R}_{s,\lambda}-2(k+1)\left(x+\frac{1}{2}\kappa\chi_{\varrho\geq R_{\infty}}\right)\partial_{x}\widehat{\psi}^{(n)}-\kappa\sum_{k=0}^{n+1}{n+1\choose k}\partial_{x}^{n+1-k}(x\chi_{\varrho\geq R_{\infty}})\partial_{x}\widehat{\psi}^{(k)}\\
+\sum_{k=0}^{n}{n\choose k}\partial_{x}^{n-k}(\chi_{\varrho\geq R_{\infty}})\pi_{\leq l}S_{\lambda}\widehat{\psi}^{(k)}.

Then

\partial_{x}^{n}(\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa}\widehat{\psi})=\partial_{x}(x(x+\kappa\chi_{\varrho\geq R_{\infty}})\partial_{x}\widehat{\psi}^{(n)})+(\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}-\lambda\pi_{\leq l}-\chi_{\varrho\geq R_{\infty}}S_{\lambda}\pi_{\leq l})\widehat{\psi}^{(n)}+\mathfrak{R}^{(n)}_{s,\lambda}.

We obtain the following analogue of Proposition
12.8
, including additional commutation with vector fields
(x^{2}\partial_{x})^{k}
.

Corollary 12.7
.

Let
N\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,
0<\delta<1
and
p\geq 0
and assume the following smallness relations:

\displaystyle 0<\eta<

\displaystyle\>\frac{1}{4},

\displaystyle\kappa\ll

\displaystyle\>\eta R_{\infty}^{-1},

\displaystyle M^{-1}R_{\infty}\gg

\displaystyle\>1,

\displaystyle\lambda-N(N+1)\gg

\displaystyle\>1+|s|^{2}\eta^{-2}R_{\infty}^{2}+p.

Then, there exists a numerical constant
C=C(N)>0
such that:

(12.9)

\sum_{k=0}^{N}\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\int_{x(\eta^{-1})}^{3R_{\infty}^{-1}}x^{1-\delta}(\mathfrak{l}+1)^{-w_{p}}\Bigg(|\partial_{x}(x(x+\kappa\chi_{\varrho\geq R_{\infty}})\partial_{x}(x^{2}\partial_{x})^{k}\widehat{\psi}_{\ell})|^{2}+|(\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}-\lambda\pi_{\leq l}-\chi_{\varrho\geq R_{\infty}}S_{\lambda}\pi_{\leq l})(x^{2}\partial_{x})^{k}\widehat{\psi}_{\ell}|^{2}\\
+2x(x+\kappa\chi_{\varrho\geq R_{\infty}})\left[|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\partial_{x}(x^{2}\partial_{x})^{k}\widehat{\psi}_{\ell}|^{2}+\lambda\pi_{\leq l}|\partial_{x}(x^{2}\partial_{x})^{k}\widehat{\psi}_{\ell}|^{2}+\chi_{\varrho\geq R_{\infty}}|\sqrt{S_{\lambda}\pi_{\leq l}}\partial_{x}(x^{2}\partial_{x})^{k}\widehat{\psi}_{\ell}|^{2}\right]\Bigg)\,d\upsigma dx\\
\leq\sum_{k=0}^{N}\boxed{C\int_{\frac{1}{4}R_{\infty}}^{\frac{1}{3}R_{\infty}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}r^{-2+\delta}\left|(\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}-\lambda\pi_{\leq l})\partial_{r}^{k}\widehat{\psi}\right|\cdot\left|\partial_{r}^{k+1}\widehat{\psi}\right|\,d\upsigma dr}\\
+\boxed{C(\mathfrak{l}+1)^{-p}\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}x^{2+4k-\delta}(x+\kappa)\mathfrak{l}(\mathfrak{l}+1)|\widehat{\psi}_{\ell}^{(k)}|\cdot|\partial_{x}\widehat{\psi}_{\ell}^{(k)}|\,d\upsigma\Big|_{x=\eta R_{\infty}^{-1}}}\\
+C\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\int_{x(\eta^{-1})}^{4R_{\infty}^{-1}}x^{1-\delta}(\mathfrak{l}+1)^{-w_{p}}|(x^{2}\partial_{x})^{k}(\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa}\widehat{\psi})_{\ell}|^{2}\,d\upsigma dx.

Proof.

We repeat the proof of Proposition
12.6
with
\widehat{\psi}^{(k)}
replacing
\widehat{\psi}
and with
q=1-\delta+4k
, sum over
k
, and use that all the terms coming from
\mathfrak{R}^{(n)}_{s,\lambda}
can be absorbed in the terms
J_{0}
and
J_{2}
.
∎

12.3.
Combining the estimates in (A) and (B)

In this section we combine the estimates from Corollary
12.3
and Corollary
12.7
by taking
\lambda
suitably large.

Corollary 12.8
.

Let
N_{+}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,
0<\delta<1
,
p\geq 0
, and
{\re s}>-\kappa_{+}\left(\frac{1}{2}+N_{+}\right)
, and assume the following smallness relations:

\displaystyle 0<\eta<

\displaystyle\>\frac{1}{4},

\displaystyle\kappa\ll

\displaystyle\>\eta R_{\infty}^{-1},

\displaystyle M^{-1}R_{\infty}\gg

\displaystyle\>1,

\displaystyle\lambda-N_{+}(N_{+}+1)\gg

\displaystyle\>p+N_{+}|s|^{2}(1+2N_{+}+2\kappa_{+}^{-1}{\min\{\re s,0\}})^{-1}+(1+2N_{+}+2\kappa_{+}^{-1}{\min\{\re s,0\}})^{-2}

\displaystyle+|s|^{2}\eta^{-2}R_{\infty}^{2}+(M^{-1}R_{\infty})^{2(1+N_{+})}.

Then, there exists a constant
C=C(N_{+})>0
such that:

(12.10)

\sum_{k=0}^{N_{+}}\int_{r_{+}}^{\frac{1}{2}R_{\infty}}|\partial_{r}^{1+k}\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\widehat{\psi}|^{2}+\lambda|\partial_{r}^{1+k}\widehat{\psi}|^{2}+|(\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}-\lambda\pi_{\leq l})\widehat{\psi}|^{2}+\chi_{r\geq\frac{1}{3}R_{\infty}}|\partial_{r}^{2+k}\psi|^{2}\,d\upsigma dr\\
+\sum_{k=0}^{N_{+}}\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\int_{\eta R_{\infty}^{-1}}^{x(\frac{1}{2}R_{\infty})}x^{1-\delta}(\mathfrak{l}+1)^{-w_{p}}\Bigg(|\partial_{x}(x(x+\kappa\chi_{\varrho\geq R_{\infty}})\partial_{x}(x^{2}\partial_{x})^{k}\widehat{\psi}_{\ell})|^{2}+|(\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}-\lambda\pi_{\leq l}-\chi_{\varrho\geq R_{\infty}}S_{\lambda}\pi_{\leq l})(x^{2}\partial_{x})^{k}\widehat{\psi}_{\ell}|^{2}\\
+2x(x+\kappa\chi_{\varrho\geq R_{\infty}})\left[|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\partial_{x}(x^{2}\partial_{x})^{k}\widehat{\psi}_{\ell}|^{2}+\lambda\pi_{\leq l}|\partial_{x}(x^{2}\partial_{x})^{k}\widehat{\psi}_{\ell}|^{2}+\chi_{\varrho\geq R_{\infty}}|\sqrt{S_{\lambda}\pi_{\leq l}}\partial_{x}(x^{2}\partial_{x})^{k}\widehat{\psi}_{\ell}|^{2}\right]\Bigg)\,d\upsigma dx\\
\leq\boxed{C(\mathfrak{l}+1)^{-p}\sum_{k=0}^{N_{+}}\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}x^{2+4k-\delta}(x+\kappa)\mathfrak{l}(\mathfrak{l}+1)|\widehat{\psi}_{\ell}^{(k)}|\cdot|\partial_{x}\widehat{\psi}_{\ell}^{(k)}|\,d\upsigma\Big|_{x=\eta R_{\infty}^{-1}}}\\
+C_{s}\sum_{k=0}^{N_{+}}\int_{r_{+}}^{\frac{1}{2}R_{\infty}}|\partial_{r}^{k}\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa}\widehat{\psi}|^{2}+\chi_{r\leq\frac{1}{3}R_{\infty}}(|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\partial_{r}^{k}\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa}\widehat{\psi}|^{2}+\lambda\pi_{\leq l}|\partial_{r}^{k}\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa}\widehat{\psi}|^{2})\,d\upsigma dr\\
+C_{s}\sum_{k=0}^{N_{+}}\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\int_{\eta R_{\infty}^{-1}}^{x(\frac{1}{2}R_{\infty})}x^{1-\delta}(\mathfrak{l}+1)^{-w_{p}}|(x^{2}\partial_{x})^{k}(\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa}\widehat{\psi})_{\ell}|^{2}\,d\upsigma dx.

Furthermore, for suitably small
\frac{|a|}{M}
or for
\widehat{\phi}=\sum_{m=-m_{0}}^{m_{0}}\widehat{\phi}_{m}
, with
m_{0}>0
, we can omit the
|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\partial_{r}^{k}\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa}\widehat{\psi}|^{2}
term on the right-hand side.

Proof.

We apply Corollary
12.3
with arbitrary
N_{+}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
m\in\{0,1\}
and we also apply Corollary
12.7
with the same value of
N_{+}
. We estimate the first term on the right-hand side of (
12.9
) as follows: let
\epsilon>0
be arbitrarily small, then we apply Young’s inequality and use that
\widehat{\psi}=r\phi
in the region under consideration to obtain:

C\sum_{k=0}^{N_{+}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}r^{-2+\delta}\left|(\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}-\lambda\pi_{\leq l})\partial_{r}^{k}\widehat{\psi}\right|\cdot\left|\partial_{r}^{k+1}\widehat{\psi}\right|\,d\upsigma\leq\sum_{k=0}^{N_{+}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\epsilon r^{-1+\delta}\left|(\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}-\lambda\pi_{\leq l})\partial_{r}^{k}\widehat{\phi}\right|^{2}+\frac{C^{2}}{4\epsilon\lambda}\lambda r^{1+\delta}\left|\partial_{r}^{k+1}\widehat{\phi}\right|^{2}\,d\upsigma.

We take
\lambda
suitably large so that
\frac{C^{2}}{4\epsilon\lambda}<\epsilon
and
\epsilon\ll(1+2N_{+}+2\kappa_{+}^{-1}{\re s})
. Then we can absorb the right-hand side above into the left-hand side of (
12.4
) with
m=0
, and
m=1
, after multiplying the
m=0
estimates by a factor
\lambda
.

By taking
\epsilon
suitably small compared to the constant
CR_{\infty}^{2N_{+}}
appearing on the right-hand side of (
12.4
), we can also absorb the first integral on the right-hand side of (
12.4
) into the left-hand side of (
12.9
).
∎

Remark 12.1
.

Note that the estimate (
12.10
) still has
\widehat{\psi}
and its derivatives appearing in the boxed boundary term at
x=\eta R_{\infty}^{-1}
. In the next section we will show that we can absorb this term into the left-hand side of appropriate Gevrey-type estimates.

13.
Gevrey estimates

In this section, we consider estimates in the region
x\leq x_{0}
, where we define:

x_{0}:=\frac{1}{2R_{\infty}}.

By (
7.11
), we can express in the region
x\leq x_{0}
:

(\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa}\widehat{\psi})_{\ell}=\partial_{x}(x(\kappa+x)\partial_{x}\widehat{\psi}_{\ell})+2s\partial_{x}\widehat{\psi}_{\ell}-\mathfrak{l}(\mathfrak{l}+1)\widehat{\psi}_{\ell}.

Hence,
all the estimates in this section are estimates for the rescaled Laplace-transformed wave operator in Minkowski
, restricted to fixed spherical harmonic modes with angular frequencies
\geq\mathfrak{l}
. We will moreover require these estimates to be uniform in
\mathfrak{l}
.

Denote
\widehat{\psi}^{(n)}=\partial_{x}^{n}\widehat{\psi}
. Then it can be easily verified that:

(13.1)

(\partial_{x}^{n}\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa}\widehat{\psi})_{\ell}=x(\kappa+x)\partial_{x}\widehat{\psi}^{(n+1)}_{\ell}+2(n+1)\left(x+\frac{\kappa}{2}\right)\partial_{x}\widehat{\psi}^{(n)}+2s\partial_{x}\widehat{\psi}^{(n)}_{\ell}+(n(n+1)-\mathfrak{l}(\mathfrak{l}+1))\widehat{\psi}_{\ell}^{(n)}.

We will estimate higher-order energy norms of
\widehat{\psi}
in terms of similar energy norms of
\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa}\widehat{\psi}
in two steps.

Step 1:

We consider
\widehat{\psi}^{(n)}_{\ell}=\partial_{x}^{n}\widehat{\psi}_{\ell}
and derive
\dot{H}^{1}
estimates for
\widehat{\psi}^{(n)}_{\ell}
with
n\geq\mathfrak{l}
. We then sum the estimates for different values of
n
to obtain an
L^{2}
-based Gevrey-type estimate.
Here it is important that we will not see any terms
\widehat{\psi}^{(n)}_{\ell}
with
n<\mathfrak{l}
.

Step 2:

We estimate weighted
\dot{H}^{1}
norms of
\widehat{\psi}^{(n)}_{\ell}
with
n<\mathfrak{l}
in terms of a weighted
\dot{H}^{1}
norm of
\widehat{\psi}^{(n)}_{\ell}
. To be able to sum these lower-order estimates, we apply elliptic-type estimates in the region
x\leq x_{0}
.

13.1.
Step 1: higher-order derivatives

We will first derive weighted
L^{2}
estimates for
\widehat{\psi}^{(n)}
with
n\geq\mathfrak{l}
. We obtain these estimates by simply rearranging the terms in (
13.1
) to obtain:

(13.2)

x(\kappa+x)\partial_{x}\widehat{\psi}^{(n+1)}_{\ell}+2(n+1)\left(x+\frac{\kappa}{2}\right)\partial_{x}\widehat{\psi}_{\ell}^{(n)}+2s\partial_{x}\widehat{\psi}^{(n)}_{\ell}=(\mathfrak{l}(\mathfrak{l}+1)-n(n+1))\widehat{\psi}_{\ell}^{(n)}+(\partial_{x}^{n}\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa}\widehat{\psi})_{\ell}.

and by taking the square norm on both sides, treating the right-hand side as an inhomogeneous term. By considering appropriate weighted sums in
n
of these
L^{2}
estimates, we will then be able to absorb the terms arising from
(\mathfrak{l}(\mathfrak{l}+1)-n(n+1))\widehat{\psi}_{\ell}^{(n)}
on the right-hand side of (
13.2
).

Proposition 13.1
.

Let
\kappa\geq 0
,
M=1
,
\ell,l\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,
N_{\infty}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, with
N_{\infty}>\mathfrak{l}
, and let
\alpha,\beta,\nu,\tilde{\sigma},\epsilon>0
, with
\mu\in(0,1)
. Then there exists a constant
C>0
such that:

(13.3)

\sum_{n=\mathfrak{l}}^{N_{\infty}}\frac{|2s|^{2}\tilde{\sigma}^{2n}}{n!^{2}(n+1)!^{2}}\int_{0}^{x_{0}}(1-\mu\nu^{-1})x^{2}(x+\kappa)^{2}|\hat{\psi}_{\ell}^{(n+2)}|^{2}\,dx\\
+\sum_{n=\mathfrak{l}}^{N_{\infty}}\frac{|2s|^{2}\tilde{\sigma}^{2n}}{n!^{2}(n+1)!^{2}}\int_{0}^{x_{0}}\left[4-\beta-(1-\mu)(\alpha+\tilde{\sigma}^{-2}\alpha^{-1})-6(n+1)^{-1}\right]\\
\times(n+1)^{2}\left(x+\frac{1}{2}\kappa\right)^{2}|\hat{\psi}_{\ell}^{(n+1)}|^{2}\,dx\\
+\sum_{n=\mathfrak{l}}^{N_{\infty}}\frac{|2s|^{2}\tilde{\sigma}^{2n}}{n!^{2}(n+1)!^{2}}\int_{0}^{x_{0}}\left(1-\mu\nu-4\beta^{-1}\left(\frac{\max\{-\re s,0\}}{|s|}\right)^{2}-(1+\epsilon)\tilde{\sigma}^{2}\left(1+2(n-1)^{-1}\right)^{2}\right)\\
\times|2s|^{2}|\hat{\psi}_{\ell}^{(n+1)}|^{2}\,dx\\
\boxed{-\frac{|2s|^{2(N_{\infty}+1)}\tilde{\sigma}^{2(N_{\infty}+1)}}{(N_{\infty}+1)!^{2}(N_{\infty}+2)!^{2}}\int_{0}^{x_{0}}(1-\mu)\alpha^{-1}\tilde{\sigma}^{-2}(N_{\infty}+2)^{2}\left(x+\frac{1}{x^{-1}+2\kappa^{-1}}\right)^{2}|\hat{\psi}_{\ell}^{(N_{\infty}+2)}|^{2}\,dx}\\
+\sum_{n=\mathfrak{l}}^{N_{\infty}}\frac{|2s|^{2}\tilde{\sigma}^{2n}}{n!^{2}(n+1)!^{2}}x_{0}^{3}(n+1)|\widehat{\psi}^{(n+1)}|^{2}\big|_{x=x_{0}}\\
\leq C(1+\epsilon^{-1})\sum_{n=\mathfrak{l}}^{N_{\infty}}\frac{|2s|^{2}\tilde{\sigma}^{2n}}{n!^{2}(n+1)!^{2}}\int_{0}^{x_{0}}|\partial_{x}^{n}(\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa}\widehat{\psi})_{\ell}|^{2}\,dx.

Proof.

We will omit the subscript
\ell
in
\widehat{\psi}_{\ell}
for the sake of convenience. Rearranging (
13.1
) and taking the square norm on both sides, we obtain:

x^{2}(x+\kappa)^{2}|\hat{\psi}^{(n+2)}|^{2}+4(n+1)^{2}\left(x+\frac{1}{2}\kappa\right)^{2}|\hat{\psi}^{(n+1)}|^{2}+4|s|^{2}|\hat{\psi}^{(n+1)}|^{2}\\
+T_{1}+T_{2}+T_{3}=\left|[\mathfrak{l}(\mathfrak{l}+1)-n(n+1)]\hat{\psi}^{(n)}+\partial_{x}^{n}(\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa}\widehat{\psi})_{\ell}\right|^{2},

with

\displaystyle T_{1}:=

\displaystyle\>2(n+1)x\left(x+\frac{1}{2}\kappa\right)(x+\kappa)\partial_{x}(|\hat{\psi}^{(n+1)}|^{2}),

\displaystyle T_{2}:=

\displaystyle\>4\re(2s)(n+1)\left(x+\frac{1}{2}\kappa\right)|\hat{\psi}^{(n+1)}|^{2},

\displaystyle T_{3}:=

\displaystyle\>2x(x+\kappa)\re\left(\hat{\psi}^{(n+2)}2\overline{s}\overline{\hat{\psi}^{(n+1)}}\right).

We integrate
T_{1}
by parts to obtain:

\int_{0}^{x_{0}}T_{1}\,dx\geq 2(n+1)x_{0}\left(x_{0}+\frac{1}{2}\kappa\right)(x_{0}+\kappa)|\hat{\psi}^{(n+1)}|^{2}|_{x=x_{0}}\\
-\int_{0}^{x_{0}}(n+1)(6x^{2}+6\kappa x+\kappa^{2})|\hat{\psi}^{(n+1)}|^{2}\,dx\\
\geq 2(n+1)x_{0}^{3}|\hat{\psi}^{(n+1)}|^{2}|_{x=x_{0}}-\int_{0}^{x_{0}}6(n+1)\left(x+\frac{1}{2}\kappa\right)^{2}|\hat{\psi}^{(n+1)}|^{2}\,dx.

If
{\re s}\geq 0
, then
T_{2}\geq 0
. If
{\re s}<0
, then we introduce a constant
\beta>0
and apply Young’s inequality to split:

|T_{2}|\leq\beta(n+1)^{2}\left(x+\frac{1}{2}\kappa\right)^{2}|\hat{\psi}^{(n+1)}|^{2}+4\beta^{-1}\left(\frac{|\re s|}{|s|}\right)^{2}|2s|^{2}|\hat{\psi}^{(n+1)}|^{2}.

We introduce additionally
0\leq\mu\leq 1
and split

\begin{split}|T_{3}|\leq 2x(x+\kappa)|2s||\hat{\psi}^{(n+1)}\|\hat{\psi}^{(n+2)}|=&\>\mu\cdot 2x(x+\kappa)|2s||\hat{\psi}^{(n+1)}\|\hat{\psi}^{(n+2)}|\\
&+(1-\mu)\cdot 2x(x+\kappa)|2s||\hat{\psi}^{(n+1)}\|\hat{\psi}^{(n+2)}|.\end{split}

We further estimate via Young’s inequality with an additional weight
\nu>0
:

\mu\cdot 2x(x+\kappa)|2s||\hat{\psi}^{(n+1)}\|\hat{\psi}^{(n+2)}|\leq\mu\nu|2s|^{2}|\hat{\psi}^{(n+1)}|^{2}+\mu\nu^{-1}x^{2}(x+\kappa)^{2}|\hat{\psi}^{(n+2)}|^{2},

and we estimate the remaining term with a slightly different Young’s inequality, involving the weight
\alpha
and an additional factor
\frac{|2s|^{2}\tilde{\sigma}^{2}}{(n+1)^{2}(n+2)^{2}}
that appears in the summation below:

\begin{split}(1-\mu)\cdot&2x(x+\kappa)|2s\|\hat{\psi}^{(n+1)}\|\hat{\psi}^{(n+2)}|\leq\alpha(1-\mu)(n+1)^{2}\left(x+\frac{1}{2}\kappa\right)^{2}|\hat{\psi}^{(n+1)}|^{2}\\
&+(1-\mu)\alpha^{-1}\tilde{\sigma}^{-2}\cdot\frac{|2s|^{2}\tilde{\sigma}^{2}}{(n+1)^{2}(n+2)^{2}}(n+1+1)^{2}\frac{x^{2}(x+\kappa)^{2}}{\left(x+\frac{1}{2}\kappa\right)^{2}}|\hat{\psi}^{(n+1+1)}|^{2}.\end{split}

We moreover have that
\frac{x^{2}(x+\kappa)^{2}}{\left(x+\frac{1}{2}\kappa\right)^{2}}=(x+\frac{1}{x^{-1}+2\kappa^{-1}})^{2}\leq(x+\frac{1}{2}\kappa)^{2}

Finally, we estimate

|[\mathfrak{l}(\mathfrak{l}+1)-n(n+1)]\hat{\psi}^{(n)}+\partial_{x}^{n}((\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa}\widehat{\psi})_{\ell})|^{2}\leq(1+\epsilon)(n(n+1)-\mathfrak{l}(\mathfrak{l}+1))^{2}|\hat{\psi}^{(n+1-1)}|^{2}\\
+(1+\epsilon^{-1})|(\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa}\widehat{\psi})_{\ell}|^{2}.

Now we integrate the above estimates over the interval
[0,x_{0}]
to obtain:

\int_{0}^{x_{0}}(1-\mu\nu^{-1})x^{2}(x+\kappa)^{2}|\hat{\psi}^{(n+2)}|^{2}\,dx+2(n+1)x_{0}^{3}|\hat{\psi}^{(n+1)}|^{2}|_{x=x_{0}}\\
+\int_{0}^{x_{0}}\left[4-\beta-\alpha(1-\mu)-6(n+1)^{-1}\right](n+1)^{2}\left(x+\frac{1}{2}\kappa\right)^{2}|\hat{\psi}^{(n+1)}|^{2}\,dx\\
\boxed{-\int_{0}^{x_{0}}(1-\mu)\alpha^{-1}\tilde{\sigma}^{-2}\cdot\frac{|2s|^{2}\tilde{\sigma}^{2}}{(n+1)^{2}(n+2)^{2}}(n+1+1)^{2}\left(x+\frac{1}{x^{-1}+2\kappa^{-1}}\right)^{2}|\hat{\psi}^{(n+1+1)}|^{2}\,dx}\\
+\int_{0}^{x_{0}}\left(1-\mu\nu-4\beta^{-1}\left(\frac{\max\{-\re s,0\}}{|s|}\right)^{2}\right)|2s|^{2}|\hat{\psi}^{(n+1)}|^{2}\,dx\\
\boxed{-\int_{0}^{x_{0}}(1+\epsilon)|2s|^{2}\tilde{\sigma}^{2}\left(\frac{n(n+1)-\mathfrak{l}(\mathfrak{l}+1)}{n(n-1)}\right)^{2}\frac{n^{2}(n-1)^{2}}{|2s|^{2}\tilde{\sigma}^{2}}|\hat{\psi}^{(n+1-1)}|^{2}\,dx}\\
\leq(1+\epsilon^{-1})\int_{0}^{x_{0}}|\partial_{x}^{n}(\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa}\widehat{\psi})_{\ell}|^{2}\,dx.

We can sum the above estimate over
n
with the following weights:

\sum_{n=\mathfrak{l}}^{N_{\infty}}\frac{|2s|^{2n}\tilde{\sigma}^{2n}}{n!^{2}(n+1)!^{2}}\left[\cdot\right],

and absorbing all but the top order term with a bad sign into the terms with good signs, we obtain (
13.3
).
∎

In order for the unboxed terms on the left-hand side of (
13.3
) to be non-negative definite, the coefficients
\tilde{\sigma},\alpha,\beta,\mu,\nu
need to satisfy the following compatibility conditions: when
{\re s}<0

(13.4)

\displaystyle 0<\mu<

\displaystyle\>\nu,

(13.5)

\displaystyle 4-\beta-(1-\mu)(\alpha+\tilde{\sigma}^{-2}\alpha^{-1})>

\displaystyle\>0,

(13.6)

\displaystyle 1-\mu\nu-4\beta^{-1}\left(\frac{|{\re s}|}{|s|}\right)^{2}-\tilde{\sigma}^{2}>

\displaystyle\>0.

For
{\re s}\geq 0
, we can take
\mu=0
,
\beta=1
and
\alpha=2
, so we instead need

(13.7)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{4}<\tilde{\sigma}^{2}<1.

Indeed, if (
13.4
)–(
13.7
) are satisfied, then we can take
\lambda
sufficiently large and
\epsilon
sufficiently small, to guarantee non-negative definiteness of the unboxed terms on the left-hand side of (
13.3
).

Lemma 13.2
.

There exist
\tilde{\sigma}
,
\alpha,\beta,\mu,\nu
satisfying (
13.4
)–(
13.7
) if and only if:

(i)

-{\re s}\leq\frac{1}{2}|s|
,

(ii)

For fixed
2|s|\tilde{\sigma}=\sigma
, with
\sigma\in\mathbb{R}
, we have
s\in\Omega_{\sigma}
, where

\Omega_{\sigma}:=\left\{x+iy\in\mathbb{C}\,|\,x\in\mathbb{R}_{-},\,y\in\mathbb{R},\,x^{2}+y^{2}<\sigma^{2},\,3y^{2}-5x^{2}>\sigma^{2}\right\}\\
\cup\left\{x+iy\in\mathbb{C}\,|\,x\in[0,\infty),\,\frac{\sigma^{2}}{4}<x^{2}+y^{2}<\sigma^{2}\right\}.

(iii)

For
s\in\{-\frac{1}{2}|s|\leq\re s<0\}
.

\frac{1}{4}<\tilde{\sigma}^{2}<\frac{3}{4}-2\left(\frac{\re s}{|s|}\right)^{2}.

Furthermore,

\Omega:=\bigcup_{\sigma\in\mathbb{R}}\Omega_{\sigma}=\left\{z\in\mathbb{C}\,,\,|\arg(z)|<\frac{2\pi}{3}\right\}.

Proof.

Appendix
A
.
∎

By combining Proposition
13.1
with Lemma
13.2
, we immediately obtain:

Corollary 13.3
.

Let
\kappa\geq 0
,
M=1
,
\ell,l\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,
N_{\infty}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, with
N_{\infty}>\mathfrak{l}
. Let
\sigma\in\mathbb{R}
and let
s\in\Omega_{\sigma}
. Then, there exists a constant
C_{s,\sigma}=C(s,\sigma)>0
, such that:

(13.8)

\sum_{n=\mathfrak{l}}^{N_{\infty}}\frac{\sigma^{2n}}{n!^{2}(n+1)!^{2}}\int_{0}^{x_{0}}x^{2}(x+\kappa)^{2}|\hat{\psi}_{\ell}^{(n+2)}|^{2}\,dx+\sum_{n=\mathfrak{l}}^{N_{\infty}}\frac{\sigma^{2n}}{n!^{2}(n+1)!^{2}}(n+1)x_{0}^{3}|\widehat{\psi}^{(n+1)}|^{2}\big|_{x=x_{0}}\\
+\sum_{n=\mathfrak{l}}^{N_{\infty}}\frac{\sigma^{2n}}{n!^{2}(n+1)!^{2}}\int_{0}^{x_{0}}\left[|2s|^{2}+(n+1)^{2}\left(x+\frac{1}{2}\kappa\right)^{2}\right]|\hat{\psi}_{\ell}^{(n+1)}|^{2}\,dx\\
\boxed{-C_{s,\sigma}\frac{|2s|^{2(N_{\infty}+1)}\tilde{\sigma}^{2(N_{\infty}+1)}}{(N_{\infty}+1)!^{2}(N_{\infty}+2)!^{2}}\int_{0}^{x_{0}}(N_{\infty}+2)^{2}\left(x+\frac{1}{x^{-1}+2\kappa^{-1}}\right)^{2}|\hat{\psi}_{\ell}^{(N_{\infty}+2)}|^{2}\,dx}\\
\leq C_{s,\sigma}\sum_{n=\mathfrak{l}}^{N_{\infty}}\frac{\sigma^{2n}}{n!^{2}(n+1)!^{2}}\int_{0}^{x_{0}}|\partial_{x}^{n}(\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa}\widehat{\psi})_{\ell}|^{2}\,dx.

We now restrict to the case
\kappa>0
to absorb the boxed boundary term in (
13.9
) and arrive at a closed, uniform estimate in
\kappa
:

Corollary 13.4
.

Let
\kappa>0
,
M=1
,
\ell,l\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,
N_{\infty}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, with
N_{\infty}>\mathfrak{l}
. Let
\sigma\in\mathbb{R}
and let
s\in\Omega_{\sigma}
. Then there exists a constant
C_{s,\sigma}=C(s,\sigma)>0
and a
N_{\kappa}>\mathfrak{l}
, such that for all
N_{\infty}\geq N_{\kappa}
:

(13.9)

\sum_{n=\mathfrak{l}}^{N_{\infty}}\frac{\sigma^{2n}}{n!^{2}(n+1)!^{2}}\int_{0}^{x_{0}}x^{2}(x+\kappa)^{2}|\hat{\psi}_{\ell}^{(n+2)}|^{2}\,dx+\sum_{n=\mathfrak{l}}^{N_{\infty}}\frac{\sigma^{2n}}{n!^{2}(n+1)!^{2}}(n+1)x_{0}^{3}|\widehat{\psi}^{(n+1)}|^{2}\big|_{x=x_{0}}\\
+\sum_{n=\mathfrak{l}}^{N_{\infty}}\frac{\sigma^{2n}}{n!^{2}(n+1)!^{2}}\int_{0}^{x_{0}}\left[|2s|^{2}+(n+1)^{2}\left(x+\frac{1}{2}\kappa\right)^{2}\right]|\hat{\psi}_{\ell}^{(n+1)}|^{2}\,dx\\
\leq C_{s,\sigma}\sum_{n=\mathfrak{l}}^{N_{\infty}}\frac{\sigma^{2n}}{n!^{2}(n+1)!^{2}}\int_{0}^{x_{0}}|\partial_{x}^{n}(\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa}\widehat{\psi})_{\ell}|^{2}\,dx.

Proof.

Note that

C_{s,\sigma}\frac{|2s|^{2}\tilde{\sigma}^{2}}{(N_{\infty}+1)^{2}(N_{\infty}+2)^{2}}(N_{\infty}+2)^{2}\left(x+\frac{1}{x^{-1}+2\kappa^{-1}}\right)^{2}<4\frac{C_{s,\sigma}|2s|^{2}\tilde{\sigma}^{2}\kappa^{-2}}{(N_{\infty}+1)^{2}}\kappa^{2}x^{2},

so, for
N_{\infty}+1>4\sqrt{C_{s,\sigma}}|2s\|\tilde{\sigma}|\kappa^{-1}=:N_{\kappa}+1
, we can absorb the boxed term in (
13.9
) into the first term on the right-hand side of (
13.9
).
∎

Remark 13.1
.

Note that the estimate (
13.9
) is
closed
, i.e. there are only terms involving
(\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa}\widehat{\psi})_{\ell}
on the right-hand side. This means that we can control weighted
L^{2}
-norms of
\widehat{\psi}^{(n)}_{\ell}
with
n\geq\mathfrak{l}+1
in
x\leq x_{0}

independently
from
\widehat{\psi}^{(n)}_{\ell}
with
n\leq\mathfrak{l}
.

13.2.
Step 2: lower-order derivatives

In this section, we will establish estimates for appropriately weighted
\dot{H}^{1}
estimates in the region
\{0<x\leq x_{0}\}
for
\widehat{\psi}^{(n)}
with
n\leq\mathfrak{l}
in terms of a
\dot{H^{1}}
norm for
\widehat{\psi}^{(\mathfrak{l}+1)}
and lower-order boundary terms.

The estimates in this section rely on a different rearranging of (
13.1
), compared to Step 1:

x(\kappa+x)\partial_{x}\widehat{\psi}^{(n+1)}_{\ell}+2(n+1)\left(x+\frac{\kappa}{2}\right)\partial_{x}\widehat{\psi}_{\ell}^{(n)}-(\mathfrak{l}(\mathfrak{l}+1)-n(n+1))\widehat{\psi}_{\ell}^{(n)}=-2s\partial_{x}\widehat{\psi}^{(n)}_{\ell}+(\partial_{x}^{n}\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa}\widehat{\psi})_{\ell}.

The main estimates are obtained by considering the square norm of both sides of the equation and summing over
n
with a appropriately weighted sum.

Proposition 13.5
.

Let
\kappa\leq x_{0}
and
\gamma>1
. Then there exists a constant
C=C(\gamma)>0
such that

(13.10)

\sum_{n=0}^{\mathfrak{l}-1}\frac{\gamma^{2n}|2s|^{2n}(\mathfrak{l}-(n+1))!^{2}}{(\mathfrak{l}+n+1)!^{2}}\int_{0}^{x_{0}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(x^{4}+\kappa^{2}x^{2})|\hat{\psi}^{(n+2)}|^{2}+(x^{2}(\mathfrak{l}+1)^{2}+\kappa^{2}n(n+1)+|2s|^{2})|\hat{\psi}^{(n+1)}|^{2}\\
+(\mathfrak{l}-n)^{2}(\mathfrak{l}+n+1)^{2}|\hat{\psi}^{(n)}|^{2}\,d\upsigma dx\\
\leq C\frac{|2s|^{2(\mathfrak{l}-1)}\gamma^{2(\mathfrak{l}-1)}}{(2\mathfrak{l}!)^{2}}\int_{0}^{x_{0}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}|\hat{\psi}^{(\mathfrak{l}+1)}|^{2}\,dx+C\frac{|2s|^{2(\mathfrak{l}-1)}\gamma^{2(\mathfrak{l}-1)}}{(2\mathfrak{l}!)^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}|\hat{\psi}^{(\mathfrak{l})}|^{2}|_{x=x_{0}}\,d\upsigma\\
+C\sum_{n=0}^{\mathfrak{l}-1}\frac{\gamma^{2n}|2s|^{2n}(\mathfrak{l}-(n+1))!^{2}}{(\mathfrak{l}+n+1)!^{2}}(\mathfrak{l}-n)(\mathfrak{l}+n+1)(\mathfrak{l}+1)^{2}x_{0}^{-1}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}|\widehat{\psi}^{(n)}|^{2}|_{x=x_{0}}d\upsigma\\
+C\sum_{n=0}^{\mathfrak{l}-1}\frac{\gamma^{2n}|2s|^{2n}(\mathfrak{l}-(n+1))!^{2}}{(\mathfrak{l}+n+1)!^{2}}\int_{0}^{x_{0}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}|\partial_{x}^{n}\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa}\widehat{\psi}|^{2}\,d\upsigma dx.

Furthermore, there exists a constant
C=C(\gamma,|s|)>0
such that

(13.11)

(\mathfrak{l}+1)^{-(4N_{+}+1)}\sum_{n=0}^{N_{+}+1}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}x|\hat{\psi}^{(n)}|^{2}|_{x=\eta x_{0}}d\upsigma\leq C\frac{|2s|^{2(\mathfrak{l}-1)}\gamma^{2(\mathfrak{l}-1)}}{(2\mathfrak{l}!)^{2}}\int_{0}^{x_{0}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}|\hat{\psi}^{(\mathfrak{l}+1)}|^{2}\,dx\\
+C\frac{|2s|^{2(\mathfrak{l}-1)}\gamma^{2(\mathfrak{l}-1)}}{(2\mathfrak{l}!)^{2}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}|\hat{\psi}^{(\mathfrak{l})}|^{2}|_{x=x_{0}}\,d\upsigma\\
+C\sum_{n=0}^{\mathfrak{l}-1}\frac{\gamma^{2n}|2s|^{2n}(\mathfrak{l}-(n+1))!^{2}}{(\mathfrak{l}+n+1)!^{2}}(\mathfrak{l}-n)(\mathfrak{l}+n+1)(\mathfrak{l}+1)^{2}x_{0}^{-1}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}|\widehat{\psi}^{(n)}|^{2}|_{x=x_{0}}d\upsigma\\
+C\sum_{n=0}^{\mathfrak{l}-1}\frac{\gamma^{2n}|2s|^{2n}(\mathfrak{l}-(n+1))!^{2}}{(\mathfrak{l}+n+1)!^{2}}\int_{0}^{x_{0}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}|\partial_{x}^{n}\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa}\widehat{\psi}|^{2}\,d\upsigma dx.

Proof.

Consider (
13.1
), suppress the
\ell
subscript and rearrange the terms to obtain:

(13.12)

|(\partial_{x}^{n}\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa}\widehat{\psi})-2s\widehat{\psi}^{(n+1)}|^{2}=|x(\kappa+x)\partial_{x}^{2}\widehat{\psi}^{(n)}+2(n+1)\left(x+\frac{\kappa}{2}\right)\partial_{x}\widehat{\psi}^{(n)}-(\mathfrak{l}(\mathfrak{l}+1)-n(n+1))\widehat{\psi}^{(n)}|^{2}\\
=x^{2}(x+\kappa)^{2}|\hat{\psi}^{(n+2)}|^{2}+4(n+1)^{2}\left(x+\frac{1}{2}\kappa\right)^{2}|\hat{\psi}^{(n+1)}|^{2}+(\mathfrak{l}(\mathfrak{l}+1)-n(n+1))^{2}|\hat{\psi}^{(n)}|^{2}\\
+T_{1}+T_{2}+T_{3},

with

\displaystyle T_{1}:=

\displaystyle\>2(n+1)x\left(x+\frac{1}{2}\kappa\right)(x+\kappa)\partial_{x}(|\hat{\psi}^{(n+1)}|^{2}),

\displaystyle T_{2}:=

\displaystyle\>-2(\mathfrak{l}(\mathfrak{l}+1)-n(n+1))x(\kappa+x)\re(\partial_{x}^{2}\widehat{\psi}^{(n)}\cdot\overline{\widehat{\psi}^{(n)}}),

\displaystyle T_{3}:=

\displaystyle\>-2(n+1)(\mathfrak{l}(\mathfrak{l}+1)-n(n+1))\left(x+\frac{\kappa}{2}\right)\partial_{x}(|\widehat{\psi}^{(n)}|^{2}).

We now integrate both sides of (
13.12
) over
[0,x_{0}]\times\mathbb{S}^{2}
, but we suppress the integration over
\mathbb{S}^{2}
in the notation.

We first estimate the integral of
T_{1}
by integrating by parts:

\int_{0}^{x_{0}}T_{1}\,dx=-\int_{0}^{x_{0}}(n+1)(6x^{2}+6\kappa x+\kappa^{2})|\widehat{\psi}^{(n+1)}|^{2}\,dx\\
+2(n+1)x_{0}\left(x_{0}+\frac{1}{2}\kappa\right)(x_{0}+\kappa)|\hat{\psi}^{(n+1)}|^{2}|_{x=x_{0}}\\
\geq-\int_{0}^{x_{0}}6(n+1)(x(x+\kappa)+\frac{1}{6}\kappa^{2})|\hat{\psi}^{(n+1)}|^{2}\,dx+2(n+1)x_{0}\left(x_{0}+\frac{1}{2}\kappa\right)(x_{0}+\kappa)|\hat{\psi}^{(n+1)}|^{2}|_{x=x_{0}}

To estimate
T_{2}
, we integrate by parts twice:

\int_{0}^{x_{0}}T_{2}\,dx=\int_{0}^{x_{0}}2(\mathfrak{l}(\mathfrak{l}+1)-n(n+1))x(\kappa+x)|\widehat{\psi}^{(n+1)}|^{2}\,dx-\int_{0}^{x_{0}}(\mathfrak{l}(\mathfrak{l}+1)-n(n+1))(\kappa+2x)\partial_{x}(|\widehat{\psi}^{(n)}|^{2})\,dx\\
-2(\mathfrak{l}(\mathfrak{l}+1)-n(n+1))x_{0}(\kappa+x_{0})\re(\widehat{\psi}^{(n+1)}\cdot\overline{\widehat{\psi}^{(n)}})|_{x=x_{0}}\\
\geq\int_{0}^{x_{0}}2(\mathfrak{l}(\mathfrak{l}+1)-n(n+1))x(\kappa+x)|\widehat{\psi}^{(n+1)}|^{2}\,dx+2\int_{0}^{x_{0}}(\mathfrak{l}(\mathfrak{l}+1)-n(n+1))|\widehat{\psi}^{(n)}|^{2}\,dx\\
-2(\mathfrak{l}(\mathfrak{l}+1)-n(n+1))x_{0}(\kappa+x_{0})\re(\widehat{\psi}^{(n+1)}\cdot\overline{\widehat{\psi}^{(n)}})|_{x=x_{0}}-(\mathfrak{l}(\mathfrak{l}+1)-n(n+1))(\kappa+2x_{0})|\widehat{\psi}^{(n)}|^{2}|_{x=x_{0}}

We also integrate
T_{3}
by parts to obtain:

\int_{0}^{x_{0}}T_{3}\,dx\geq\int_{0}^{x_{0}}2(n+1)(\mathfrak{l}(\mathfrak{l}+1)-n(n+1))|\widehat{\psi}^{(n)}|^{2}\,dx-2(n+1)(\mathfrak{l}(\mathfrak{l}+1)-n(n+1))\left(x_{0}+\frac{\kappa}{2}\right)|\widehat{\psi}^{(n)}|^{2}|_{x=x_{0}}.

Finally, we apply Young’s inequality to estimate for
\epsilon>0
:

|(\partial_{x}^{n}\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa}\widehat{\psi})-2s\widehat{\psi}^{(n+1)}|^{2}\leq(1+\epsilon)|2s|^{2}|\widehat{\psi}^{(n+1)}|^{2}+(1+\epsilon^{-1})|\partial_{x}^{n}\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa}\widehat{\psi}|^{2}.

We combine the above estimates to obtain for
\gamma>1
:

\int_{0}^{x_{0}}x^{2}(x+\kappa)^{2}|\hat{\psi}^{(n+2)}|^{2}\,dx\\
+\int_{0}^{x_{0}}\left[(2(n+1)(n-1)+2\mathfrak{l}(\mathfrak{l}+1))x(\kappa+x)+(n+1)^{2}\kappa^{2}\right]|\hat{\psi}^{(n+1)}|^{2}\,dx\\
+\int_{0}^{x_{0}}(\mathfrak{l}(\mathfrak{l}+1)-n(n+1))(\mathfrak{l}(\mathfrak{l}+1)-n(n+1)+2(n+2))|\hat{\psi}^{(n)}|^{2}\,dx\\
\boxed{-\int_{0}^{x_{0}}\frac{|2s|^{2}\gamma^{2}}{(\mathfrak{l}(\mathfrak{l}+1)-(n+1)(n+2))^{2}}\gamma^{-2}(1+\epsilon)(\mathfrak{l}(\mathfrak{l}+1)-(n+1)(n+2))^{2}|\hat{\psi}^{(n+1)}|^{2}\,dx}\\
\leq C(\mathfrak{l}(\mathfrak{l}+1)-n(n+1))(\mathfrak{l}+1)^{2}x_{0}^{-1}|\widehat{\psi}^{(n)}|^{2}|_{x=x_{0}}+(1+\epsilon^{-1})\int_{0}^{x_{0}}|\partial_{x}^{n}\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa}\widehat{\psi}|^{2}\,dx.

In order to absorb the boxed term above, we will sum over
n
with the following weights:

\sum_{n=0}^{\mathfrak{l}-1}\frac{\gamma^{2n}|2s|^{2n}}{(\mathfrak{l}(\mathfrak{l}+1))^{2}(\mathfrak{l}(\mathfrak{l}+1)-1(1+1))^{2}\ldots(\mathfrak{l}(\mathfrak{l}+1)-n(n+1))^{2}}.

Using that
\mathfrak{l}(\mathfrak{l}+1)-n(n+1)=(\mathfrak{l}-n)(\mathfrak{l}+n+1)
. We can simplify for
n\leq\mathfrak{l}-1
:

(\mathfrak{l}(\mathfrak{l}+1))^{2}(\mathfrak{l}(\mathfrak{l}+1)-1(1+1))^{2}\ldots(\mathfrak{l}(\mathfrak{l}+1)-n(n+1))^{2}=\frac{(\mathfrak{l}+n+1)!^{2}}{(\mathfrak{l}-(n+1))!^{2}},

so that the summation becomes

\sum_{n=0}^{\mathfrak{l}-1}\frac{\gamma^{2n}|2s|^{2n}(\mathfrak{l}-(n+1))!^{2}}{(\mathfrak{l}+n+1)!^{2}}.

We obtain:

\sum_{n=0}^{\mathfrak{l}-1}\frac{\gamma^{2n}|2s|^{2n}(\mathfrak{l}-(n+1))!^{2}}{(\mathfrak{l}+n+1)!^{2}}\int_{0}^{x_{0}}x^{4}|\hat{\psi}^{(n+2)}|^{2}\,dx\\
+\int_{0}^{x_{0}}\left[((n+1)^{2}+\mathfrak{l}(\mathfrak{l}+1))x^{2}+|2s|^{2}\right]|\hat{\psi}^{(n+1)}|^{2}\,dx\\
+\int_{0}^{x_{0}}(1-(1+\epsilon)\gamma^{-2})(\mathfrak{l}(\mathfrak{l}+1)-n(n+1))^{2}|\hat{\psi}^{(n)}|^{2}\,dx\\
\leq C\frac{|2s|^{2(\mathfrak{l}-1)}\gamma^{2(\mathfrak{l}-1)}}{(2\mathfrak{l}!)^{2}}\int_{0}^{x_{0}}|\hat{\psi}^{(\mathfrak{l})}|^{2}\,dx\\
+C\sum_{n=0}^{\mathfrak{l}-1}\frac{\gamma^{2n}|2s|^{2n}(\mathfrak{l}-(n+1))!^{2}}{(\mathfrak{l}+n+1)!^{2}}(\mathfrak{l}-n)(\mathfrak{l}+n+1)(\mathfrak{l}+1)^{2}x_{0}^{-1}|\widehat{\psi}^{(n)}|^{2}|_{x=x_{0}}\\
+C(1+\epsilon^{-1})\sum_{n=0}^{\mathfrak{l}-1}\int_{0}^{x_{0}}\frac{\gamma^{2n}|2s|^{2n}(\mathfrak{l}-(n+1))!^{2}}{(\mathfrak{l}+n+1)!^{2}}|\partial_{x}^{n}\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa}\widehat{\psi}|^{2}\,dx.

By applying a Hardy inequality we can further estimate:

\frac{|2s|^{2(\mathfrak{l}-1)}\gamma^{2(\mathfrak{l}-1)}}{(2\mathfrak{l}!)^{2}}\int_{0}^{x_{0}}|\hat{\psi}^{(\mathfrak{l})}|^{2}\,dx\leq 4x_{0}^{2}\frac{|2s|^{2(\mathfrak{l}-1)}\gamma^{2(\mathfrak{l}-1)}}{(2\mathfrak{l}!)^{2}}\int_{0}^{x_{0}}|\hat{\psi}^{(\mathfrak{l}+1)}|^{2}\,dx+2\frac{|2s|^{2(\mathfrak{l}-1)}\gamma^{2(\mathfrak{l}-1)}}{(2\mathfrak{l}!)^{2}}x_{0}|\widehat{\psi}^{(\mathfrak{l})}|^{2}|_{x=x_{0}}.

We conclude (
13.10
).

By integrating by parts
T_{3}
in the region
[\eta x_{0},x_{0}]\times\mathbb{S}^{2}
in the argument above and using the integral estimates above, we can moreover estimate boundary terms at
x=\eta x_{0}
. We obtain in particular also:

(\mathfrak{l}+1)^{-4(N_{+}+1)}\sum_{n=0}^{N_{+}+1}x|\hat{\psi}^{(n)}|^{2}|_{x=\eta x_{0}}\leq C\frac{|2s|^{2(\mathfrak{l}-1)}\gamma^{2(\mathfrak{l}-1)}}{(2\mathfrak{l}!)^{2}}\int_{0}^{x_{0}}|\hat{\psi}^{(\mathfrak{l})}|^{2}\,dx\\
+C\sum_{n=0}^{\mathfrak{l}-1}\frac{\gamma^{2n}|2s|^{2n}(\mathfrak{l}-(n+1))!^{2}}{(\mathfrak{l}+n+1)!^{2}}(\mathfrak{l}-n)(\mathfrak{l}+n+1)(\mathfrak{l}+1)^{2}x_{0}^{-1}|\widehat{\psi}^{(n)}|^{2}|_{x=x_{0}}\\
+C(1+\epsilon^{-1})\sum_{n=0}^{\mathfrak{l}-1}\int_{0}^{x_{0}}\frac{\gamma^{2n}|2s|^{2n}(\mathfrak{l}-(n+1))!^{2}}{(\mathfrak{l}+n+1)!^{2}}|\partial_{x}^{n}\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa}\widehat{\psi}|^{2}\,dx.

∎

We now estimate the boundary terms at
x=x_{0}
that appear on the right-hand sides of the estimates in Proposition
13.5
.

Lemma 13.6
.

Let
\kappa\leq x_{0}
and
\mathfrak{l}^{2}\gg|s|^{2}x_{0}^{-2}
. Then there exists constants
C>0
suitably large and
d_{0}>0
suitably small, with
d_{0}
independent of
|s|
,
\mathfrak{l}
and
x_{0}
, such that for all
n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
:

(13.13)

\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{(d_{0}x_{0})^{2n}}{(\max\{\mathfrak{l}+1,n+1\})^{2n}}|\hat{\psi}^{(n+2)}_{\ell}|^{2}\big|_{x=x_{0}}\leq Cx_{0}^{-4}\left[(\mathfrak{l}+1)^{4}|\hat{\psi}_{\ell}|^{2}+(x_{0}^{2}+|2s|^{2})|\hat{\psi}^{(1)}_{\ell}|^{2}\right]\big|_{x=x_{0}}\\
+Cx_{0}^{-4}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{(d_{0}x_{0})^{2n}}{(\max\{\mathfrak{l}+1,n+1\})^{2n}}|\partial_{x}^{n}(\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa}\widehat{\psi})_{\ell}|^{2}\big|_{x=x_{0}}.

Furthermore, for any
\gamma>0
, with
\mathfrak{l}\gg d_{0}^{-1}x_{0}^{-1}\gamma|s|
, we can estimate

(13.14)

\sum_{n=0}^{\mathfrak{l}-1}\frac{\gamma^{2n}|2s|^{2n}(\mathfrak{l}-(n+1))!^{2}}{(\mathfrak{l}+n+1)!^{2}}|\hat{\psi}^{(n+2)}_{\ell}|^{2}\big|_{x=x_{0}}\leq Cx_{0}^{-4}\left[|\hat{\psi}_{\ell}|^{2}+(x_{0}^{2}+|2s|^{2})(\mathfrak{l}+1)^{-4}|\hat{\psi}^{(1)}_{\ell}|^{2}\right]\big|_{x=x_{0}}\\
+C(\mathfrak{l}+1)^{-4}x_{0}^{-4}\sum_{n=0}^{\mathfrak{l}-1}\frac{(d_{0}x_{0})^{2n}}{(\max\{\mathfrak{l}+1,n+1\})^{2n}}|\partial_{x}^{n}(\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa}\widehat{\psi})_{\ell}|^{2}\big|_{x=x_{0}}.

Proof.

We will first establish the following estimate inductively: for
n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}

(13.15)

\frac{(d_{0}^{\prime}x_{0})^{2n}}{(\max\{\mathfrak{l}+1,n+1\})^{2n}}|\hat{\psi}^{(n+2)}_{\ell}|^{2}\big|_{x=x_{0}}\leq Cx_{0}^{-4}\left[(\mathfrak{l}+1)^{4}|\hat{\psi}_{\ell}|^{2}+(x_{0}^{2}+|2s|^{2})|\hat{\psi}^{(1)}_{\ell}|^{2}\right]\\
+Cx_{0}^{-4}\sum_{k=0}^{n}\frac{(d_{0}^{\prime}x_{0})^{2n}}{(\max\{\mathfrak{l}+1,k+1\})^{2k}}|\partial_{x}^{k}(\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa}\widehat{\psi})_{\ell}|^{2}\big|_{x=x_{0}}.

with
d_{0}<d_{0}^{\prime}
. Note that the
n=0
case follows immediately from (
13.1
) with
n=0
. Now suppose (
13.15
) holds for
n\leq N
. By applying (
13.1
) with
n=N+1
, we obtain

\frac{(d_{0}^{\prime}x_{0})^{2(N+1)}}{(\max\{\mathfrak{l}+1,N+2\})^{2(N+1)}}|\hat{\psi}^{(N+3)}|^{2}\big|_{x=x_{0}}\leq C\frac{(d_{0}^{\prime}x_{0})^{2(N+1)}}{(\max\{\mathfrak{l}+1,N+1\})^{2N}}\\
\times\Bigg[((N+2)^{2}x_{0}^{-2}+|2s|^{2})|\hat{\psi}^{(N+2)}|^{2}\big|_{x=x_{0}}+x_{0}^{-4}(\mathfrak{l}(\mathfrak{l}+1)-(N+1)(N+2))^{2}|\hat{\psi}^{(N+1)}|^{2}\big|_{x=x_{0}}\\
+x_{0}^{-4}|\partial_{x}^{N+1}(\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa}\widehat{\psi})_{\ell}|^{2}\big|_{x=x_{0}}\Bigg]\\
\leq C\frac{(d_{0}^{\prime}x_{0})^{2N}}{(\max\{\mathfrak{l}+1,N+1\})^{2N}}\frac{d_{0}^{\prime 2}((N+2)^{2}+|2s|^{2}x_{0}^{-2})}{(\max\{\mathfrak{l}+1,N+1\})^{2}}|\hat{\psi}^{(N+2)}|^{2}\big|_{x=x_{0}}\\
+C\frac{(d_{0}^{\prime}x_{0})^{2(N-1)}}{(\max\{\mathfrak{l}+1,N\})^{2(N-1)}}\frac{d_{0}^{\prime 4}(\mathfrak{l}(\mathfrak{l}+1)-(N+1)(N+2))^{2}}{(\max\{\mathfrak{l}+1,N+1\})^{2}(\max\{\mathfrak{l}+1,N+2\})^{2}}|\hat{\psi}^{(N+1)}|^{2}\big|_{x=x_{0}}\\
+C\frac{(d_{0}^{\prime}x_{0})^{2(N+1)}}{(\max\{\mathfrak{l}+1,N+2\})^{2(N+1)}}x_{0}^{-4}|\partial_{x}^{N+1}(\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa}\widehat{\psi})_{\ell}|^{2}\\
\leq\frac{C}{2}\frac{(d_{0}^{\prime}x_{0})^{2N}}{(\max\{\mathfrak{l}+1,N+1\})^{2N}}|\hat{\psi}^{(N+2)}|^{2}\big|_{x=x_{0}}+\frac{C}{2}\frac{(d_{0}^{\prime}x_{0})^{2(N-1)}}{(\max\{\mathfrak{l}+1,N\})^{2(N-1)}}|\hat{\psi}^{(N+1)}|^{2}\big|_{x=x_{0}}\\
+C\frac{(d_{0}^{\prime}x_{0})^{2(N+1)}}{(\max\{\mathfrak{l}+1,N+2\})^{2(N+1)}}x_{0}^{-4}|\partial_{x}^{N+1}(\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa}\widehat{\psi})_{\ell}|^{2},

where we arrived at the final inequality by taking
d_{0}^{\prime}
suitably small,
|2s|^{2}x_{0}^{-2}\leq\mathfrak{l}^{2}
. We now conclude (
13.15
) with
n=N+1
by applying (
13.15
) with
n=N
and
n=N-1
.

To obtain (
13.13
), we let
0<{d_{0}}<{d_{0}}^{\prime}
and we sum both sides of (
13.15
) to obtain:

\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{(d_{0}x_{0})^{2n}}{(\max\{\mathfrak{l}+1,n+1\})^{2n}}|\hat{\psi}^{(n+2)}_{\ell}|^{2}\big|_{x=x_{0}}\leq C\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\left(\frac{d_{0}}{d_{0}^{\prime}}\right)^{n}x_{0}^{-4}\left[|\hat{\psi}_{\ell}|^{2}+(x_{0}^{2}+|2s|^{2})(\mathfrak{l}(\mathfrak{l}+1))^{-2}|\hat{\psi}^{(1)}_{\ell}|^{2}\right]\\
+Cx_{0}^{-4}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\left(\frac{d_{0}}{d_{0}^{\prime}}\right)^{2(n-k)}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{(d_{0}x_{0})^{2k}}{(\max\{\mathfrak{l}+1,k+1\})^{2k}}|\partial_{x}^{k}(\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa}\widehat{\psi})_{\ell}|^{2}\big|_{x=x_{0}}\\
\leq Cx_{0}^{-4}\left[|\hat{\psi}_{\ell}|^{2}+(x_{0}^{2}+|2s|^{2})(\mathfrak{l}(\mathfrak{l}+1))^{-2}|\hat{\psi}^{(1)}_{\ell}|^{2}\right]\\
+Cx_{0}^{-4}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{(d_{0}x_{0})^{2n}}{(\max\{\mathfrak{l}+1,n+1\})^{2n}}|\partial_{x}^{n}(\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa}\widehat{\psi})_{\ell}|^{2},

where we used that
\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\left(\frac{d_{0}}{d_{0}^{\prime}}\right)^{2j}<\infty
. We now conclude (
13.13
).

By applying Stirling’s approximation in the following form: for any
k\in\mathbb{N}

k!\sim k^{k+\frac{1}{2}}e^{-k},

we can estimate for
n\leq\mathfrak{l}-1
:

\frac{\gamma^{2n}|2s|^{2n}(\mathfrak{l}-(n+1))!^{2}}{(\mathfrak{l}+(n+1))!^{2}}\leq C\gamma^{2n}|2s|^{2n}\frac{(\mathfrak{l}-(n+1))^{2\mathfrak{l}-2(n+1)+1}}{(\mathfrak{l}+(n+1))^{2\mathfrak{l}+2(n+1)+1}}e^{4(n+1)}\\
=C\gamma^{2n}|2s|^{2n}e^{4(n+1)}\left(1-2\frac{n+1}{\mathfrak{l}+(n+1)}\right)^{4(n+1)}(\mathfrak{l}+(n+1))^{-4(n+1)}\\
\leq C\left(e^{-2}d_{0}x_{0}|2s|^{-1}\gamma^{-1}(\mathfrak{l}+1)\right)^{-2(n+1)}(d_{0}x_{0})^{2n}(\mathfrak{l}+1)^{-2n-4}\leq(\mathfrak{l}+1)^{-4}(d_{0}x_{0})^{2n}(\mathfrak{l}+1)^{-2n}

for
\mathfrak{l}\gg d_{0}^{-1}x_{0}^{-1}\gamma|2s|
. Hence, (
13.14
) follows.
∎

13.3.
Combining all derivatives

We combine Corollary
13.3
with Proposition
13.5
and Lemma
13.6
to obtain an estimate in the region
\{x\leq x_{0}\}
, with on the right-hand side only the terms
\partial_{x}^{n}(\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa}\widehat{\psi})_{\ell}
and boundary terms at
x=x_{0}
involving only the lowest two derivatives
\widehat{\psi}
and
\widehat{\psi}^{(1)}
:

Corollary 13.7
.

Let
M=1
,
N_{+}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,
\mathfrak{l}>|s|x_{0}^{-1}
and
0<\kappa<x_{0}
and let
d_{0}
be the constant from Lemma
13.6
. Let
s\in\Omega_{\sigma}
. Then, for
\lambda
suitably large, there exists a constant
C_{s,\sigma}=C(s,\sigma)>0
, an numerical constant
C>0
, such that for all
\mathbb{N}_{0}\ni N_{\infty}\geq N_{\kappa}
:

(13.16)

\sum_{n=0}^{\mathfrak{l}-1}\frac{(4\sigma)^{2n}(\mathfrak{l}-(n+1))!^{2}}{(\mathfrak{l}+n+1)!^{2}}\int_{0}^{x_{0}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(x^{4}+\kappa^{2}x^{2})|\hat{\psi}^{(n+2)}|^{2}+(x^{2}(\mathfrak{l}+1)^{2}+(n+1)^{2}\kappa^{2}+|2s|^{2})|\hat{\psi}^{(n+1)}|^{2}\\
+(\mathfrak{l}-n)^{2}(\mathfrak{l}+n+1)^{2}|\hat{\psi}^{(n)}|^{2}\,d\upsigma dx\\
+(\mathfrak{l}+1)^{3}\sum_{n=\mathfrak{l}}^{N_{\infty}}\frac{\sigma^{2n}}{n!^{2}(n+1)!^{2}}\int_{0}^{x_{0}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}x^{4}|s|^{-2}|\widehat{\psi}^{(n+2)}|^{2}+\left[1+(n+1)^{2}x^{2}|s|^{-2}\right]|\hat{\psi}_{\ell}^{(n+1)}|^{2}\,d\upsigma dx\\
+\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{(d_{0}x_{0})^{2n}}{(\max\{\mathfrak{l}+1,n+1\})^{2n}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}|\widehat{\psi}^{(n+2)}|^{2}|_{x=x_{0}}d\upsigma+(\mathfrak{l}+1)^{-4(N_{+}+1)}\sum_{n=0}^{N_{+}+1}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}x|\hat{\psi}^{(n)}|^{2}|_{x=\eta x_{0}}d\upsigma\\
\leq\boxed{C\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}|\hat{\psi}_{\ell}|^{2}+(\mathfrak{l}+1)^{-4}|\hat{\psi}^{(1)}_{\ell}|^{2}\big|_{x=x_{0}}\,d\upsigma}\\
+C\sum_{n=0}^{\mathfrak{l}-1}\frac{(4\sigma)^{2n}(\mathfrak{l}-(n+1))!^{2}}{(\mathfrak{l}+n+1)!^{2}}\int_{0}^{x_{0}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}|\partial_{x}^{n}(\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa}\widehat{\psi})_{\ell}|^{2}\,d\upsigma dx\\
+C(\mathfrak{l}+1)^{3}\sum_{n=\mathfrak{l}}^{N_{\infty}}\frac{\sigma^{2n}}{n!^{2}(n+1)!^{2}}\int_{0}^{x_{0}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}|\partial_{x}^{n}(\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa}\widehat{\psi})_{\ell}|^{2}\,d\upsigma dx\\
+C\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{(d_{0}x_{0})^{2n}}{(\max\{\mathfrak{l}+1,n+1\})^{2n}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}|\partial_{x}^{n}(\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa}\widehat{\psi})_{\ell}|^{2}|_{x=x_{0}}d\upsigma.

Proof.

Note first that the boundary terms at
x=x_{0}
on the right-hand sides of (
13.10
) and (
13.11
) can be estimated directly via (
13.14
).

Furthermore, the
x
-integral of
\widehat{\psi}^{(\mathfrak{l}+1)}
on the right-hand sides of (
13.10
) and (
13.11
) can be controlled by the
x
-integral of
\widehat{\psi}^{(\mathfrak{l}+1)}
on the left-hand side of (
13.9
), provided we multiply both sides of (
13.9
) with the factor

|s|^{-2}\frac{\gamma^{2\mathfrak{l}}(\mathfrak{l}+1)!^{2}\mathfrak{l}!^{2}}{\tilde{\sigma}^{2\mathfrak{l}}(2\mathfrak{l})!^{2}}=|s|^{-2}(\mathfrak{l}+1)^{2}\frac{\gamma^{2\mathfrak{l}}}{\tilde{\sigma}^{2\mathfrak{l}}}\frac{\mathfrak{l}!^{4}}{(2\mathfrak{l})!^{2}}

By applying Stirling’s approximation, we have that

\frac{\mathfrak{l}!^{4}}{(2\mathfrak{l})!^{2}}\sim\frac{\mathfrak{l}^{4\mathfrak{l}+2}e^{-4\mathfrak{l}}}{(2\mathfrak{l})^{4\mathfrak{l}+1}e^{-4\mathfrak{l}}}=2^{-4\mathfrak{l}}\mathfrak{l}.

By taking
\gamma=4\tilde{\sigma}
, we therefore obtain:

|s|^{2}\frac{\gamma^{2\mathfrak{l}}(\mathfrak{l}+1)!^{2}\mathfrak{l}!^{2}}{\tilde{\sigma}^{2\mathfrak{l}}(2\mathfrak{l})!^{2}}\sim|s|^{-2}(\mathfrak{l}+1)^{3}.\qed

14.
A global estimate for
\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa}

We combine Corollaries
12.8
and
13.7
, to obtain a global estimate for an
\widehat{\psi}
in terms of
\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa}\widehat{\psi}
. Recall the Hilbert spaces
H^{N_{+}}_{\sigma,\delta}
and
H^{N_{+}+1}_{\sigma,R_{\infty},2}
introduced in §
8
.

Proposition 14.1
.

Let
M=1
,
N_{+}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,
0<\kappa\ll\eta x_{0}
. Take
s\in\Omega_{\sigma}\cap\{{\re s}>(\frac{1}{2}+N_{+})\kappa_{+}\}
and
p=4(N_{+}+1)+2
. Then, for
M^{-1}R_{\infty}
and
\lambda
suitably large, there exists a constant
C_{s,\lambda,\delta,R_{\infty}}>0
and a constant
N_{\kappa}>\mathfrak{l}
, such that for
\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa}\widehat{\psi}\in H^{N_{+}}_{\sigma,\delta}
:

(14.1)

\|\widehat{\psi}\|_{H^{N_{+}+1}_{\sigma,R_{\infty},2}}^{2}+\kappa^{2}(\|x\partial_{x}^{2}\widehat{\psi}\|_{G_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}}^{2}+\|\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\widehat{\psi}\|_{G_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}}^{2})\leq C_{s,\sigma}\|\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa}\widehat{\psi}\|_{H^{N_{+}}_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}}^{2}.

Proof.

We need to show that:

(14.2)

\sum_{k=0}^{1}\|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{k}\widehat{\psi}\|_{H^{N_{+}+1}(\Sigma\cap\{\varrho\leq\frac{1}{3}R_{\infty}\})}^{2}+\|\widehat{\psi}\|_{H^{N_{+}+2}(\Sigma\cap\{\frac{1}{3}R_{\infty}\leq\varrho\leq 3R_{\infty}\})}^{2}\\
+\left|\left\|(-\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}+\mathbf{1})^{-\frac{w_{p}}{4}}\widehat{\psi}\right|\right|_{H^{N_{+}+2}(\Sigma\cap\{3R_{\infty}\leq\varrho\leq 4R_{\infty}\})}^{2}+\|\widehat{\psi}\|_{B_{R_{\infty},2}}^{2}+\|\widehat{\psi}\|_{G_{\sigma,R_{\infty},2}}^{2}+\kappa^{2}\|x\partial_{x}^{2}\widehat{\psi}\|_{G_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}}^{2}\\
\leq C_{s,\lambda,R_{\infty}}\Biggl[\|\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa}\widehat{\psi}\|_{H^{N_{+}}(\Sigma\cap\{\varrho\leq 3R_{\infty}\})}^{2}+\|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa}\widehat{\psi}\|_{H^{N_{+}}(\Sigma\cap\{\varrho\leq\frac{1}{3}R_{\infty}\})}^{2}\\
+\|(-\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}+\mathbf{1})^{-\frac{w_{p}}{4}}(\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa}\widehat{\psi})\|_{H^{N_{+}}(\Sigma\cap\{3R_{\infty}\leq\varrho\leq 4R_{\infty}\})}^{2}+\|\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa}\widehat{\psi}\|_{B_{R_{\infty}}}^{2}+\|\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa}\widehat{\psi}\|_{G_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}}^{2}\Biggr].

We consider (
12.10
) and we commute additionally with
\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}
to control additional angular derivatives on the left-hand side and obtain control of the desired
H^{N_{+}}
,
H^{N_{+}+1}
and
H^{N_{+}+2}
norms.

By using that
M^{-1}R_{\infty}\ll\sqrt{\lambda}
, we can absorb the boxed term on the right-hand side of (
13.16
) into the left-hand side of (
12.10
). In order to subsequently absorb the boxed terms that appear on the right-hand side of (
12.10
) into the left-hand side of (
13.16
), we take
p=4(N_{+}+1)+2
. Finally, we note that

\sum_{\ell=\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(\mathfrak{l}+1)^{-w_{p}}|f_{\ell}|^{2}\,d\upsigma\leq\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}2^{\frac{w_{p}}{2}}(\ell(\ell+1)+\lambda)^{-\frac{p}{2}}|f_{\ell}|^{2}\,d\upsigma\leq 2^{\frac{w_{p}}{2}}(1+\lambda^{-\frac{w_{p}}{2}})\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}|(-\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}+\mathbf{1})^{-\frac{w_{p}}{4}}f|^{2}\,d\upsigma.

We also have that

\sum_{\ell=\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(\mathfrak{l}+1)^{-w_{p}}|f_{\ell}|^{2}\,d\upsigma=\sum_{\ell=0}^{l}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(\ell+l+1)^{-w_{p}}|f_{\ell}|^{2}\,d\upsigma+\sum_{\ell=l+1}^{\infty}(\ell+1)^{-w_{p}}|f_{\ell}|^{2}\,d\upsigma\\
\geq((2l+1)^{w_{p}}+1)\sum_{\ell=0}^{\infty}(1+\ell(\ell+1))^{\frac{-w_{p}}{2}}|f_{\ell}|^{2}\,d\upsigma\geq((2l+1)^{w_{p}}+1)\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}|(-\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}+\mathbf{1})^{-\frac{w_{p}}{4}}f|^{2}\,d\upsigma.

Finally, we observe that in the case
\ell>l
, we have that
\mathfrak{l}=\ell
, whereas in the case
\ell\leq l
, we use boundedness of
\ell
to ensure that the
\mathfrak{l}
-dependent norms in (
12.10
) are comparable to the norms appearing in (
14.2
).
∎

15.
Inverting
\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa}

In this section, we will construct
\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa}^{-1}
for
\kappa\geq 0
with an appropriate Hilbert space domain and with a suitably large
\lambda
.

We first make use of the regularizing effect of
\kappa>0
to invert
\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa}
as an operator on suitable Sobolev spaces. We then appeal to the uniform-in-
\kappa
estimate from Proposition
14.1
to construct
\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa=0}^{-1}
as a limit of a sequence of operators
\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa_{n}}^{-1}
, with
\kappa_{n}>0
,
\kappa_{n}\to 0
as
n\to\infty
.

Define
H^{k}(\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa})
as the closure of
C^{\infty}([0,r_{+}^{-1}]_{x}\times\mathbb{S}^{2})
with respect to the graph norm:

\|\cdot\|_{H^{k}([0,r_{+}^{-1}]_{x}\times\mathbb{S}^{2})}+\|\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa}(\cdot)\|_{H^{k}([0,r_{+}^{-1}]_{x}\times\mathbb{S}^{2})}.

Then the map:

\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa}:H^{k}(\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa})\to H^{k}([0,r_{+}^{-1}]_{x}\times\mathbb{S}^{2})

is a well-defined bounded linear operator for all
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
.

Theorem 15.1
.

Let
N\geq N_{+}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and
{\re s}>-(N+\frac{1}{2})\min\{\kappa_{+},\kappa\}
. Then, there exists a
\lambda>0
suitably large, such that the inverse map:

\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa}^{-1}:H^{N}([0,r_{+}^{-1}]_{x}\times\mathbb{S}^{2})\cap\left\{f\in L^{2}([0,r_{+}^{-1}]_{x}\times\mathbb{S}^{2})\,|\,\exists m\in\mathbb{Z}\,:\,\partial_{\varphi}f=imf\right\}\\
\to H^{N}(\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa})\subseteq H^{N+1}([0,r_{+}^{-1}]_{x}\times\mathbb{S}^{2})

is a bounded linear operator.

Proof.

Since we are considering a domain restricted to finite azimuthal modes (with
\varphi
-dependence
e^{im\varphi}
), we can apply a straightforward modification of
[
War15
]
[Theorem 5.7] to obtain the existence of the desired inverse.
∎

Remark 15.1
.

The ability to restrict to fixed azimuthal modes on Kerr is not crucial. One can in principle derive an analogue of Theorem
15.1
