without making use of this assumption and instead make use of red-shift estimates depending on
\kappa
in the large-
r
-region, instead of Gevrey-type estimates. See also the related results in
[
Vas13
]
.

By applying Proposition
14.1
, which is uniform in
\kappa
and
m
, we can obtain the existence of
\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa}^{-1}
with domain
H_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}^{N_{+}}
.

Corollary 15.2
.

Let
\sigma\in\mathbb{R}
and take
s\in\Omega_{\sigma}\cap\{{\re s}>(\frac{1}{2}+N_{+})\kappa_{+}\}
. Then, there exists a suitably small
\kappa>0
and a suitably large
R_{\infty}
and
\lambda\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, such that:

\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa}^{-1}:H_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}^{N_{+}}\to H_{\sigma,R_{\infty},2}^{N_{+}+1}

is a bounded linear operator and there exists a constant
C_{s,\sigma,R_{\infty}}>0
such that:

(15.1)

\|\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa}^{-1}\|_{H_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}^{N_{+}}\to H_{\sigma,R_{\infty},2}^{N_{+}+1}}\leq C_{s,\sigma,R_{\infty}}.

Furthermore, for all
f\in H_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}^{N_{+}}
,

(15.2)

\kappa\|x\partial_{x}^{2}\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa}^{-1}(f)\|_{G_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}}+\kappa^{2}\|\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa}^{-1}(f)\|_{G_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}}\leq C_{s,\sigma}\|f\|_{H_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}^{N_{+}}}.

Proof.

Let
\kappa_{+}>\kappa>0
and
s\in\Omega_{\sigma}\cap\{{\re z}>(\frac{1}{2}+N_{+})\kappa_{+}\}
. Then there exists a
N_{\kappa}>N_{+}
such that
{\re s}>-(N_{\kappa}+\frac{1}{2})\kappa
. Let
f_{m}\in C^{\infty}([0,r_{+}^{-1}]_{x}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}))\cap H_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}^{N_{+}}
, such that
\partial_{\varphi}f_{m}=imf_{m}
for some
m\in\mathbb{Z}
. Then we can apply Theorem
15.1
to conclude that
\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa}^{-1}(f_{m})\in C^{\infty}([0,r_{+}^{-1}]_{x}\times\mathbb{S}^{2})
is well-defined for suitably large
\lambda
.

By Proposition
14.1
, it then follows that for
\lambda
suitably large and
\kappa
suitably small, we can estimate

\|\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa}^{-1}(f_{m})\|_{H^{N_{+}+1}_{\sigma,R_{\infty},2}}^{2}\leq C_{s,\sigma,\lambda}\|f_{m}\|_{H^{N_{+}}_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}}^{2}.

Let
f\in C^{\infty}([0,r_{+}^{-1}]_{x}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}))\cap H_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}^{N_{+}}
. Then we can decompose
f=\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}f_{m}
, with
\partial_{\varphi}f_{m}=imf_{m}
.
Since the constant in the above estimate does not depend on
m
, we can sum over
m
to conclude that for general
f\in C^{\infty}([0,r_{+}^{-1}]_{x}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}))\cap H_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}^{N_{+}}
,
\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa}^{-1}(f)=\sum_{m\in\mathbb{Z}}\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa}^{-1}(f_{m})
is well defined.

Hence,
\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa}^{-1}(C^{\infty}([0,r_{+}^{-1}]_{x}\times\mathbb{S}^{2})\cap H_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}^{N_{+}})\subseteq H_{\sigma,R_{\infty},2}^{N_{+}+1}
. Since
C^{\infty}([0,r_{+}^{-1}]_{x}\times\mathbb{S}^{2})\cap H_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}^{N_{+}}
is dense in
H_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}^{N_{+}}
, we can extend
\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa}^{-1}
to act on
H_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}^{N_{+}}
and we must have that
\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa}^{-1}(H_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}^{N_{+}})\subseteq H_{\sigma,R_{\infty},2}^{N_{+}+1}
.
∎

Proposition 15.3
.

Let
\sigma\in\mathbb{R}
and take
s\in\Omega_{\sigma}\cap\{{\re z}>-(\frac{1}{2}+N_{+})\kappa_{+}\}
. Then, there exists a suitably large
\lambda\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, such that:

(15.3)

\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda}^{-1}=\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,0}^{-1}:H_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}^{N_{+}}\to H_{\sigma,R_{\infty},2}^{N_{+}+1}

is a bounded linear operator and there exists a constant
C_{s,\sigma}>0
such that:

\|\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda}^{-1}\|_{H_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}^{N_{+}}\to H_{\sigma,R_{\infty},2}^{N_{+}+1}}\leq C_{s,\sigma}.

Furthermore, for any sequence
\{\kappa_{n}\}
, with
\kappa_{n}\to 0
as
n\to\infty

\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa_{n}}^{-1}\to\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda}^{-1}

as
n\to\infty
, with respect to the operator norm on
H_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}^{N_{+}}
.

Proof.

Note that there exist
\sigma_{2}^{2}>\sigma_{1}^{2}>\sigma^{2}
, such that
s\in\Omega_{\sigma_{1}}\cap\Omega_{\sigma_{2}}
. Let
f\in C^{\infty}([0,r_{+}^{-1}]_{x}\times\mathbb{S}^{2})\cap H_{\sigma_{2},R_{\infty}}^{N_{+}+1}
.

Let
\kappa_{n}
be a monotonically non-increasing sequence with
\kappa_{n}>0
and
\kappa_{n}\to 0
as
n\to\infty
. By Corollary
15.2
,
\widehat{\psi}_{n}:=\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa_{n}}^{-1}(f)
is a well-defined, uniformly bounded sequence in
H_{\sigma_{2},R_{\infty},2}^{N_{+}+1}
. By (
B.2
),
H_{\sigma_{2},R_{\infty},2}^{N_{+}+1}\subset\joinrel\subset H_{\sigma_{1},R_{\infty},2}^{N_{+}+1}
, so
\widehat{\psi}_{n}
admits a convergent subsequence
\{\widehat{\psi}_{n_{j}}\}
with respect to
H_{\sigma_{1},R_{\infty},2}^{N_{+}+1}
, with limit
\widehat{\psi}\in H_{\sigma_{1},R_{\infty},2}^{N_{+}+1}
. In fact, since
\{\widehat{\psi}_{n_{j}}\}
is a bounded sequence in
H_{\sigma_{2},R_{\infty},2}^{N_{+}+1}
, it admits a further subsequence with a weak limit in
H_{\sigma_{2},R_{\infty},2}^{N_{+}+1}
. By the uniqueness of limits, this limit must also be
\widehat{\psi}
, so in fact
\widehat{\psi}\in H_{\sigma_{2},R_{\infty},2}^{N_{+}+1}
.

We will now show that
\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,0}(\widehat{\psi})=f
. We have that

(15.4)

\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,0}(\widehat{\psi})=f-(\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa_{n_{j}}}-\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,0})(\widehat{\psi})+\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa_{n}}(\widehat{\psi}-\widehat{\psi}_{n_{j}}).

Note that:

(\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa_{n_{j}}}-\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,0})(\widehat{\psi})=\kappa_{n_{j}}\chi_{\varrho\geq R_{\infty}}(x\partial_{x}^{2}\widehat{\psi}+\partial_{x}\widehat{\psi}).

Hence, by Proposition
C.1

\|(\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa_{n_{j}}}-\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,0})(\widehat{\psi})\|_{H^{N_{+}}_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}}\leq 2\kappa_{n_{j}}(\|\chi_{\varrho\geq R_{\infty}}\widehat{\psi}^{(1)}\|_{H^{N_{+}}_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}}+\|x\chi_{\varrho\geq R_{\infty}}\widehat{\psi}^{(2)}\|_{H^{N_{+}}_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}})\\
\leq C\kappa_{n_{j}}(\|\widehat{\psi}\|_{H^{N_{+}+2}_{\sigma,R_{\infty},2}}+\|x\widehat{\psi}^{(2)}\|_{G_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}}).

To bound
\|x\widehat{\psi}^{(2)}\|_{G_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}}
, we first apply Lemma
C.3
, from which it follows that

\|x\widehat{\psi}^{(2)}\|_{G_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}}\leq C\|\widehat{\psi}^{(2)}\|_{G_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}}\leq C\|\widehat{\psi}^{(1)}\|_{G_{\sigma,R_{\infty},2}}.

Then we observe that we can use that
\sigma_{1}>\sigma
to further estimate:

\|\widehat{\psi}^{(1)}\|_{G_{\sigma,R_{\infty},2}}\leq C\|\widehat{\psi}\|_{G_{\sigma_{1},R_{\infty},2}}.

We can similarly bound:

\|\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa_{n_{j}}}(\widehat{\psi}-\widehat{\psi}_{n_{j}})\|_{H^{N_{+}}_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}}\leq C\|\widehat{\psi}-\widehat{\psi}_{n_{j}}\|_{H^{N_{+}+2}_{\sigma_{1},R_{\infty},2}}\to 0\quad\textnormal{as $j\to\infty$}.

Taking the limit
j\to\infty
in (
15.4
) therefore gives
\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,0}(\widehat{\psi})=f
. Hence,
\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,0}^{-1}:C^{\infty}([0,r_{+}^{-1}]_{x}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}))\cap H_{\sigma_{2},R_{\infty}}^{N_{+}}\to H_{\sigma_{2},R_{\infty},2}^{N_{+}+1}
is well-defined. Using density of
C^{\infty}([0,r_{+}^{-1}]_{x}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}))\cap H_{\sigma_{2},\delta_{2}}^{N_{+}}
in
H_{\sigma_{2},\delta_{2}}^{N_{+}}
, we conclude (
15.3
).

To conclude uniform operator convergence, we first write:

\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa_{m}}^{-1}-\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa_{n}}^{-1}=\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa_{m}}^{-1}\circ(\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa_{n}}-\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa_{m}})\circ\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa_{n}}^{-1}

Let
f\in H_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}^{N_{+}}
, then
\widehat{\psi}:=\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa_{n}}^{-1}(f)\in H_{\sigma,R_{\infty},2}^{N_{+}+1}
. Furthermore,

(\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa_{n}}-\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa_{m}})(\widehat{\psi})=(\kappa_{n}-\kappa_{m})\partial_{x}(\chi_{\varrho\geq R_{\infty}}x\partial_{x}\widehat{\psi}),

so
(\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa_{n}}-\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa_{m}})(\widehat{\psi})
vanishes in
\varrho\leq\frac{1}{2}R_{\infty}
. Define

\widehat{\psi}^{\prime}:=\chi_{\varrho\geq R_{\infty}}\partial_{x}\widehat{\psi}\in H_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}^{N_{+}},

then
\|\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa_{m}}^{-1}(\widehat{\psi}^{\prime})\|_{H_{\sigma,R_{\infty},2}^{N_{+}+1}}\leq C_{s,\sigma}\|f\|_{H_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}^{N_{+}}}
by (
15.1
). We now express:

(15.5)

(\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa_{n}}-\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa_{m}})(\widehat{\psi})=(\kappa_{n}-\kappa_{m})\partial_{x}(x\widehat{\psi}^{\prime})=(\kappa_{n}-\kappa_{m})\partial_{x}(x\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa_{m}}\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa_{m}}^{-1}(\widehat{\psi}^{\prime}))\\
=(\kappa_{n}-\kappa_{m})\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa_{m}}(\partial_{x}\circ x\cdot)\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa_{m}}^{-1}(\widehat{\psi}^{\prime})+(\kappa_{n}-\kappa_{m})[\partial_{x}\circ x,\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa_{m}}\cdot]\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa_{m}}^{-1}(\widehat{\psi}^{\prime})

and hence

(\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa_{m}}^{-1}-\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa_{n}}^{-1})(f)=(\kappa_{n}-\kappa_{m})\left(\partial_{x}(x\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa_{m}}^{-1}(\widehat{\psi}^{\prime}))+\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa_{m}}^{-1}[\partial_{x}\circ x\cdot,\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa_{m}}]\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa_{m}}^{-1}(\widehat{\psi}^{\prime})\right).

For
x\leq x_{0}
, we have that

[\partial_{x}\circ x\cdot,\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa_{m}}]=-\kappa_{m}x\partial_{x}^{2}-(2s+\kappa_{m})\partial_{x}.

Using that
\|\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa_{m}}^{-1}(\widehat{\psi}^{\prime})\|_{H_{\sigma,R_{\infty},2}^{N_{+}+1}}\leq C_{s,\sigma}\|f\|_{H_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}^{N_{+}}}
and additionally that
\kappa_{m}\|x\partial_{x}^{2}\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa_{m}}^{-1}(\widehat{\psi}^{\prime})\|_{G_{\sigma,\delta}}\leq C_{s,\sigma}\|f\|_{H_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}^{N_{+}}}
by (
15.2
), we conclude that

\|(\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa_{m}}^{-1}-\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa_{n}}^{-1})(f)\|_{H_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}^{N_{+}}}\leq C\cdot C_{s,\sigma}|\kappa_{n}-\kappa_{m}\||f\|_{H_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}^{N_{+}}}.

Since
\{\kappa_{n}\}
is converges to 0, it is Cauchy, and by the above estimate,
\{\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa_{n}}^{-1}\}
must be Cauchy in the Banach space of bounded linear operators
B(H_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}^{N_{+}},H_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}^{N_{+}})
with respect to the operator norm, so it converges. By uniqueness of limits, the limit must be equal to the operator
\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,0}^{-1}
defined above.
∎

16.
Inverting
L_{s}
: boundedness, compactness and Fredholm theory

In this section, we will establish the meromorphicity of the maps

s\mapsto L_{s,\kappa}^{-1}

with
\kappa\geq 0
.

By (
7.8
) and (
7.13
), we have the following relation between
L_{s,\kappa}
and
\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa}
.

L_{s,\kappa}=\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa}+\epsilon_{0}\cdot B_{s}+K_{s,\lambda},

where
\epsilon_{0}>0
can be taken arbitrarily small.

Proposition 16.1
.

Let
\lambda\in\mathbb{R}
. Assume that
\mathbbm{h}\equiv 1
in
\{\varrho\leq\frac{1}{2}R_{\infty}\}
. Then the linear operators

\displaystyle B_{s}:H_{\sigma,R_{\infty},2}^{N_{+}+1}\to H_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}^{N_{+}},

\displaystyle K_{s,\lambda}:H_{\sigma,R_{\infty},2}^{N_{+}+1}\to H_{\sigma,R_{\infty},2}^{N_{+}+1}

are bounded independently of
\epsilon_{0}
. Let
\mathcal{R}:H_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}^{N_{+}}\to H_{\sigma,R_{\infty},2}^{N_{+}+1}
be bounded. Then
K_{s,\lambda}\circ\mathcal{R}:H_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}^{N_{+}}\to H_{\sigma,R_{\infty},2}^{N_{+}+1}\subset H_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}^{N_{+}}
is compact.

Proof.

Note first that the compactness of
K_{s,\lambda}\circ\mathcal{R}
follows directly from the boundedness of
K_{s,\lambda}
by the fact that
H_{\sigma,R_{\infty},2}^{N_{+}+1}\subset\joinrel\subset H_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}^{N_{+}}
by (
B.3
).

Recall that

\displaystyle B_{s}=

\displaystyle\>M\epsilon_{0}^{-1}x\chi_{\varrho\geq R_{\infty}}{B}^{\infty}_{s},

\displaystyle K_{s,\lambda}=

\displaystyle\>\lambda\pi_{\leq l}+\chi_{\varrho\geq R_{\infty}}S_{\lambda}\pi_{\leq l}+{K}^{\infty}_{s}+\chi_{\varrho\leq\frac{1}{2}R_{\infty}}{K}_{s}^{+}.

We first consider
B_{s}
. Take
M^{-1}R_{\infty}
to be suitably large, so that
\epsilon_{0}^{-1}x\leq 1
in
\supp\chi_{\varrho\geq R_{\infty}}
. We need to establish boundedness of
\chi_{\varrho\geq R_{\infty}}{B}^{\infty}_{s}
.

Recall moreover that

\displaystyle B^{\infty}_{s}f=

\displaystyle\>b_{xx}\cdot x^{2}\partial_{x}^{2}f+sb_{x\tau}\cdot\partial_{x}f+s^{2}b_{\tau\tau}f+a^{2}(xb_{x\vartheta}\cdot x^{2}\partial_{x}+sxb_{\tau\vartheta})\sin\vartheta\partial_{\vartheta}f

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle\>a^{4}x^{4}\cdot b_{\vartheta\vartheta}(\sin\vartheta\partial_{\vartheta})^{2}f+b_{x}\cdot x\partial_{x}f+a^{2}x^{2}b_{\vartheta}\cdot\sin\vartheta\partial_{\vartheta}f+sb_{\tau}f+b\cdot f.

We apply Proposition
C.1
to estimate
\mathcal{B}^{\infty}_{s}f
:

\|{B}_{s}^{\infty}f\|_{H^{N_{+}}_{\sigma,\delta}}\leq C_{s}\left[\sum_{\star\in\{x,\tau,\vartheta\}}\|b_{\star\star}\|_{H^{N_{+}}_{\sigma,R_{\infty};\textnormal{coeff}}}+\|b_{\star}\|_{H^{N_{+}}_{\sigma,R_{\infty};\textnormal{coeff}}}+\|b\|_{H^{N_{+}}_{\sigma,R_{\infty};\textnormal{coeff}}}\right]\\
\times\left(\|f\|_{H^{N_{+}}_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}}+\|\partial_{x}f\|_{H^{N_{+}}_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}}+a^{2}\|x^{2}\sin\vartheta\partial_{\vartheta}f\|_{H^{N_{+}}_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}}+\|\chi_{\varrho\geq R_{\infty}}x^{2}\partial_{x}^{2}f\|_{H^{N_{+}}_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}}\right)\\
+a^{2}\|\chi_{\varrho\geq R_{\infty}}x^{4}(\sin\vartheta\partial_{\vartheta})^{2}f\|_{H^{N_{+}}_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}})\\
\leq C_{s}\left[\sum_{\star\in\{x,\tau,\vartheta\}}\|b_{\star\star}\|_{H^{N_{+}}_{\sigma,R_{\infty};\textnormal{coeff}}}+\|b_{\star}\|_{H^{N_{+}}_{\sigma,R_{\infty};\textnormal{coeff}}}+\|b\|_{H^{N_{+}}_{\sigma,R_{\infty};\textnormal{coeff}}}\right]\|f\|_{H^{N_{+}+1}_{\sigma,R_{\infty},2}}\\
\leq C_{{\rm coeff},s}\cdot\|f\|_{H^{N_{+}+1}_{\sigma,R_{\infty},2}},

where the final inequality follows from the analyticity properties of
b,b_{\star}
and
b_{\star\star}
that were established in Lemma
7.1
. Note moreover that we used here that the
H^{N_{+}+1}_{\sigma,R_{\infty},2}
norm controls two more derivatives (with degenerate factors) than the
H^{N_{+}}_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}
norm in the region
\varrho\geq\frac{1}{2}R_{\infty}
and one more derivative globally. Boundedness of
\|B_{s}\|
follows by combining the above estimates.

We now turn to
K_{s,\lambda}
. Recall that

\displaystyle K^{\infty}_{s}f=

\displaystyle\>-2M^{2}h(0)s^{2}f,

\displaystyle r^{-2}\rho^{2}{K}_{s}^{+}f=

\displaystyle\>\left[2r-\frac{d}{dr}(\Delta\mathbbm{h})\right]s(r^{-1}f)+[\mathbbm{h}^{2}\Delta-2\mathbbm{h}(r^{2}+a^{2})+a^{2}\sin^{2}\theta]s^{2}f

\displaystyle-2a(\mathbbm{h}-1)s\partial_{\varphi_{*}}f.

In order for all terms in
K_{s}
to be zeroth-order terms, we use the assumption
\mathbbm{h}\equiv 1
in
\varrho\leq R_{\infty}
.

Hence, applying Proposition
C.1
, it is straightforward to estimate

\|\chi_{\varrho\leq\frac{1}{2}R_{\infty}}{K}_{s}^{+}f\|_{H^{N_{+}+1}_{\sigma,R_{\infty},2}}\leq C_{{\rm coeff},s}\cdot\|f\|_{H^{N_{+}+1}_{\sigma,R_{\infty},2}}.

Boundedness of
K^{\infty}_{s},\chi_{\varrho\leq\frac{1}{2}R_{\infty}}{K}_{s}^{+}:H_{s,R_{\infty},2}^{N_{+}+1}\to H_{s,R_{\infty},2}^{N_{+}+1}
is therefore immediate. Consider
\lambda\pi_{\leq l}
and
\chi_{\varrho\geq R_{\infty}}S_{\lambda}\pi_{\leq l}
. The operator
\lambda\pi_{\leq l}:H_{s,R_{\infty},2}^{N_{+}+1}\to H_{s,R_{\infty},2}^{N_{+}+1}
can easily be seen to be bounded. Finally, since
\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}|S_{\lambda}\pi_{\leq l}f|^{2}\,d\upsigma\leq\lambda^{2}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}f_{\leq l}|^{2}\leq\lambda^{4}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}|f_{\leq l}|^{2}\,d\upsigma
, we have that

\chi_{\varrho\geq R_{\infty}}S_{\lambda}\pi_{\leq l}:H_{\sigma,R_{\infty},2}^{N_{+}+1}\to H_{\sigma,R_{\infty},2}^{N_{+}+1}.

is bounded.
∎

Recall the definitions of a meromorphic family of operators in Definition
10.1
and the definition of the cokernel of an operator in Definition
10.2
. In the proposition below, we establish meromorphicity of the inverses
L_{s,\kappa}^{-1}
with
\kappa\geq 0
, and we relate the poles to the cokernel of
L_{s,\kappa}
.

Proposition 16.2
.

Let
\sigma\in\mathbb{R}
and
N_{+}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. For
\kappa\geq 0
sufficiently small and
M^{-1}R_{\infty}
sufficiently large:

(i)

The family of operators
\{L_{s,\kappa}^{-1}:H_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}^{N_{+}}\to H_{\sigma,R_{\infty},2}^{N_{+}+1}\}
with
s\in\Omega_{\sigma}\cap\{{\re s}>-(\frac{1}{2}+N_{+})\kappa_{+}\}
is meromorphic. We denote with
\mathscr{Q}_{\sigma,R_{\infty},N_{+},\kappa}
the set of poles of
\{L_{s,\kappa}^{-1}\}
. The set
\mathscr{Q}_{\sigma,R_{\infty},N_{+},\kappa}
is independent of the precise choice of
\mathbbm{h}
.

(ii)

Let
s\in\Omega_{\sigma}\cap\{{\re s}>-(\frac{1}{2}+N_{+})\kappa_{+}\}
. Then the space
\ker L_{s}
is finite dimensional with
\dim\ker L_{s,\kappa}=\dim\textnormal{coker}\,L_{s,\kappa}
and
\textnormal{ran}\,L_{s,\kappa}^{*}=(\ker L_{s,\kappa})^{\perp}\subset H_{\sigma,R_{\infty},2}^{N_{+}+1}
, with
L_{s,\kappa}^{*}
the Hermitian adjoint and
\textnormal{coker}\,L_{s,\kappa}^{*}
is isomorphic to
\ker L_{s,\kappa}
.

Proof.

Without loss of generality, we consider the
\kappa=0
case. The argument proceeds in exactly the same way for
\kappa>0
. We first suppose that
\mathbbm{h}=1
in
\{\varrho\leq\frac{1}{2}R_{\infty}\}
. By Proposition
15.3
,
\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda}^{-1}:H_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}^{N_{+}}\to H_{\sigma,R_{\infty},2}^{N_{+}+1}
is well-defined for suitably large
\lambda
. We can express:

\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda}+\epsilon_{0}B_{s}=(\mathbf{1}+\epsilon_{0}B_{s}\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda}^{-1})\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda}.

By Proposition
16.1
,
B_{s}:H_{\sigma,R_{\infty},2}^{N_{+}+1}\to H_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}^{N_{+}}
is bounded, so if we take
\epsilon_{0}>\frac{1}{\|B_{s}\|\cdot\|\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda}^{-1}\|}
, we have that
\epsilon_{0}B_{s}\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda}^{-1}:H_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}^{N_{+}}\to H_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}^{N_{+}}
is bounded with operator norm less than 1. Hence,
(\mathbf{1}+\epsilon_{0}B_{s}\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda}^{-1})^{-1}:H_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}^{N_{+}}\to H_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}^{N_{+}}
is a bounded operator that we can express as follows in terms of a Neumann series:

(\mathbf{1}+\epsilon_{0}B_{s}\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda}^{-1})^{-1}=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(-1)^{k}(\epsilon_{0}B_{s}\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda}^{-1})^{k}.

We now conclude that

-T_{s}:=(\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda}+\epsilon_{0}B_{s})^{-1}=\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda}^{-1}(\mathbf{1}+\epsilon_{0}B_{s}\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda}^{-1})^{-1}:H_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}^{N_{+}}\to H_{\sigma,R_{\infty},2}^{N_{+}+1}

is a well-defined bounded linear operator and we can express:

L_{s}=-(\mathbf{1}-K_{s,\lambda}T_{s})T_{s}^{-1}

on
H_{\sigma,R_{\infty},2}^{N_{+}+1}
.

Formally,
L_{s}^{-1}=-T_{s}(\mathbf{1}-K_{s,\lambda}T_{s})^{-1}
. By the analytic Fredholm theorem (see for example
[
RR06
]
[Theorem 8.92]),
(\mathbf{1}-K_{s,\lambda}T_{s})^{-1}:H_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}^{N_{+}}\to H_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}^{N_{+}}
defines a meromorphic family of bounded operators if
\{K_{s,\lambda}T_{s}\}
is holomorphic and compact as an operator from
H_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}^{N_{+}}
to
H_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}^{N_{+}}
. Compactness follows directly from Proposition
16.1
. Consider the following difference quotient: let

\frac{K_{s,\lambda}T_{s}-K_{s_{0},\lambda}T_{s_{0}}}{s-s_{0}}(f)=\frac{K_{s,\lambda}-K_{s_{0},\lambda}}{s-s_{0}}T_{s}+K_{s_{0},\lambda}\frac{T_{s}-T_{s_{0}}}{s-s_{0}}\\
=\frac{K_{s,\lambda}-K_{s_{0},\lambda}}{s-s_{0}}T_{s}-K_{s_{0},\lambda}T_{s}\frac{\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda}-\mathfrak{L}_{s_{0},\lambda}+\epsilon_{0}(B_{s}-B_{s_{0}})}{s-s_{0}}T_{s_{0}}\\
=\frac{K_{s,\lambda}-K_{s_{0},\lambda}}{s-s_{0}}T_{s}-K_{s_{0},\lambda}T_{s}\frac{L_{s}-{L}_{s_{0}}-(K_{s,\lambda}-K_{s_{0},\lambda})}{s-s_{0}}T_{s_{0}}

Note that

\displaystyle\frac{L_{s}-{L}_{s_{0}}}{s-s_{0}}(f)=

\displaystyle\>2\partial_{x}f+Mx\left[b_{x\tau}\partial_{x}f+(s+s_{0})b_{\tau\tau}f+xb_{\tau\vartheta})\sin\vartheta\partial_{\vartheta}f+b_{\tau}f\right]-2M^{2}(s+s_{0})h(0)f,

\displaystyle\frac{K_{s,\lambda}-K_{s_{0},\lambda}}{s-s_{0}}=

\displaystyle\>-2M^{2}(s+s_{0})h(0)f

By the above expressions, it is straightforward to see that
\lim_{s\to s_{0}}\frac{K_{s,\lambda}T_{s}-K_{s_{0},\lambda}T_{s_{0}}}{s-s_{0}}
is a well-defined operator from
H_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}^{N_{+}}
to
H_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}^{N_{+}}
.

We conclude that
L_{s}^{-1}:H_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}^{N_{+}}\to H_{\sigma,\delta,2}^{N_{+}+2}
constitute a meromorphic family of operators, so (i) holds when
\mathbbm{h}=1
in
\{\varrho\leq\frac{1}{2}R_{\infty}\}
.

Since
K_{s,\lambda}T_{s}
is compact, we have by the Fredholm alternative, that

\dim\ker(T_{s}L_{s})^{*}=\dim\ker(\mathbf{1}-(K_{s,\lambda}T_{s})^{*})=\dim\ker(\mathbf{1}-(K_{s,\lambda}T_{s})^{*})=\dim\ker(T_{s}L_{s})<\infty

and

\textnormal{ran}((T_{s}L_{s})^{*})=\textnormal{ran}\,(\mathbf{1}-(K_{s,\lambda}T_{s})^{*})=[\ker(\mathbf{1}-K_{s,\lambda}T_{s})]^{\perp}=(\ker(T_{s}L_{s}))^{\perp},

with
T_{s}^{*}
and
L_{s}^{*}
the Hilbert space adjoints of
T_{s}
and
L_{s}
, respectively. By the boundedness of
T_{s}
and
T_{s}L_{s}
, we have that
(T_{s}L_{s})^{*}=L_{s}^{*}T_{s}^{*}:H_{\sigma,R_{\infty},2}^{N_{+}+1}\to H_{\sigma,R_{\infty},2}^{N_{+}+1}
.

Suppose
\widehat{\psi}\in\ker L_{s}
. Then
L_{s}\widehat{\psi}=0
, so
T_{s}L_{s}\widehat{\psi}=0
, so
\widehat{\psi}\in\ker(T_{s}L_{s})
. Suppose now that
\widehat{\psi}\in\ker(T_{s}L_{s})
, then
T_{s}L_{s}\widehat{\psi}=0
. Since
\ker T_{s}=\{0\}
, we must have that
L_{s}\widehat{\psi}=0
, so
\ker L_{s}=\ker(T_{s}L_{s})
. Similarly, since
\ker T_{s}=\{0\}
, we have that
\textnormal{ran}\,T_{s}^{*}=H_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}^{N_{+}}
, so
\textnormal{ran}\,L_{s}^{*}=\textnormal{ran}\,(L_{s}^{*}T_{s}^{*})
.

Finally, observe that

\textnormal{coker}\,(L_{s}^{*})=H_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}^{N_{+}}/\textnormal{ran}\,(L_{s}^{*})\cong(\textnormal{ran}\,(L_{s}^{*}))^{\perp}=(\textnormal{ran}\,((T_{s}L_{s})^{*}))^{\perp}=\ker(T_{s}L_{s})=\ker L_{s}

and we conclude (ii) when
\mathbbm{h}=1
in
\{\varrho\leq\frac{1}{2}R_{\infty}\}
.

Now, consider the case of more general
\mathbbm{h}
, which do not need to satisfy
\mathbbm{h}\equiv 1
in
\{\varrho\leq\frac{R_{\infty}}{2}\}
. Denote with
\Sigma^{\mathbbm{h}}
the hypersurface
\Sigma
corresponding to the more general function
\mathbbm{h}
. Similarly, denote with
L_{s,\kappa}^{\mathbbm{h}}
and
\tau^{\mathbbm{h}}
the operator
L_{s}
and the time function
\tau
with respect to
\mathbbm{h}
, respectively. Let
\mathbbm{h}_{0}
satisfy
\mathbbm{h}_{0}=1
in
\{\varrho\leq\frac{1}{2}R_{\infty}\}
.

By assumption on the hyperboloidal time functions, we must have that
\mathbbm{h}_{0}-\mathbbm{h}
is integrable in
r
, so

\tau^{\mathbbm{h}}-\tau^{\mathbbm{h}_{0}}=\int_{\varrho}^{\infty}(\mathbbm{h}_{0}-\mathbbm{h})(\varrho^{\prime})\,d\varrho^{\prime}

is well-defined.

We will show that invertibility of
L_{s,\kappa}^{\mathbbm{h}_{0}}
is equivalent to invertibility of
L_{s,\kappa}^{\mathbbm{h}}
.

Let
Q_{s}:L^{2}(\Sigma^{\mathbbm{h}})\to L^{2}(\Sigma^{\mathbbm{h}_{0}})
be defined as follows: let
\overline{f}:D^{+}(\Sigma^{\mathbbm{h}})\to\mathbb{C}
denote the trivial extension of
f\in L^{2}(\Sigma^{\mathbbm{h}})
to
D^{+}(\Sigma^{\mathbbm{h}})
, i.e.
\overline{f}(\tau^{\mathbbm{h}},\varrho,\vartheta,\varphi_{*})=\overline{f}(\varrho,\vartheta,\varphi_{*})
. Then we define

Q_{s}(f)(\varrho,\vartheta,\varphi_{*})=(e^{s\tau^{\mathbbm{h}}}\overline{f})|_{\Sigma^{\tau^{\mathbbm{h}_{0}}}}(\varrho,\vartheta,\varphi_{*})=e^{s\int_{\varrho}^{\infty}(\mathbbm{h}_{0}-\mathbbm{h})(\varrho^{\prime})\,d\varrho^{\prime}}f(\varrho,\vartheta,\varphi_{*})

to obtain

L_{s}^{\mathbbm{h}}=Q_{s}^{-1}\circ L_{s}^{\mathbbm{h}_{0}}\circ Q_{s},

with
Q_{s}^{-1}(f)=e^{-s\int_{\varrho}^{R_{\infty}}(\mathbbm{h}_{0}-\mathbbm{h})(\varrho^{\prime})\,d\varrho^{\prime}}f
. Since the factor
e^{s\int_{\varrho}^{R_{\infty}}(\mathbbm{h}_{0}-\mathbbm{h})(\varrho^{\prime})\,d\varrho^{\prime}}
is analytic in
x=\frac{1}{\varrho}
near
x=0
by assumption on
\mathbbm{h}
, we can apply Proposition
C.1
to conclude that there exists a constant
B>0
such that

\displaystyle\|Q_{s}(f)\|_{H^{N_{+}}_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}}\leq

\displaystyle\>B\|f\|_{H^{N_{+}}_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}}\quad\textnormal{and}\quad\|Q_{s}(f)\|_{H^{N_{+}+1}_{\sigma,R_{\infty},2}}\leq B\|f\|_{H^{N_{+}+1}_{\sigma,R_{\infty},2}},

\displaystyle\|Q_{s}^{-1}(f)\|_{H^{N_{+}}_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}}\leq

\displaystyle\>B\|f\|_{H^{N_{+}}_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}}\quad\textnormal{and}\quad\|Q_{s}^{-1}(f)\|_{H^{N_{+}+1}_{\sigma,R_{\infty},2}}\leq B\|f\|_{H^{N_{+}+1}_{\sigma,R_{\infty},2}}.

The restrictions
Q_{s},Q_{s}^{-1}:H^{N_{+}}_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}\to H^{N_{+}}_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}
and
Q_{s},Q_{s}^{-1}:H^{N_{+}+1}_{\sigma,R_{\infty},2}\to H^{N_{+}+1}_{\sigma,R_{\infty},2}
are therefore bounded operators, so

(L_{s}^{\mathbbm{h}})^{-1}=Q_{s}^{-1}\circ(L_{s}^{\mathbbm{h}_{0}})^{-1}\circ Q_{s}:H^{N_{+}}_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}\to H^{N_{+}+1}_{\sigma,R_{\infty},2}

is well-defined and bounded if and only if
(L_{s}^{\mathbbm{h}_{0}})^{-1}:H^{N_{+}}_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}\to H^{N_{+}+1}_{\sigma,R_{\infty},2}
is well-defined and bounded. This moreover implies that the poles of
(L_{s}^{\mathbbm{h}})^{-1}
are independent of the choice of
\mathbbm{h}
, provided that
\mathbbm{h}
satisfies the assumptions stated in §
6.2
.
∎

We denote
\mathscr{Q}_{\sigma,R_{\infty},N_{+}}:=\mathscr{Q}_{\sigma,R_{\infty},N_{+},0}
. In the next proposition, we will show that sets
\mathscr{Q}_{\sigma,R_{\infty},N_{+}}
do not depend on the choice of
R_{\infty}
,
N_{+}
and
\sigma
, provided
R_{\infty}
is taken to be suitably large and
\sigma
is restricted to an appropriate interval. We will also show that the union of
\mathscr{Q}_{\rm reg}:=\bigcup_{\sigma\in\mathbb{R},N_{+}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\mathscr{Q}_{\sigma,R_{\infty},N_{+}}
has no accumulation points in
\Omega=\left\{z\in\mathbb{C}\setminus\{0\}\,,\,|{\rm arg}(z)|<\frac{2}{3}\pi\right\}
.

We first need to establish suitably convergence properties of the elements in
\mathscr{Q}_{\sigma,R_{\infty},N_{+},\kappa}
as
\kappa\downarrow 0
and with respect to small perturbations
L_{s,\kappa}+\delta\widetilde{Q}
:

Lemma 16.3
.

Let
\{\kappa_{n}\}
and
\{\delta_{n}\}
be a sequences in
[0,\infty)
such that
\kappa_{n}\to 0
and
\delta_{n}\to 0
as
n\to\infty
. Define
T_{s,n}:=-(\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa_{n}}+\delta_{n}\cdot\widetilde{Q}+\epsilon_{0}B_{s})^{-1}
, with
\widetilde{Q}\in B(H_{\sigma,R_{\infty},2}^{N_{+}+1},H_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}^{N_{+}})
and let
T_{s}:=-(\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda}+\epsilon_{0}B_{s})^{-1}
.

(i)

Suppose that there exists a
s_{*}\in\Omega_{\sigma}\cap\{\re(z)>-(\frac{1}{2}+N_{+})\kappa_{+}\}
such that
\ker(\mathbf{1}-K_{s_{*},\lambda}T_{s_{*}})\neq\{0\}
. Then there exist a sequence
\{s_{n}\}
in
\Omega_{\sigma}\cap\{\re(z)>-(\frac{1}{2}+N_{+})\kappa_{+}\}
such that
s_{n}\to s_{*}
as
n\to\infty
and

\ker(\mathbf{1}-K_{s_{n},\lambda}T_{s_{n},n})\neq\{0\}.

Furthermore, for all
\widehat{\psi}_{s_{*}}\in\ker L_{s_{*}}
, there exists a sequence
\{\widehat{\psi}_{s_{n}}\}
in
\ker(\mathbf{1}-K_{s_{n},\lambda}T_{s_{n},n})
, such that

\|\widehat{\psi}_{s_{n}}-\widehat{\psi}_{s_{*}}\|_{H_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}^{N_{+}}}\to 0

as
n\to\infty
.

(ii)

Conversely, suppose that there exists a sequence
\{s_{n}\}
in
\Omega_{\sigma}\cap\{\re(z)>-(\frac{1}{2}+N_{+})\kappa_{+}\}
,
\widehat{\psi}_{s_{n}}
with
||\widehat{\psi}_{s_{n}}||_{H^{N_{+}}_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}}=1
and
\{\kappa_{n}\}
, such that
s_{n}\to s_{*}\in\Omega_{\sigma}\cap\{\re(z)>-(\frac{1}{2}+N_{+})\kappa_{+}\}
,
\kappa_{n}\downarrow 0
and
\widehat{\psi}_{n}\in\ker(\mathbf{1}-K_{s_{n},\lambda}T_{s_{n},n})
. Then there exists a
0\neq\widehat{\psi}\in\ker L_{s_{*}}
.

Proof.

The key ingredients of the proof of (i) are the convergence property
\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa_{n}}^{-1}\to\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda}^{-1}
with respect to the operator norm, which we established in (
15.3
), together with the fact that
(\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa}+\delta_{n}\cdot\widetilde{Q})^{-1}
is a well-defined bounded operator for
\delta_{n}<\frac{1}{\|\widetilde{Q}\|\cdot\|\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa}^{-1}\|}
, which follows from the proof of Proposition
16.2
, and
(\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa}+\delta_{n}\cdot\widetilde{Q})^{-1}
converges to
\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa}^{-1}
with respect to the operator norm. We also use that
\ker L_{s}=\ker(\mathbf{1}-K_{s_{*},\lambda}T_{s_{*}})
. Then Hurwitz’s theorem is applied. We refer the reader to
[
GW21
]
[Proposition 11.6 and 11.7] for the details, where
K_{s_{n},\lambda}T_{s_{n},n}
replaces the operator
A_{\kappa_{n},s}
from
[
GW21
]
.

We prove (ii) by first proceeding as in the proof of Proposition
15.3
. Let
\sigma^{\prime}<\sigma
. Then
\widehat{\psi}_{s_{n}}\in H^{N_{+}+1}_{\sigma,R_{\infty},2}\subset\joinrel\subset H^{N_{+}+2}_{\sigma^{\prime},R_{\infty},2}
, so
\{\widehat{\psi}_{n}\}
admits a convergent subsequence with non-trivial limit
\widehat{\psi}_{s_{*}}\in H^{N_{+}+1}_{\sigma^{\prime},R_{\infty},2}
. Since
\{\widehat{\psi}_{n}\}
is also weakly convergent in
H^{N_{+}+1}_{\sigma,R_{\infty},2}
and there is uniqueness of limits, we must have that
\widehat{\psi}_{s_{*}}\in H^{N_{+}+1}_{\sigma,R_{\infty},2}
.

We will now show that
\widehat{\psi}_{s_{*}}\in\ker L_{s_{*}}
. By splitting

\mathbf{1}-K_{s_{*},\lambda}T_{s_{*}}=(\mathbf{1}-K_{s_{n},\lambda}T_{s_{n},n})+(K_{s_{n},\lambda}T_{s_{n},n}-K_{s_{*},\lambda}T_{s_{n}}),

we obtain

(\mathbf{1}-K_{s_{*},\lambda}T_{s_{*}})\widehat{\psi}_{s_{*}}=(\mathbf{1}-K_{s_{*},\lambda}T_{s_{*}})(\widehat{\psi}_{s_{*}}-\widehat{\psi}_{s_{n}})+(K_{s_{n},\lambda}T_{s_{n},n}-K_{s_{*},\lambda}T_{s_{n}})\widehat{\psi}_{s_{*}}.

The terms on the right-hand side go to zero in
H^{N_{+}+1}_{\sigma^{\prime},R_{\infty},2}
as
n\to\infty
, so we conclude that
\widehat{\psi}_{s_{*}}\in\ker L_{s_{*}}
.
∎

Proposition 16.4
.

Let
N+\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
and let
R_{\infty}>0
be suitably large, such that Proposition
16.2
applies.

(i)

Let
\tilde{N}_{+}\geq N_{+}
. Then
\mathscr{Q}_{\sigma,R_{\infty},\tilde{N}_{+}}=\mathscr{Q}_{\sigma,R_{\infty},N_{+}}
,
\ker L_{s}\subset C^{\infty}(\hat{\Sigma})
and
\ker(\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa}+\epsilon_{0}B_{s}+K_{s,\lambda})\subset C^{\infty}(\hat{\Sigma})
for
s\in\Omega_{\sigma}\cap\{\re(z)>-(\frac{1}{2}+N_{+})\kappa_{+}\}
with
\sigma\in\mathbb{R}
.

(ii)

Let
\widetilde{R}_{\infty}\geq R_{\infty}
. Then
\mathscr{Q}_{\sigma,\widetilde{R}_{\infty},N_{+}}=\mathscr{Q}_{\sigma,R_{\infty},N_{+}}
.

(iii)

Let
|\sigma^{\prime}|>|\sigma|
and
\Omega_{\sigma^{\prime}}\cap\Omega_{\sigma}\neq\emptyset
. Then

\mathscr{Q}_{\sigma,R_{\infty},N_{+}}\cap(\Omega_{\tilde{\sigma}}\cap\Omega_{\sigma})=\mathscr{Q}_{\sigma^{\prime},R_{\infty},N_{+}}\cap(\Omega_{\sigma^{\prime}}\cap\Omega_{\sigma}).

In particular,
\mathscr{Q}_{\rm reg}=\bigcup_{\sigma\in\mathbb{R},N_{+}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\mathscr{Q}_{\sigma,R_{\infty},N_{+}}
has no accumulation points in

\Omega=\left\{z\in\mathbb{C}\setminus\{0\}\,,\,|{\rm arg}(z)|<\frac{2}{3}\pi\right\}
.

Proof.

We will first prove (i). Let
\widehat{\psi}_{s}\in\ker L_{s}\subset H^{N_{+}+1}_{\sigma,R_{\infty},2}
. Then

(\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda}+\epsilon_{0}B_{s})\widehat{\psi}_{s}=-K_{s,\lambda}\widehat{\psi}_{s}.

In the proof of Proposition
16.2
, we showed that for
\epsilon_{0}
suitably small,
(\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda}+\epsilon_{0}B_{s})^{-1}:H_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}^{N_{+}}\to H_{\sigma,R_{\infty},2}^{N_{+}+1}
is a well-defined bounded operator and hence, there exists a constant
C>0
, such that

(16.1)

\|\widehat{\psi}_{s}\|_{H^{N_{+}+1}_{\sigma,R_{\infty},2}}=\|(\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda}+\epsilon_{0}B_{s})^{-1}K_{s,\lambda}\widehat{\psi}_{s}\|_{H^{N_{+}+1}_{\sigma,R_{\infty},2}}\leq C\|K_{s,\lambda}\widehat{\psi}_{s}\|_{H^{N_{+}}_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}}.

By Proposition
16.1
, we have in fact that
\|K_{s,\lambda}\widehat{\psi}_{s}\|_{H^{N_{+}+1}_{\sigma,R_{\infty},2}}<\infty
.

Now let
\tilde{N}_{+}=N_{+}+1
, then (
16.1
) applies with
\tilde{N}_{+}
replacing
N_{+}
:

\|\widehat{\psi}_{s}\|_{H^{\tilde{N}_{+}+1}_{\sigma,R_{\infty},2}}\leq C\|K_{s,\lambda}\psi_{s}\|_{H^{\tilde{N}_{+}}_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}}\leq C\|K_{s,\lambda}\widehat{\psi}_{s}\|_{H^{N_{+}+1}_{\sigma,R_{\infty},2}}<\infty.

Therefore
\widehat{\psi}_{s}\in H^{N_{+}+2}_{\sigma,R_{\infty},2}
.

Conversely, suppose that
\widehat{\psi}\in\ker L_{s}\subset H^{\tilde{N}_{+}+1}_{\sigma,R_{\infty},2}
with
\tilde{N}_{+}=N_{+}+1
. By (
16.1
), we have that

\|\widehat{\psi}_{s}\|_{H^{N_{+}+1}_{\sigma,R_{\infty},2}}\leq\|K_{s,\lambda}\widehat{\psi}_{s}\|_{H^{N_{+}}_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}}\leq\|K_{s,\lambda}\widehat{\psi}_{s}\|_{H^{\tilde{N}_{+}+1}_{\sigma,R_{\infty},2}}<\infty,

where we used in particular that for
w_{\tilde{p}}
defined with
\tilde{p}=4(\tilde{N}_{+}+1)+2=4(N_{+}+1)+2+4
and
w_{p}
defined with
p=4(N_{+}+1)+2
:

\|(\mathbf{1}-\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}})^{-\frac{w_{p}}{4}}K_{s,\lambda}\widehat{\psi}_{s}\|_{H^{N_{+}}_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}}\leq\|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(\mathbf{1}-\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}})^{-\frac{w_{\tilde{p}}}{4}}K_{s,\lambda}\widehat{\psi}_{s}\|_{H^{\tilde{N}_{+}}_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}}\leq\|(\mathbf{1}-\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}})^{-\frac{w_{\tilde{p}}}{4}}\widehat{\psi}_{s}\|_{H^{\tilde{N}_{+}+1}_{\sigma,R_{\infty},2}}.

Hence,
\widehat{\psi}\in\ker L_{s}\subset H^{{N}_{+}+1}_{\sigma,R_{\infty},2}
.

We can repeat this procedure inductively to conclude that the kernel of
L_{s}
with respect to
H^{N_{+}+1}_{\sigma,R_{\infty},2}
agrees with the kernel of
L_{s}
with respect to
H^{\tilde{N}_{+}+1}_{\sigma,R_{\infty},2}
for arbitrary
\tilde{N}_{+}\geq N_{+}
. Therefore, we must have that
\mathscr{Q}_{\sigma,R_{\infty},\tilde{N}_{+}}=\mathscr{Q}_{\sigma,R_{\infty},N_{+}}
.

Note that
(\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda}+\epsilon_{0}B_{s})(\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\widehat{\psi}_{s})=-\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}K_{s,\lambda}\widehat{\psi}_{s}+[\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}},\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda}+\epsilon_{0}B_{s}]\widehat{\psi}_{s}
.

It is straightforward to show that

\|[\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}},\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda}+\epsilon_{0}B_{s}]\widehat{\psi}_{s}\|_{H^{N_{+}}_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}}\leq C\|\widehat{\psi}_{s}\|_{H^{N_{+}+1}_{\sigma,R_{\infty},2}}.

Hence, we can proceed as above to conclude that

\|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\widehat{\psi}_{s}\|_{H^{N_{+}+1}_{\sigma,R_{\infty},2}}\leq\|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}K_{s,\lambda}\psi_{s}\|_{H^{N_{+}}_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}}+\|[\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}},\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda}+\epsilon_{0}B_{s}]\psi_{s}\|_{H^{N_{+}}_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}}\leq C\|\psi_{s}\|_{H^{N_{+}+1}_{\sigma,R_{\infty},2}}<\infty.

Hence,
\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\widehat{\psi}_{s}\in H^{N_{+}+1}_{\sigma,R_{\infty},2}
. By repeating this procedure for
\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}^{k}\widehat{\psi}_{s}
, we conclude in particular that

(16.2)

\|\widehat{\psi}_{s}\|_{H^{N_{+}+1}(\hat{\Sigma})}+\|\psi_{s}\|_{H^{N_{+}+1}_{\sigma,R_{\infty},2}}\leq C\|\psi_{s}\|_{H^{N_{+}+1}_{\sigma,R_{\infty},2}}

so
\widehat{\psi}_{s}\in H^{N_{+}+1}_{\sigma,R_{\infty},2}\cap H^{N_{+}+1}(\hat{\Sigma})
, and in fact
\widehat{\psi}_{s}\in H^{\tilde{N}_{+}+1}_{\sigma,R_{\infty},2}\cap H^{\tilde{N}_{+}+1}(\hat{\Sigma})
for all
\tilde{N}_{+}\geq N_{+}
, so
\widehat{\psi}_{s}\in C^{\infty}(\hat{\Sigma})\cap H^{N_{+}+1}_{\sigma,R_{\infty},2}
. To conclude that
\ker(\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa}+\epsilon_{0}B_{s}+K_{s,\lambda})\subset C^{\infty}(\hat{\Sigma})
, we simply repeat the above arguments with
\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa}
replacing
\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda}
.

We now turn to (ii). Since
\widehat{\psi}_{s}\in C^{\infty}(\hat{\Sigma})
, it follows immediately that for
\widehat{\psi}_{s}\in\ker L_{s}\subseteq H^{N_{+}+1}_{\sigma,R_{\infty},2}
with
\tilde{R}_{\infty}\geq R_{\infty}
,
\widehat{\psi}_{s}\in H^{N_{+}+1}_{\sigma,\tilde{R}_{\infty},2}
. It remains to show that
\widehat{\psi}_{s}\in\ker L_{s}\subseteq H^{N_{+}+1}_{\sigma,\tilde{R}_{\infty},2}
implies that
\widehat{\psi}_{s}\in H^{N_{+}+1}_{\sigma,R_{\infty},2}
. For this, we will apply Lemma
16.3
with
Q\equiv 0
, which says that there exists a sequence
\{\kappa_{n}\}
in
(0,\infty)
and
s_{n}\in\Omega_{\sigma}\cap\{\re(z)>-(\frac{1}{2}+N_{+})\kappa_{+}\}
with
\kappa_{n}\downarrow 0
as
n\to\infty
, and there exists a sequence
\psi_{s_{n}}\in\ker(\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa_{n}}+\epsilon B_{s}+K_{s,\lambda})
such that

\|\widehat{\psi}_{s_{n}}-\widehat{\psi}_{s}\|_{H_{\sigma,\tilde{R}_{\infty}}^{N_{+}}}\to 0

as
n\to\infty
.

Observe that

(\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa_{n}}+\epsilon_{0}B_{s})\widehat{\psi}_{s_{n}}=-K_{s,\lambda}\widehat{\psi}_{s_{n}}.

Since any estimates involving
\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa_{n}}
apply with
H^{N_{+}+1}_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}
and
H^{N_{+}+1}_{\sigma,R_{\infty},2}
norms where the infinite sums
\sum_{n=\ldots}^{\infty}
are replaced with sums
\sum_{n=\ldots}^{N_{\infty}}
with
N_{\infty}<\infty
suitably large depending on
\kappa_{n}
, we can first apply the estimates with finite
N_{\infty}
, using that
\widehat{\psi}_{s_{n}},\widehat{\psi}_{s_{m}}\in C^{\infty}(\hat{\Sigma})
, and subsequently take the limit
N_{\infty}\to\infty
to obtain: there exists an
N>0
(independent of
n
) such that

\|\widehat{\psi}_{s_{n}}\|_{H^{N_{+}+1}_{\sigma,R_{\infty},2}}\leq C\|\widehat{\psi}_{s_{n}}\|_{H^{N}(\hat{\Sigma})},

where we moreover used that all sufficiently higher-order derivatives can appearing on the right-hand side of the
N_{\infty}<\infty
estimates can be absorbed to the left-hand side, leaving only lower-order derivatives on the right-hand side. For
N_{+}
suitably large depending on
N
, we obtain from (
16.2
) applied to the
\kappa>0
setting with
\tilde{R}_{\infty}
replacing
R_{\infty}
:

\|\widehat{\psi}_{s_{n}}\|_{H^{N_{+}+1}_{\sigma,R_{\infty},2}}+\|\widehat{\psi}_{s_{n}}\|_{H^{N_{+}+1}(\hat{\Sigma})}\leq C\|\widehat{\psi}_{s_{n}}\|_{H^{N_{+}+1}_{\sigma,\tilde{R}_{\infty},2}}.

In particular,
\{\widehat{\psi}_{s_{n}}\}
is a bounded sequence in
H^{N_{+}+1}_{\sigma,R_{\infty},2}\cap H^{N_{+}+1}(\hat{\Sigma})
, with respect to the norm
\|\cdot \|_{H^{N_{+}+1}_{\sigma,R_{\infty},2}}+\|\cdot\|_{H^{N_{+}+1}(\hat{\Sigma})}
so it admits a weakly convergent subsequence. Since
H^{N_{+}+1}_{\sigma,R_{\infty},2}\cap H^{N_{+}+1}(\hat{\Sigma})\subset H^{N_{+}+1}_{\sigma,\tilde{R}_{\infty},2}
and
\widehat{\psi}_{s_{n}}
converges strongly to
\psi_{s}
with respect to
\|\cdot\|_{H^{N_{+}+1}_{\sigma,\tilde{R}_{\infty},2}}
, we can conclude by uniqueness of limits that
\widehat{\psi}_{s}\in H^{N_{+}+1}_{\sigma,\tilde{R}_{\infty},2}
.

We therefore conclude that
\mathscr{Q}_{\sigma,\widetilde{R}_{\infty},N_{+}}=\mathscr{Q}_{\sigma,R_{\infty},N_{+}}
.

Finally, consider (iii). First of all, we have that
H^{N_{+}+1}_{\tilde{\sigma},R_{\infty},2}\subset H^{N_{+}+1}_{\sigma,R_{\infty},2}
, so if
\psi_{s}\in\ker L_{s}
with respect to
H^{N_{+}}_{\tilde{\sigma},R_{\infty}}
, then
\psi_{s}\in\ker L_{s}
with respect to
H^{N_{+}}_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}
.

Conversely, suppose that
\psi_{s}\in\ker L_{s}
with respect to
H^{N_{+}}_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}
. Suppose also that
s\in\Omega_{\sigma^{\prime}}
. As above, we can find a sequence
\{\kappa_{n}\}
in
(0,\infty)
,
s_{n}\in\Omega_{\sigma}\cap\Omega_{\sigma^{\prime}}\cap\{\re(z)>-(\frac{1}{2}+N_{+})\kappa_{+}\}
with
\kappa_{n}\downarrow 0
as
n\to\infty
and
\psi_{s_{n}}\in\ker(\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa_{n}}+\epsilon B_{s}+K_{s,\lambda})
with

\|\widehat{\psi}_{s_{n}}-\widehat{\psi}_{s}\|_{H_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}^{N_{+}}}\to 0.

We use the same strategy as before: we apply the
N_{\infty}<\infty
estimates with respect to
H^{N_{+}+1}_{\sigma^{\prime},R_{\infty}}
to
(\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa_{n}}+\epsilon_{0}B_{s})\widehat{\psi}_{s_{n}}
and use that any higher-order derivatives can be absorbed into the left-hand side, leaving

\|\widehat{\psi}_{s_{n}}\|_{H^{N_{+}+1}_{\sigma,R_{\infty},2}}\leq C\|\widehat{\psi}_{s_{n}}\|_{H^{N}(\hat{\Sigma}\cap\{x\leq x_{0}\})}\leq\|\widehat{\psi}_{s_{n}}\|_{H^{N_{+}+1}_{\sigma,R_{\infty},2}}.

Hence,
\{\widehat{\psi}_{s_{n}}\}
admits a weakly convergent subsequence with limit in
H^{N_{+}+1}_{\sigma^{\prime},R_{\infty},2}
. Since
H^{N_{+}+1}_{\sigma^{\prime},R_{\infty},2}\subset H^{N_{+}+1}_{\sigma,R_{\infty},2}
and uniqueness of limits holds, we conclude that
\psi_{s}\in H^{N_{+}+1}_{\sigma^{\prime},R_{\infty},2}
. Hence,
s
is an element of
\ker L_{s}
with respect to
H^{N_{+}+1}_{\sigma^{\prime},R_{\infty},2}
, from which (iii) follows.
∎

17.
Stability of the quasinormal spectrum

Recall the definition of the stability of the spectrum of a family of operators in Definition
10.3
. The following proposition forms the main ingredient for the stability results in Theorem
10.3
.

Proposition 17.1
.

Consider the operator
L_{s}+\delta\cdot\widetilde{Q}:H^{N_{+}}_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}(L_{s})\to H^{N_{+}}_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}
, with
\widetilde{Q}:H^{N_{+}}_{\sigma,R_{\infty},2}\to H^{N_{+}}_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}
bounded for all
|\sigma|<\sigma_{0}
, with
\sigma_{0}>0
or
\sigma_{0}=\infty
. Let
\epsilon>0
and
D\subset\Omega_{\sigma}
. Then for all
\epsilon>0
and
K\subset\Omega_{\sigma}
compact, there exists a
\delta_{0}>0
such that for all
0<\delta\leq\delta_{0}
:

(i)

For
|\sigma|<\sigma_{0}
, the family
s\mapsto(L_{s}+\delta\cdot\widetilde{Q})^{-1}:H^{N_{+}}_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}\to H^{N_{+}}_{\sigma,R_{\infty},2}
is meromorphic on
K
and holomorphic on
K\setminus\bigcup_{z\in\mathscr{Q}_{\rm reg}}B_{\delta}(z)\cap K
.

(ii)

For all
z\in\mathscr{Q}_{\rm reg}\cap B_{\sigma_{0}^{2}}
, there exists an
s\in B_{\delta}(z)
, such that
s
is a pole of
s\mapsto(L_{s}+\delta\cdot\widetilde{Q})^{-1}
.

Proof.

Part (i) follows from the fact that for
s\in K\setminus(\bigcup_{z\in\mathscr{Q}_{\rm reg}}B_{\delta}(z)\cap K)
, we can write.

L_{s}+\delta\cdot\widetilde{Q}=(1+\delta\cdot\widetilde{Q}L_{s}^{-1})L_{s}

and for suitably small
\delta
,
\|\widetilde{Q}L_{s}^{-1}\|_{H^{N_{+}}_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}\to H^{N_{+}}_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}}<1
for all
s\in K\setminus(\bigcup_{z\in\mathscr{Q}_{\rm reg}}B_{\delta}(z)\cap K)
, provided
|\sigma|<\sigma_{0}
, so

(L_{s}+\delta\cdot\widetilde{Q})^{-1}=L_{s}^{-1}(1+\delta\cdot QL_{s}^{-1})^{-1}

is well-defined.

Part (ii) follows directly from Lemma
16.3
with
\kappa_{n}=0
and the observation that for
|\sigma|<\sigma_{0}
we have that

\bigcup_{\sigma\in(-\sigma_{0},\sigma_{0})}\Omega_{\sigma}=\Omega\cap B_{\sigma_{0}^{2}}.\qed

18.
Kerr–de Sitter quasinormal modes

Let
(\mathcal{M}_{M,a,\Lambda},g_{M,a,\Lambda})
be Kerr de Sitter spacetimes with mass
M
, angular momentum/mass
a
and cosmological constant
\Lambda
, such that
\kappa_{+}
remains uniformly bounded away from zero as
\Lambda\downarrow 0
. We denote by
r_{+}
the radius of the event horizon, by
r_{c}
the radius of the cosmological horizon and by
\kappa_{c}
the surface gravity of the cosmological horizon. Then it can easily be verified that:

\kappa_{c}\sim\sqrt{\Lambda}\sim r_{c}^{-1}.

The wave operator
\square_{g_{M,a,\Lambda}}
takes the following form with respect to Boyer–Linquist coordinates
(t,r,\theta,\varphi)
:

\rho^{2}\square_{g_{M,a,\Lambda}}\phi=\partial_{r}(\Delta_{r}\partial_{r}\phi)-\Xi^{2}\left[\frac{(r^{2}+a^{2})^{2}}{\Delta_{r}}-\frac{a^{2}\sin^{2}\theta}{\Delta_{\theta}}\right]\partial_{t}^{2}\phi-2a\frac{\Xi^{2}}{\Delta_{r}}\left[(r^{2}+a^{2})-\frac{\Delta_{r}}{\Delta_{\theta}}\right]\partial_{t}\partial_{\varphi}\phi\\
+\frac{1}{\sin\theta}\partial_{\theta}(\Delta_{\theta}\sin\theta\partial_{\theta}\phi)+\Xi^{2}\left(\frac{1}{\Delta_{\theta}\sin^{2}\theta}-\frac{a^{2}}{\Delta_{r}}\right)\partial_{\varphi}^{2}\phi,

with

\displaystyle\rho^{2}=

\displaystyle\>r^{2}+a^{2}\cos^{2}\theta,

\displaystyle\Delta_{r}=

\displaystyle\>(r^{2}+a^{2})\left(1-\frac{\Lambda}{3}r^{2}\right)-2Mr,

\displaystyle\Delta_{\theta}=

\displaystyle\>1+\frac{\Lambda a^{2}}{3}\cos^{2}\theta,

\displaystyle\Xi=

\displaystyle\>1+\frac{\Lambda a^{2}}{3}.

Furthermore,
\kappa_{c}=-\frac{\Xi}{2(r_{c}^{2}+a^{2})}\frac{d}{dr}\Delta_{r}|_{r=r_{c}}
. By using that
\Delta(r_{c})=0
, we moreover obtain:

(18.1)

0\leq 1-\frac{\Lambda}{3r_{c}^{2}}=\frac{2M}{1+a^{2}r_{c}^{-2}}r_{c}^{-1}\leq 2Mr_{c}^{-1}.

With respect to the vector fields

\displaystyle T=

\displaystyle\>\Xi\partial_{t},

\displaystyle\Phi=

\displaystyle\>\Xi\partial_{\varphi},

\displaystyle Y=

\displaystyle\partial_{r}-\frac{r^{2}+a^{2}}{\Delta_{r}}\Xi\partial_{t}-\frac{a}{\Delta_{r}}\Xi\partial_{\varphi},

we can express:

\rho^{2}\square_{g_{M,a,\Lambda}}\phi=Y(\Delta_{r}Y\phi)+2aY\Phi\phi+\frac{1}{\sin\theta}\partial_{\theta}(\Delta_{\theta}\sin\theta\partial_{\theta}\phi)+\frac{1}{\Delta_{\theta}\sin^{2}\theta}\Phi^{2}\phi\\
+2(r^{2}+a^{2})YT\phi+2rT\phi+\frac{1}{\Delta_{\theta}}a^{2}\sin^{2}\theta T^{2}\phi+2a\frac{1}{\Delta_{\theta}}T\Phi\phi.

Define
v=\Xi^{-1}t+r_{*}
with
\frac{dr_{*}}{dr}(r)=\frac{r^{2}+a^{2}}{\Delta_{r}}
and
\varphi_{*}=\Xi^{-1}\varphi+\int_{r_{0}}^{r}\frac{a}{\Delta_{r}}\mod 2\pi
. With respect to
(v,r,\theta,\varphi_{*})
coordinates, we have that
T=\partial_{v}
,
\Phi=\partial_{\varphi_{*}}
and
Y=\partial_{r}
.

In a region where
r-r_{c}
is suitably small, we will view
\square_{g_{M,a,\Lambda}}\phi
as a perturbation of
\square_{g_{0,a,\Lambda_{c}}}\phi
, which is the de Sitter wave operator with cosmological constant
\Lambda_{c}=\frac{3}{r_{c}^{2}}
. We denote
\mathring{\Delta}_{\theta}=\frac{\Lambda_{c}a^{2}}{3}\cos^{2}\theta
and
\mathring{\Delta}_{r}=(r^{2}+a^{2})\left(1-\frac{\Lambda_{c}}{3}r^{2}\right)
, which correspond to the expressions for
\Delta_{r}
and
\Delta_{\theta}
, respectively, in the case
M=0
and
\Lambda=\Lambda_{c}
.

Note that we can estimate

\left|\Delta_{\theta}-\mathring{\Delta}_{\theta}\right|=a^{2}\left|\frac{\Lambda}{3}-r_{c}^{-2}\right|\cos^{2}\theta\leq Ca^{2}\kappa_{c}^{2}.

Then

\rho^{2}\square_{g_{M,a,\Lambda}}\phi=\rho^{2}\square_{g_{0,a,\Lambda_{c}}}\phi+Y((\Delta_{r}-\mathring{\Delta}_{r})Y\phi)+\frac{1}{\sin\theta}\partial_{\theta}((\Delta_{\theta}-\mathring{\Delta}_{\theta})\sin\theta\partial_{\theta}\phi)\\
+(\Delta_{\theta}^{-1}-\mathring{\Delta}_{\theta}^{-1})\left(\frac{1}{\sin^{2}\theta}\Phi^{2}\phi+a^{2}\sin^{2}\theta T^{2}\phi+2aT\Phi\phi\right).

Consider the following coordinate transformations in the region
\{r\geq\frac{R_{\infty}}{\sqrt{2}}\}
with
r_{+}<R_{\infty}<r_{c}
:

\displaystyle\varrho(r,\theta)=

\displaystyle\>\sqrt{\frac{1}{1+a^{2}r_{c}^{-2}}(r^{2}\mathring{\Delta}_{\theta}+a^{2}\sin^{2}\theta)},

\displaystyle\tan\vartheta=

\displaystyle\>\sqrt{\frac{1+a^{2}r^{-2}}{1+a^{2}r_{c}^{-2}}}\tan\theta,

\displaystyle\tilde{\varphi}=

\displaystyle\>\varphi-\frac{a}{r_{c}^{2}}t.

Note that

(18.2)

\displaystyle\varrho(r,\theta)=\>

\displaystyle r+\frac{a^{2}}{r_{c}^{2}+a^{2}}\sin^{2}\theta(r_{c}-r)+r^{-1}O_{1}((r_{c}-r)^{2}),

(18.3)

\displaystyle\vartheta(r,\theta)=\>

\displaystyle\theta+\frac{a^{2}}{r^{2}}O_{1}(r_{c}-r).

The coordinates
(t,\varrho,\vartheta,\tilde{\varphi})
correspond to standard static coordinates on de Sitter with cosmological constant
\Lambda_{c}
(see for example
[
GV17
]
[§V]). That is to say, we can write:

g_{0,a,\Lambda_{c}}=-\left(1-\Lambda_{c}\varrho^{2}\right)dt^{2}+\left(1-\Lambda_{c}\varrho^{2}\right)^{-1}d\varrho^{2}+\varrho^{2}(d\vartheta^{2}+\sin^{2}\vartheta d\tilde{\varphi}^{2}).

Let
\tilde{u}(t,\varrho)=t-\int_{R_{\infty}}^{\varrho}\frac{r_{c}^{2}}{r_{c}^{2}-\rho^{\prime 2}}\,d\varrho^{\prime}
. Let
T=\partial_{\tilde{u}}
and
X=\partial_{\varrho}
. Then we can express:

\square_{g_{0,a,\Lambda_{c}}}\phi=r_{c}^{-2}\varrho^{-2}X(\varrho^{2}(r_{c}^{2}-\varrho^{2})X\phi)+\varrho^{-2}\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2},\vartheta}\phi-2\varrho^{-1}XT(\varrho\phi).

Let
\psi=\varrho\phi
. Then

\varrho^{3}\left(\square_{g_{0,a,\Lambda_{c}}}\phi-\frac{2}{r_{c}^{2}}\phi\right)=\varrho^{2}X((1-r_{c}^{-2}\varrho^{2})X\psi)+\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2},\vartheta}\psi-2\varrho^{2}XT\psi.

Let
x=\varrho^{-1}-r_{c}^{-1}
, so that
\partial_{x}=-\rho^{2}X
.

We then obtain:

\varrho^{3}\left(\square_{g_{0,a,\Lambda_{c}}}\phi-\frac{2}{r_{c}^{2}}\phi\right)=\partial_{x}(x(x+2r_{c}^{-1})\partial_{x}\psi)+\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2},\vartheta}\psi+2\partial_{x}T\psi.

We now extend the functions
\varrho
,
\vartheta
,
\tilde{\varphi}
to
\{r\geq r_{+}\}
, such that
\varrho=r
,
\vartheta=\theta
and
\tilde{\varphi}=\varphi
for
r\leq\frac{R_{\infty}}{2}
. We consider the following time function:

\tau(t,\varrho)=\Xi^{-1}t+\int_{r_{0}}^{r}\frac{(\varrho^{2}+a^{2})(1+\mathbbm{h}(\varrho^{\prime}))}{\Delta_{r}(\varrho^{\prime})}\,d\varrho^{\prime}.

We consider
\mathbbm{h}
such that
\tau
are smooth, spacelike hypersurfaces that intersect
\mathcal{H}^{+}
and
\mathcal{C}^{+}
to the future of the bifurcation spheres. We moreover demand that the
\tau
-level sets are analytic in a neighbourhood of
\mathcal{C}^{+}
and that
\mathbbm{h}
and its derivatives can be uniformly bounded as
\Lambda\downarrow 0
.

We then consider the coordinate chart
(\tau,x=\varrho^{-1}-r_{c}^{-1},\vartheta,\tilde{\varphi})
.

Proposition 18.1
.

The conformal wave operator on Kerr–de Sitter can be decomposed as follows:

\varrho^{3}\left(\square_{g_{M,a,\Lambda}}\phi-\frac{2}{3}\Lambda\phi\right)=\partial_{x}(x(2\kappa_{c}+x)\partial_{x}\psi)+\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2},\vartheta}\psi-2\partial_{x}T\psi+\epsilon_{0}\mathcal{B}_{\Lambda}^{\infty}+\mathcal{K}_{\Lambda}^{\infty}+\kappa_{c}^{2}\mathcal{B}_{\Lambda}^{\mathcal{C}^{+}},

where the coefficients of the differential operators
\mathcal{B}_{\Lambda}^{\infty}
,
\mathcal{K}_{\Lambda}^{\infty}
and
\mathcal{B}_{\Lambda}^{\mathcal{C}^{+}}
stay bounded as
\Lambda\downarrow 0
. Furthermore,
\mathcal{B}_{\Lambda}^{\infty}
and
\mathcal{K}_{\Lambda}^{\infty}
have the same form as the operators
\mathcal{B}^{\infty}
and
\mathcal{K}^{\infty}
from Lemma
7.1
and
\mathcal{B}_{\Lambda}^{\mathcal{C}^{+}}
can contain up to second-order angular derivatives and
\tau
-derivatives, up to first order
x
-derivatives and second-order
x
-derivatives of the form
\kappa_{c}x\partial_{x}^{2}
. The constant
\epsilon_{0}>0
can be chosen arbitrarily small for
M^{-1}R_{\infty}
suitably large.

Proof.

The decomposition follows by applying Lemma
7.1
(the
\Lambda=0
) case, combined with the observation that additional terms that vanish when
\Lambda=0
contain a factor of
\Lambda
and hence can be written as follows
\kappa_{c}^{2}\mathcal{B}_{\Lambda}^{\mathcal{C}^{+}}
, where
\mathcal{B}_{\Lambda}^{\mathcal{C}^{+}}
is a second-order operator with coefficients that stay bounded as
\Lambda\downarrow 0
.

We will not derive the precise form of
\mathcal{B}_{\Lambda}^{\infty}
,
\mathcal{K}_{\Lambda}^{\infty}
and
\mathcal{B}_{\Lambda}^{\mathcal{C}^{+}}
in detail, but will will include a schematic version of the key relevant computation.

By applying (
18.2
) and (
18.3
), we can write:

\varrho^{3}\rho^{-2}Y((\Delta_{r}-\mathring{\Delta}_{r})Y\phi)=\varrho^{3}\rho^{-2}Y((\Delta_{r}-\mathring{\Delta}_{r})Y(\varrho^{-1}\psi))\\
=\varrho Y(\varrho^{-1}(\Delta_{r}-\mathring{\Delta}_{r})Y\psi)-\varrho Y(\varrho^{-2}(\Delta_{r}-\mathring{\Delta}_{r})\psi)\\
=\frac{d}{dr}(\Delta_{r}-\mathring{\Delta}_{r})\left(X\psi-\varrho^{-1}\psi\right)+\ldots\\
=-2(\kappa_{c}-r_{c}^{-1})r_{c}^{2}X\psi+\frac{d}{dr}\left(\frac{\Lambda-\Lambda_{c}}{3}r^{2}(r^{2}+a^{2})+2Mr\right)\varrho^{-1}\psi+\ldots\\
=+2(\kappa_{c}-r_{c}^{-1})\partial_{x}\psi+\frac{4}{3}(\Lambda-3r_{c}^{-2})r_{c}^{2}\psi+\ldots,

where the terms in
\ldots
are part of
\epsilon_{0}\mathcal{B}_{\Lambda}^{\infty}
,
\mathcal{K}_{\Lambda}^{\infty}
and
\kappa_{c}^{2}\mathcal{B}_{\Lambda}^{\mathcal{C}^{+}}
, for
M^{2}\Lambda
suitably small depending on
\epsilon_{0}
.

Note moreover that by (
18.1
):

\displaystyle\frac{4}{3}\left(\Lambda-3r_{c}^{-2}\right)=r_{c}^{-2}(\Lambda r_{c}^{-2}-3)=MO(r_{c}^{-3}),

\displaystyle\frac{2}{3}\Lambda_{c}r_{c}^{2}\psi=\frac{2}{3}\Lambda r_{c}^{2}\psi+MO(r_{c}^{-1})\psi.

∎

Denote
L_{s,\Lambda}:=\varrho^{3}\left(\square_{g_{M,a,\Lambda}}-\frac{2}{3}\Lambda\right)(\varrho^{-1}\cdot)
,
B_{s,\Lambda}^{\infty}=e^{-s\tau}\mathcal{B}_{\Lambda}^{\infty}(e^{s\tau}(\cdot))
,
K_{s,\Lambda}^{\infty}=e^{-s\tau}\mathcal{K}_{\Lambda}^{\infty}(e^{s\tau}(\cdot))
and
B_{s,\Lambda}^{\mathcal{C}^{+}}=e^{-s\tau}\mathcal{B}_{\Lambda}^{\mathcal{C}^{+}}(e^{s\tau}(\cdot))
. Then

L_{s,\Lambda}=\partial_{x}(x(x+2\kappa_{c})\partial_{x}\psi)+\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2},\vartheta}\psi-2s\partial_{x}\psi+MxB_{s,\Lambda}^{\infty}+K_{s,\Lambda}^{\infty}+\kappa_{c}^{2}B_{s,\Lambda}^{\mathcal{C}^{+}}.

Proposition 18.2
.

We can decompose:

L_{s,\Lambda}=\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,2\kappa_{c}}+\epsilon_{0}\cdot B_{s,\Lambda}+\kappa_{c}^{2}B_{s,\Lambda}^{\mathcal{C}^{+}}+K_{s,\lambda,\Lambda},

where

(18.4)

\displaystyle r^{-2}\rho^{2}\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa_{c}}=

\displaystyle\>r\partial_{r}(\Delta_{r}\partial_{r}(r^{-1}\cdot))+2ar\partial_{r}\partial_{\varphi_{*}}(r^{-1}\cdot)+\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(\cdot)

\displaystyle\>+sr(4Mr-2(1-\mathbbm{h})\Delta_{r})\partial_{r}(r^{-1}\cdot)-\lambda\pi_{\leq l}\quad\textnormal{for}\quad r=\varrho\leq\frac{R_{\infty}}{2},

(18.5)

\displaystyle(\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,2\kappa_{c}}f)_{\ell}=

\displaystyle\>\partial_{x}(x(2\kappa_{c}+x)\partial_{x}f_{\ell})+2s\partial_{x}f_{\ell}-\mathfrak{l}(\mathfrak{l}+1)f_{\ell}\quad\textnormal{for}\quad\varrho\geq R_{\infty}.

Furthermore:

(i)

There exists a constant
C_{s,\sigma,R_{\infty}}>0
such that:

\|\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,2\kappa_{c}}^{-1}\|_{H_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}^{N_{+}}\to H_{\sigma,R_{\infty},2}^{N_{+}+1}}\leq C_{s,\sigma,R_{\infty}}.

For all
f\in H_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}^{N_{+}}
we have additionally that

\kappa_{c}\|x\partial_{x}^{2}\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,2\kappa_{c}}^{-1}(f)\|_{G_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}}+\kappa_{c}\|\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa}^{-1}(f)\|_{G_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}}\leq C_{s,\sigma}\|f\|_{H_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}^{N_{+}}}.

(ii)

For
M^{2}\Lambda
suitably small,

B_{s,\Lambda}\circ\mathfrak{L}_{s,2\kappa_{c},\lambda}^{-1}:H^{N_{+}}_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}\to H^{N_{+}}_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}
and
\kappa_{c}B_{s}^{\mathcal{C}^{+}}\circ\mathfrak{L}_{s,2\kappa_{c},\lambda}^{-1}:H^{N_{+}}_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}\to H^{N_{+}}_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}
are bounded operators and
K_{s,\lambda,\Lambda}\circ\mathfrak{L}_{s,2\kappa_{c},\lambda}^{-1}:H^{N_{+}}_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}\to H^{N_{+}}_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}
is compact.

Proof.

The statement in (i) follows from an analogue of Corollary
15.2
. Due to the presence of the factor
\Delta_{r}
instead of
\Delta
in (
18.4
), we cannot directly apply Corollary
15.2
. Instead we need to prove an analogue of Corollary
12.3
for
\Delta
replaced with
\Delta_{r}
. Since the surface gravity
\kappa_{+}
of
\mathcal{H}^{+}
is still positive, by assumption, and away from
r=r_{+}
the difference
\Delta_{r}-\Delta
can be made arbitrarily small in
\{r\leq\frac{1}{2}R_{\infty}\}
for
M^{2}\Lambda
suitably small, the red-shift estimates in Corollary
12.3
still hold.

The statement in (ii) then follows straightforwardly by repeating the proof of Proposition
16.1
. The only difference is the presence of the operator
\kappa_{c}B_{s}^{\mathcal{C}^{+}}
, whose boundedness follows from the estimates in (i) that feature
\kappa_{c}
-dependent terms on the left-hand side.
∎

We then directly obtain the following corollary:

Corollary 18.3
.

The conclusions of Propositions
16.2
–
16.4
hold also for
L_{s,2\kappa_{c}}
replacing
L_{s,\kappa}
.

19.
Scattering resonances

In this section, we will establish meromorphicity of cut-off resolvents associated to the Boyer–Lindquist time coordinate
t
.

Let
R_{\infty}>0
be arbitrarily large. Let
\chi_{\infty}\in C^{\infty}(\Sigma)
, such that
\textnormal{supp}\,\chi_{\infty}\subseteq[r_{+},r_{\rm max}]
for some arbitrary
r_{\rm max}<\frac{1}{2}R_{\infty}
.

We first show that we can simplify the domain of
L_{s}^{-1}
by considering
\chi_{\infty}L_{s}^{-1}\chi_{\infty}
.

Corollary 19.1
.

The cut-off operators
L_{s}^{-1}\chi_{\infty}
and
\chi_{\infty}L_{s}^{-1}\chi_{\infty}
satisfy the following properties:

(i)

Let
N_{+}\in\mathbb{N}_{+}
and
\delta_{0}>0
. Then there exists a
\sigma_{0}(\delta_{0})\in\mathbb{R}
such that
L_{s}^{-1}\chi_{\infty}:H^{N_{+}}_{\not{\nabla}}(\Sigma)\to H^{N_{+}+1}_{\sigma_{0},R_{\infty},2}
defines a meromorphic family of operators in
\{|\arg z|<\frac{2}{3}\pi\}\cap\{\re z>-(\frac{1}{2}+N_{+})\kappa_{+}\}\cap\{|z|\geq\delta_{0}\}
, with poles that lie in
\mathscr{Q}_{\rm reg}
.

(ii)

\chi_{\infty}L_{s}^{-1}\chi_{\infty}:H^{N_{+}}_{\not{\nabla}}(\Sigma)\to H^{N_{+}+1}_{\not{\nabla}}(\Sigma)
defines a meromorphic family of operators for
s\in\{|\arg z|<\frac{2}{3}\pi\}\cap\{\re s>-(\frac{1}{2}+N_{+})\kappa_{+}\}
, with poles that lie in
\mathscr{Q}_{\rm reg}
.

(iii)

\chi_{\infty}L_{s}^{-1}\chi_{\infty}:C^{\infty}(\Sigma)\to C^{\infty}(\Sigma)
is well-defined for all
s\in\Omega\setminus\mathscr{Q}_{\rm reg}
.

Proof.

Let
s\in\Omega\setminus\mathscr{Q}_{\rm reg}
. By Proposition
16.2
, there exists an
N_{+}
such that
\re s>-(\frac{1}{2}+N_{+})\kappa_{+}
, so
L_{s}^{-1}:H_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}^{N}\to H_{\sigma,R_{\infty},2}^{N+1}
is well-defined for all
N\geq N_{+}
and for an appropriate choice of
\sigma
. Let
f\in H^{N_{+}}_{\not{\nabla}}(\Sigma)
. Then
\chi_{\infty}f\in H_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}^{N_{+}}
for all
\sigma\in\mathbb{R}
and
L_{s}^{-1}(\chi_{\infty}f)\in H_{\sigma,R_{\infty},2}^{N+1}
for all
\sigma
such that
s\in\Omega_{\sigma}
. Since
H_{\sigma,R_{\infty},2}^{N+1}\subset H_{\sigma^{\prime},R_{\infty},2}^{N+1}
, for all
\sigma\geq\sigma^{\prime}
, we obtain (i) by taking
\sigma_{0}
to be the minimum of
\sigma
, such that
\Omega^{2}_{\sigma}
cover the region
\{|\arg z|<\frac{2}{3}\pi\}\cap\{\re z>-(\frac{1}{2}+N_{+})\kappa_{+}\}\cap\{|z|\geq\delta_{0}\}
. The above implies moreover that
\chi_{\infty}L_{s}^{-1}(\chi_{\infty}f)\in H^{N_{+}+1}_{\not{\nabla}}(\Sigma)
, so (ii) also follows.

In particular, if
f\in C^{\infty}(\Sigma)
, then we can take
N_{+}
arbitrarily large in (ii) to conclude (iii).
∎

Now let
\chi_{\infty,+}\in C^{\infty}(\Sigma)
, such that
\textnormal{supp}\,\chi_{\infty,+}\subseteq[r_{\rm min},r_{\rm max}]
, for some arbitrary
r_{\min}>r_{+}
. Then can moreover obtain further meromorphicity results in the full set
\Omega
, in the case
\frac{|a|}{M}\ll 1
or for bounded azimuthal frequencies
m
.

Proposition 19.2
.

The operator
\chi_{\infty}L_{s}^{-1}\chi_{\infty,+}:\pi_{|m|\leq m_{0}}(L^{2}(\Sigma))\to\pi_{|m|\leq m_{0}}(H^{2}(\Sigma))
is well-defined for all
s\in\Omega\setminus\mathscr{Q}_{\rm reg}
and defines a meromorphic family of operators on
\Omega
with poles that lie in
\mathscr{Q}_{\rm reg}
. Furthermore, for
\frac{|a|}{|M|}
suitably small,
\chi_{\infty}L_{s}^{-1}\chi_{\infty,+}:L^{2}(\Sigma)\to H^{2}(\Sigma)
is well-defined and defines a meromorphic family of operators on
\Omega
with poles that lie in
\mathscr{Q}_{\rm reg}
. In both cases, there exists a constant
C>0
, with
C=C(a,r_{\rm min},r_{\rm max},R_{\infty},s)>0
or
C=C(m_{0},r_{\rm min},r_{\rm max},R_{\infty},s)>0
such that

\|\chi_{\infty}L_{s}^{-1}(\chi_{\infty,+}f)\|_{H^{2}(\Sigma)}\leq C_{s}\|f\|_{L^{2}(\Sigma)}.

Proof.

For
\frac{|a|}{|M|}
suitably small or when restricting to the span of azimuthal modes with
|m|\leq m_{0}
, we can omit the angular derivatives
|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}\partial_{r}^{k}\mathfrak{L}_{s,\lambda,\kappa}\widehat{\psi}|^{2}
on the right-hand side of (
12.10
) by applying Proposition
12.4
to obtain the following improved version of Corollary
19.1
(i): for
s\notin\mathscr{Q}_{\rm reg}

(19.1)

\|\chi_{\infty}L_{s}^{-1}(\chi_{\infty,+}f)\|_{H^{N+1}(\Sigma)}+\left|\left|\left(1-\frac{r_{+}}{r}\right)\chi_{\infty}L_{s}^{-1}(\chi_{\infty,+}f)\right|\right|_{H^{N+2}(\Sigma)}\leq C_{s}\|f\|_{H^{N}(\Sigma)}.

It is straightforward to improve (
19.1
) in the region
r\leq r_{\rm min}
by applying the estimates in the proof of Corollary
12.3
, but restricted to
r\in[r_{+},r_{\rm min}]
. It then follows that

(19.2)

\|\chi_{\infty}L_{s}^{-1}(\chi_{\infty,+}f)\|_{H^{N_{+}+2}(\Sigma)}\leq C\|f\|_{H^{N}_{+}(\Sigma)}+C\|\partial_{r}^{N_{+}+1}(\chi_{\infty,+}f)\|_{L^{2}(\Sigma\cap\{r\leq r_{\rm min}\})}\leq C\|f\|_{H^{N_{+}}(\Sigma)},

where the final inequality follows from the fact that
\chi_{\infty,+}f=0
in
\{r\leq r_{\rm min}\}
.

Suppose that
s\in\Omega
satisfies
\re s>-\frac{1}{2}\kappa_{+}
, then the proposition holds. Suppose now instead that
-\frac{3}{2}\kappa_{+}<\re s\leq-\frac{1}{2}\kappa_{+}
and let
s^{\prime}\in\Omega
be such that
\re s^{\prime}>-\frac{1}{2}\kappa_{+}
.

Let
f\in C^{\infty}(\Sigma)
and define
\widehat{\psi}=L_{s}^{-1}(\chi_{\infty,+}f)
. We can express:

L_{s}^{-1}=L_{s}^{-1}L_{s^{\prime}}L_{s^{\prime}}^{-1}=L_{s}^{-1}(L_{s}+(L_{s^{\prime}}-L_{s}))L_{s^{\prime}}^{-1}=L_{s^{\prime}}^{-1}+L_{s}^{-1}(L_{s^{\prime}}-L_{s})L_{s^{\prime}}^{-1}.

We can now estimate

\|\chi_{\infty}\widehat{\psi}\|_{H^{2}(\Sigma)}=\|\chi_{\infty}L_{s}^{-1}(\chi_{\infty,+}f)\|_{H^{2}(\Sigma)}\\
\leq\|\chi_{\infty}L_{s^{\prime}}^{-1}(\chi_{\infty,+}f)\|_{H^{2}(\Sigma)}+\|\chi_{\infty}L_{s}^{-1}(L_{s^{\prime}}-L_{s})L_{s^{\prime}}^{-1}(\chi_{\infty,+}f)\|_{H^{2}(\Sigma)}\\
\leq C_{s^{\prime},r_{\rm min}}\|f\|_{L^{2}(\Sigma)}+C_{s}\|\chi_{\infty}(L_{s^{\prime}}-L_{s})L_{s^{\prime}}^{-1}(\chi_{\infty,+}f)\||_{H^{1}(\Sigma)}\\
\leq C_{s^{\prime},r_{\rm min}}\|f\|_{L^{2}(\Sigma)}+C_{s}|s-s^{\prime}\||\chi_{\infty}L_{s^{\prime}}^{-1}(\chi_{\infty,+}f)\|_{H^{2}(\Sigma)}\\
\leq C_{s^{\prime},r_{\rm min}}(1+C_{s}|s-s^{\prime}|)\|f\|_{L^{2}(\Sigma)},

where we applied (
19.2
) with
N=0
and Corollary
19.1
(i) with
N_{+}=1
to arrive at the second line, and then we applied (
19.2
) with
N_{+}=0
once again to arrive at the last line. By a density argument, we therefore obtain
\|\chi_{\infty}\widehat{\psi}\|_{H^{2}(\Sigma)}\leq C\|f\|_{L^{2}(\Sigma)}
for all
f\in L^{2}(\Sigma)
.

We can repeat this procedure to conclude that the desired inequality holds for all
s\in\Omega
.
∎

We consider now the differential operator

A(\omega):=e^{i\omega t}\square_{g_{M,a}}(e^{-i\omega t}(\cdot)):C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}_{r}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}_{(\theta,\varphi)})\to C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}_{r}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}_{(\theta,\varphi)}).

We will construct the corresponding resolvent operator
R(\omega)=A(\omega)^{-1}
and the associated cut-off resolvent operator
\chi_{\infty,+}R(\omega)\chi_{\infty,+}
by relating
A(\omega)
to
L_{s}
. Denote
\Sigma^{\prime}:=\{t=0\}
.

Let
Q_{s}:L^{2}(\Sigma^{\prime})\to L^{2}(\Sigma\setminus\{r=r_{+}\})
be defined as follows: let
\overline{f}:D^{+}(\Sigma^{\prime})\to\mathbb{C}
denote the trivial extension of
f\in L^{2}(\Sigma^{\prime})
to
D^{+}(\Sigma^{\prime})
, i.e.
\overline{f}(t,r,\theta,\varphi_{*})=\overline{f}(r,\theta,\varphi_{*})
. Then

Q_{s}(f)(r,\theta,\varphi_{*})=(e^{st}\overline{f})|_{\Sigma}(r,\theta,\varphi^{*}).

Furthermore,
Q_{s}\chi:L^{2}(\Sigma^{\prime})\to L^{2}(\Sigma)
is well-defined and bounded. We consider also the inverse
Q_{s}^{-1}:L^{2}(\Sigma)\to L^{2}(\Sigma^{\prime})
, to obtain the bounded operator:

\chi_{\infty,+}Q_{s}^{-1}:H^{2}(\Sigma)\to H^{2}(\Sigma^{\prime}).

Let
s=-i\omega
, then we can express

A(\omega)=Q_{s}^{-1}\circ(\varrho^{-3}L_{s}(\varrho\cdot))\circ Q_{s}.

so that

\chi_{\infty,+}R(\omega)\chi_{\infty,+}=(\chi_{\infty,+}Q_{s}^{-1})\circ(\varrho^{-1}L_{s}(\varrho^{3}\cdot))^{-1}\circ(Q_{s}\chi_{\infty,+}).

Corollary 19.3
.

The cut-off resolvent operator:

\chi_{\infty,+}R(\omega)\chi_{\infty,+}:H^{N_{+}}_{\not{\nabla}}(\Sigma^{\prime})\to H^{N_{+}+1}_{\not{\nabla}}(\Sigma^{\prime})

can be meromorphically continued to
\omega\in(i\Omega\setminus i\mathscr{Q}_{\rm reg})\cap\{\im\omega>-(\frac{1}{2}+N_{+})\kappa_{+}\}
and
\omega\mapsto\chi_{\infty,+}R(\omega)\chi_{\infty,+}
is meromorphic on
i\Omega\cap\{\im\omega>-(\frac{1}{2}+N_{+})\kappa_{+}\}
. Let
i\mathscr{Q}_{\rm res}
denote the set of poles of
\omega\mapsto\chi_{\infty,+}R(\omega)\chi_{\infty,+}
in
i\Omega
. Then

\mathscr{Q}_{\rm res}\subseteq\mathscr{Q}_{\rm reg}.

Furthermore, the above results hold also with the following domain changes:

\displaystyle\chi_{\infty,+}R(\omega)\chi_{\infty,+}:

\displaystyle\>\pi_{|m|\leq m_{0}}(L^{2}(\Sigma^{\prime}))\to\pi_{|m|\leq m_{0}}(H^{2}(\Sigma^{\prime}))\quad\textnormal{for any $m_{0}\in[0,\infty)$},

\displaystyle\chi_{\infty,+}R(\omega)\chi_{\infty,+}:

\displaystyle\>L^{2}(\Sigma^{\prime})\to H^{2}(\Sigma^{\prime})\quad\textnormal{for suitably small $\frac{|a|}{M}$}.

Proof.

By taking
s=-i\omega
and and using that
r=\varrho
in
\{r\leq\frac{1}{2}R_{\infty}\}
, we obtain

\chi_{\infty,+}R(\omega)\chi_{\infty,+}=(\chi_{\infty,+}Q_{s}^{-1})\cdot r^{-1}L_{s}^{-1}\cdot(r^{3}Q_{s}\chi_{\infty,+}).

We conclude by applying Corollary
19.1
and Proposition
19.2
and using boundedness of
\chi_{\infty,+}Q_{s}^{-1}
and
Q_{s}\chi_{\infty,+}
.
∎

Appendix A
Proof of Lemma
13.2

We may assume that
\re s<0
, since the
\re s\geq 0
follows immediately from (
13.7
).

In the case
\re s<0
, the statements in Lemma
13.2
follow from the proof of
[
GW21
]
[Lemma 9.7]. For the sake of completeness, we carry out the proof below.

Recall (
13.4
), (
13.5
) and (
13.6
):

\displaystyle 0<\mu<

\displaystyle\>\nu,

\displaystyle 4-\beta-(1-\mu)(\alpha+\tilde{\sigma}^{-2}\alpha^{-1})>

\displaystyle\>0,

\displaystyle 1-\mu\nu-4\beta^{-1}\left(\frac{|{\re s}|}{|s|}\right)^{2}-\tilde{\sigma}^{2}>

\displaystyle\>0.

We will first determine for what values of
\frac{|\Re s|}{|s|}
(
13.4
)–(
13.6
) can hold simultaneously. First of all, by (
13.4
), it is sufficient to show that (
13.5
) and (
13.6
) hold with
\nu
replaced
\mu
, which together imply that:

(A.1)

\frac{1}{\frac{4-\beta}{1-\mu}\alpha-\alpha^{2}}<\tilde{\sigma}^{2}<1-\mu^{2}-4\beta^{-1}\left(\frac{|{\re s}|}{|s|}\right)^{2}

We can minimize the term on the very left-hand side by taking
\alpha=\frac{1}{2}\frac{4-\beta}{1-\mu}
, so that (
A.1
) becomes:

\frac{4(1-\mu)^{2}}{(4-\beta)^{2}}<\tilde{\sigma}^{2}<1-\mu^{2}-4\beta^{-1}\left(\frac{|{\re s}|}{|s|}\right)^{2}.

Hence, we can find a
\tilde{\sigma}
satisfying the above inequality if and only if

\left(\frac{|{\re s}|}{|s|}\right)^{2}<\frac{\beta}{4}\left(1-\mu^{2}-\frac{4(1-\mu)^{2}}{(4-\beta)^{2}}\right).

We will first pick
\mu\in[0,1]
so that the above expression is maximized. Note the right-hand side vanishes for
\mu=1
and is non-positive for
\mu=0
. Furthermore, a maximum is attained in
(0,1)
if

\frac{d}{d\mu}\left(1-\mu^{2}-\frac{4(1-\mu)^{2}}{(4-\beta)^{2}}\right)=-2\mu+\frac{8}{(4-\beta)^{2}}(1-\mu)

vanishes, which is the case for

\mu=\frac{4}{(4-\beta)^{2}}\frac{1}{\frac{4}{(4-\beta)^{2}}+1}=\frac{1}{1+\frac{(4-\beta)^{2}}{4}}=\frac{1}{1+b^{2}},

where
b=2-\frac{\beta}{2}
. Hence,

\left(\frac{|{\re s}|}{|s|}\right)^{2}<\left(1-\frac{b}{2}\right)\frac{b^{2}}{1+b^{2}}.

Finally, since the right-hand side vanishes for
b=0
and
b=2
, the maximum will be obtained if

0=\frac{d}{db}\left(\left(1-\frac{b}{2}\right)\frac{b^{2}}{1+b^{2}}\right)=-\frac{b(b-1)(b^{2}+b+4)}{2(1+b^{2})^{2}},

which is the case for
b=1
. We conclude that (
13.4
)–(
13.6
) can only hold if

\left(\frac{|{\re s}|}{|s|}\right)^{2}<\frac{1}{4}.

or
\re s>-\frac{1}{2}|s|
. Note that this corresponds to the sector
\frac{\pi}{2}<\arg z<\theta_{0}
, with
\theta_{0}\in(\frac{\pi}{2},\pi)
satisfying:
\cos\theta_{0}=-\frac{1}{2}
. That means that
\theta_{0}=\frac{2}{3}\pi
.

For
\beta=2
,
\mu=\frac{1}{2}
and
\alpha=2
, (
A.1
) reduces to:

\frac{1}{4}<\tilde{\sigma}^{2}<\frac{3}{4}-2\left(\frac{|{\re s}|}{|s|}\right)^{2}.

If we fix
\sigma^{2}=|2s|^{2}\tilde{\sigma}^{2}|
, we therefore have that
|s|^{2}<\sigma^{2}
and

\sigma^{2}<3|s|^{2}-8|\re s|^{2}=3|\im s|^{2}-5|\re s|^{2}.

This finishes the proof of Lemma
13.2
.

Appendix B
Compact embeddings

The following Hilbert spaces are compactly embedded:

Proposition B.1
.

Let
R_{\infty}\geq 3r_{+}
and
N_{+}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
. Then

(B.1)

\displaystyle H_{\sigma_{2},R_{\infty}}^{N_{+}+1}\subset\joinrel\subset

\displaystyle\>H_{\sigma_{1},R_{\infty}}^{N_{+}}\quad\textnormal{for all $0>|\sigma_{2}|>|\sigma_{1}|$},

(B.2)

\displaystyle H_{\sigma_{2},R_{\infty},2}^{N_{+}+2}\subset\joinrel\subset

\displaystyle\>H_{\sigma_{1},R_{\infty},2}^{N_{+}+1}\quad\textnormal{for all $0>|\sigma_{2}|>|\sigma_{1}|$},

(B.3)

\displaystyle H_{\sigma,R_{\infty},1}^{N_{+}+1}\subset\joinrel\subset

\displaystyle\>H_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}^{N_{+}}\quad\textnormal{for all $\sigma\in\mathbb{R}$.}

Proof.

We first consider (
B.3
). Let
\{f_{k}\}
be a sequence in
H_{\sigma,R_{\infty},1}^{N_{+}+1}
satisfying
\|f_{k}\|_{H_{\sigma,R_{\infty},1}^{N_{+}+1}}=1
. To conclude (
B.3
), we need to show that it admits a Cauchy subsequence with respect to the
H_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}^{N_{+}}
-norm.

We consider first the
B_{R_{\infty}}
-part of the norm:

\|f_{k}\|_{B_{R_{\infty}}}^{2}=\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{(d_{0}x_{0})^{2n}}{(\max\{\ell+1,n+1\})^{2n}}\||\partial_{x}^{n}(f_{k})_{\ell}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{2})}(x_{0})\\
=\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\|(f_{k})_{\ell}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{2})}(x_{0})+\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{(\max\{\ell+1,n+1\})^{2}}\frac{(d_{0}x_{0})^{2n}}{(\max\{\ell+1,n+1\})^{2n}}\||\partial_{x}^{n+1}(f_{k})_{\ell}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{2})}^{2}(x_{0})\\
=\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\sum_{|m|\leq\ell}|(f_{k})_{\ell m}|^{2}(x_{0})\\
+\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\sum_{|m|\leq\ell}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{(\max\{\ell+1,n+1\})^{2}}\frac{(d_{0}x_{0})^{2n}}{(\max\{\ell+1,n+1\})^{2n}}|\partial_{x}^{n+1}(f_{k})_{\ell m}|^{2}(x_{0})

Since
\|f_{k}\|_{B_{R_{\infty}}}^{2}\leq 1
, we have by Bolzano–Weierstrass that there exists a subsequence
f_{k_{l}}
such that for fixed
L>0

\displaystyle\sum_{\ell=0}^{L}\sum_{|m|\leq\ell}|(f_{k_{l}})_{\ell m}|^{2}(x_{0})\to

\displaystyle\>a_{L,N},

\displaystyle\sum_{\ell=0}^{L}\sum_{|m|\leq\ell}\sum_{n=0}^{N}\frac{1}{(\max\{\ell+1,n+1\})^{2}}\frac{(d_{0}x_{0})^{2n}}{(\max\{\ell+1,n+1\})^{2n}}|\partial_{x}^{n+1}(f_{k_{l}})_{\ell m}|^{2}(x_{0})\to

\displaystyle\>b_{L,N}

for fixed
L,N
. We moreover have that the “high-frequency part” of the norm of the difference
f_{k}-f_{r}
can be estimated as follows:

\displaystyle\sum_{\ell\geq L}\|(f_{k}-f_{r})_{\ell}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{2})}(x_{0})\leq\frac{1}{L(L+1)}

\displaystyle\sum_{\ell\geq L}\|(\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(f_{k}-f_{r}))_{\ell}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{2})}(x_{0})\leq\frac{C}{L(L+1)},

\displaystyle\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{N}_{0},n\in\mathbb{N}_{0},\textnormal{$\ell\geq L$ or $n\geq N$}}\frac{1}{(\max\{\ell+1,n+1\})^{2}}

\displaystyle\frac{(d_{0}x_{0})^{2n}}{(\max\{\ell+1,n+1\})^{2n}}\||\partial_{x}^{n+1}(f_{k}-f_{r})_{\ell}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{2})}^{2}(x_{0})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{(\min\{N,L\}+1)^{2}}(\|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(f_{k}-f_{r})\|_{B_{R_{\infty}}}^{2}

\displaystyle+\|\partial_{x}(f_{k}-f_{r})\|_{B_{R_{\infty}}}^{2})\leq\frac{C}{(\min\{N,L\}+1)^{2}}

Hence,
\{f_{k_{l}}\}
is Cauchy with respect to
\|\cdot\|_{B_{R_{\infty}}}
.

Consider now the
G_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}
-part of the norm:

\|f_{k}\|_{G_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}}^{2}=\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\sum_{n=0}^{\max\{\ell-1,0\}}\frac{(4\sigma)^{2n}(\ell-(n+1))!^{2}}{(\ell+n+1)!^{2}}\int_{0}^{x_{0}}\|\partial_{x}^{n}f_{\ell}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{2})}(x)\,dx\\
+(\ell+1)^{3}\sum_{n=\ell}^{\infty}\frac{\sigma^{2n}}{n!^{2}(n+1)!^{2}}\int_{0}^{x_{0}}\|\partial_{x}^{n}f_{\ell}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{2})}(x)\,dx\\
=\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\sum_{|m|\leq\ell}\sum_{n=0}^{\max\{\ell-1,0\}}\frac{(4\sigma)^{2n}(\ell-(n+1))!^{2}}{(\ell+n+1)!^{2}}\int_{0}^{x_{0}}|\partial_{x}^{n}f_{m\ell}|^{2}(x)\,dx\\
+(\ell+1)^{3}\sum_{n=\ell}^{\infty}\frac{\sigma^{2n}}{n!^{2}(n+1)!^{2}}\int_{0}^{x_{0}}|\partial_{x}^{n}f_{m\ell}|^{2}(x)\,dx.

Now we can proceed as above: we first split the sum into a part where
\ell\leq L
and
n\leq N
and, rather than applying Bolzano–Weierstrass, we apply Arzelà–Ascoli for functions on
[0,x_{0}]
to obtain a convergent subsequence. Here we moreover use that we can consider the
C^{N}([0,x_{0}])
norm rather than the
H^{N}([0,x_{0}])
norm via a Sobolev inequality on
[0,x_{0}]
, since the norm
\|f_{k}\|_{G_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}}
bounds arbitrarily many
x
-derivatives.

Subsequently, we bound the remaining high-frequency part part of
\|f_{k}-f_{r}\|_{G_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}}^{2}
, where
n>N
or
\ell\geq L
, with a constant
\frac{1}{(\min\{N,L\}+1)^{2}}
by using the boundedness of
\|f_{k}-f_{r}\|_{G_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}}+\|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(f_{k}-f_{r})\|_{G_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}}+\|\partial_{x}(f_{k}-f_{r})\|_{G_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}}
.

To conclude the proof of (
B.3
), we consider
\|f\|_{H^{N_{+}}(\Sigma\cap\{\varrho\leq 2R_{\infty}\})}^{2}+\|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}f\|_{H^{N_{+}}(\Sigma\cap\{\varrho\leq\frac{1}{3}R_{\infty}\})}^{2}+\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}|(\ell(\ell+1)+1)^{-\frac{w_{p}}{4}}f_{\ell}\|^{2}_{H^{N_{+}}(\Sigma\cap\{\frac{1}{3}R_{\infty}\leq\varrho\leq 3R_{\infty}\})}
. We proceed as before, but rather than applying Arzelà–Ascoli to estimate the bounded angular frequencies
\ell\leq L
, we apply Rellich–Kondrachov on
[r_{+}^{-1},\frac{1}{R_{\infty}}]
.

We can prove (
B.1
) and (
B.2
) in an analogous manner, where we use obtain an smallness factor in the high-frequency estimates by using that
(4\sigma_{1})^{2n}=(\frac{\sigma_{2}}{\sigma_{1}})^{-2n}(4\sigma_{2})^{2n}
.
∎

Appendix C
Norms of pointwise products

In this section, we consider norms of products of functions:
\|g\cdot f\|_{H^{N_{+}}_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}}
, where
g
will take the role of a coefficient in a differential operator and
f
will take the role of a function in the domain of that operator. Since the
H^{N_{+}}_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}
-norm features an infinite number of derivatives, one cannot immediately estimate
\|g\cdot f\|_{H^{N_{+}}_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}}
by a product of
\|f\|_{H^{N_{+}}_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}}
with an appropriate norm for
g
.

In this section, we will prove:

Proposition C.1
.

Let
\beta>2
. Then there exists a constant
C>0
such that for all appropriately regular functions
f,g;[0,x_{0}]\times\mathbb{S}^{2}\to\mathbb{C}
, we can estimate:

\|g\cdot f\|_{H^{N_{+}}_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}}\leq C\|g\|_{H^{N_{+}}_{\sigma,R_{\infty};\textnormal{coeff}}}\cdot\|f\|_{H^{N_{+}}_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}},

with

\|g\|^{2}_{H^{N_{+}}_{\sigma,R_{\infty};\textnormal{coeff}}}:=\|g\|_{H^{N_{+}+2}(\Sigma\cap\{\varrho\leq 3R_{\infty}\})}^{2}+\|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}g\|_{H^{N_{+}+2}(\Sigma\cap\{\varrho\leq\frac{1}{3}R_{\infty}\})}^{2}\\
+\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\|(1+\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}})^{\frac{w_{p}+5}{4}}g_{\ell}\|_{H^{N_{+}+2}(\Sigma\cap\{3R_{\infty}\leq\varrho\leq 4R_{\infty}\})}^{2}+\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}e^{2\ell}(\ell+1)^{5}\frac{(d_{0}x_{0})^{2j}(j+1)^{2}}{j!^{2}}\|\partial_{x}^{j}g_{\ell}|_{x=x_{0}}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{2})}\\
+\sum_{j\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\frac{e^{5(\ell+j)}}{j!^{4}}\|\partial_{x}^{j}g_{\ell}\|^{2}_{L^{\infty}([0,x_{0}]\times\mathbb{S}^{2})}.

The proof follows from combining Lemmas
C.2
and
C.3
below, which each deal with a different part of the norm
\|g\cdot f\|_{H^{N_{+}}_{\sigma,R_{\infty}}}
.

Lemma C.2
.

Let
\beta>2
. Then there exists a constant
C>0
such that for all appropriately regular functions
f,g;[0,x_{0}]\times\mathbb{S}^{2}\to\mathbb{C}
, we can estimate:

(C.1)

\|gf\|_{B_{R_{\infty}}}^{2}\leq C\|f\|_{B_{R_{\infty}}}^{2}\left[\sum_{j=0}^{\infty}\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}e^{2\ell}(\ell+1)^{5}\frac{(d_{0}x_{0})^{2j}(j+1)^{2}}{j!^{2}}\|\partial_{x}^{j}g_{\ell}|_{x=x_{0}}\|^{2}_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{2})}\right].

Furthermore,

(C.2)

\|gf\|_{H^{N_{+}}(\Sigma\cap\{r\leq 3R_{\infty}\})}^{2}+\|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}(gf)\|_{H^{N_{+}}(\Sigma\cap\{r\leq\frac{1}{3}R_{\infty}\})}^{2}+\|(-\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}-\mathbf{1})^{-\frac{w_{p}}{4}}(gf)_{\ell}\|_{H^{N_{+}}(\Sigma\cap\{3R_{\infty}\leq r\leq 4R_{\infty}\})}^{2}\\
\leq\|f\|_{H^{N_{+}}(\Sigma\cap\{r\leq 3R_{\infty}\})}^{2}\|g\|_{H^{N_{+}+2}(\Sigma\cap\{r\leq 3R_{\infty}\})}^{2}+\|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}f\|_{H^{N_{+}}(\Sigma\cap\{r\leq\frac{1}{3}R_{\infty}\})}^{2}\|\not{\nabla}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}g\|_{H^{N_{+}+2}(\Sigma\cap\{r\leq\frac{1}{3}R_{\infty}\})}^{2}\\
+\|(\mathbf{1}-\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}})^{-\frac{w_{p}}{4}}f\|_{H^{N_{+}}(\Sigma\cap\{3R_{\infty}\leq r\leq 4R_{\infty}\})}^{2}\|(\mathbf{1}-\not{\Delta}_{\mathbb{S}^{2}})^{\frac{w_{p}+5}{4}}g\|_{H^{N_{+}}(\Sigma\cap\{3R_{\infty}\leq r\leq 4R_{\infty}\})}^{2}.

Proof.

Consider the functions
g,f:[r_{+},\infty)_{\varrho}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}_{\vartheta,\varphi*}\to\mathbb{C}
. We denote with
g^{(n)}=\partial_{x}^{n}g
and
f^{(n)}=\partial_{x}^{n}f
. We can expand the product
gf
in spherical harmonics:

(gf)_{\ell m}(x)=\sum_{\tilde{\ell}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\sum_{\tilde{m}=-\tilde{\ell}}^{\tilde{\ell}}\sum_{\ell^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\sum_{m^{\prime}=-\ell^{\prime}}^{m=\ell^{\prime}}g_{\ell^{\prime}m^{\prime}}(x)f_{\tilde{\ell}\tilde{m}}(x)\langle Y_{\ell^{\prime}m^{\prime}}Y_{\tilde{\ell}\tilde{m}},Y_{\ell m}\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{2})}.

Since
\langle Y_{\ell^{\prime}m^{\prime}}Y_{\tilde{\ell}\tilde{m}},Y_{\ell m}\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{2})}=0
if
m\neq m^{\prime}+\tilde{m}
, we can write

\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}|(gf)_{\ell}|^{2}\,d\upsigma=\sum_{\tilde{\ell}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\sum_{\tilde{m}=-\tilde{\ell}}^{\tilde{\ell}}\sum_{\ell^{\prime}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\sum_{m^{\prime}=-\ell^{\prime}}^{m=\ell^{\prime}}|g_{\ell^{\prime}m^{\prime}}(x)|^{2}|f_{\tilde{\ell}\tilde{m}}(x)|^{2}|\langle Y_{\ell^{\prime}m^{\prime}}Y_{\tilde{\ell}\tilde{m}},Y_{\ell(m^{\prime}+\tilde{m})}\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{2})}|^{2}.

By applying Cauchy–Schwarz and using that spherical harmonics have unit norm on
\mathbb{S}^{2}
, we can further estimate:

|\langle Y_{\ell^{\prime}m^{\prime}}Y_{\tilde{\ell}\tilde{m}},Y_{\ell(m^{\prime}+\tilde{m})}\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{2})}|^{2}\leq\|Y_{\ell^{\prime}m^{\prime}}Y_{\tilde{\ell}\tilde{m}}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{2})}^{2}\|Y_{\ell(m^{\prime}+\tilde{m})}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{2})}^{2}\\
\leq\|Y_{\ell^{\prime}m^{\prime}}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{S}^{2})}^{2}\|Y_{\tilde{\ell}\tilde{m}}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{2})}^{2}\|Y_{\ell(m^{\prime}+\tilde{m})}\|_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{2})}^{2}\leq\|Y_{\ell^{\prime}m^{\prime}}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{S}^{2})}^{2}.

By a standard Sobolev inequality on
\mathbb{S}^{2}
, we can further estimate:

\|Y_{\ell^{\prime}m^{\prime}}\|_{L^{\infty}(\mathbb{S}^{2})}^{2}\leq C\|Y_{\ell^{\prime}m^{\prime}}\|_{H^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{2})}^{2}\leq C(\ell^{\prime}+1)^{4}.

Furthermore, from the vanishing properties of Clebsch–Gordan coefficients (see for example
[
Sak94
]
[§3.7])
\langle Y_{\ell^{\prime}m^{\prime}}Y_{\tilde{\ell}\tilde{m}},Y_{\ell(m^{\prime}+\tilde{m})}\rangle_{L^{2}(\mathbb{S}^{2})}=0
if
\ell^{\prime}+\tilde{\ell}<\ell
or
|\tilde{\ell}-\ell^{\prime}|>\ell
.
Finally, observe that by Cauchy–Schwarz and
\sum_{j=1}j^{-2}=\frac{\pi^{2}}{6}
:

|(gf)_{\ell}^{(n)}|^{2}=\left|\sum_{k=0}^{n}\binom{n}{k}(g^{(n-k)}f^{(k)})_{\ell}\right|^{2}\leq\sum_{k=0}^{n}(n-k+1)^{-2}\sum_{k=0}^{n}\binom{n}{k}^{2}(n-k+1)^{2}|(g^{(n-k)}f^{(k)})_{\ell}|^{2}\\
\leq\frac{\pi^{2}}{6}\sum_{k=0}^{n}\binom{n}{k}^{2}(n-k+1)^{2}|(g^{(n-k)}f^{(k)})_{\ell}|^{2}.

Combining the above observations, we obtain:

\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{(d_{0}x_{0})^{2n}}{(\max\{\ell+1,n+1\})^{2n}}\int_{\mathbb{S}^{2}}|(gf)_{\ell}^{(n)}|^{2}\,d\upsigma\\
\leq C\sum_{\ell\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\sum_{\{\ell^{\prime},\tilde{\ell}\in\mathbb{N}_{0}\,:\,|\tilde{\ell}-\ell^{\prime}|\leq\ell\leq\ell+\ell^{\prime}\}}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{(d_{0}x_{0})^{2n}}{(\max\{\ell+1,n+1\})^{2n}}\\
